Courbes et fibrés vectoriels en théorie de Hodge z z -adique globale
Siyan Daniel Li-Huerta

Abstract
. We study the global analogue of the Fargues–Fontaine curve over function fields F F . We prove some foundational results about its moduli of G G -bundles Bun G , F \Bun_{G,F} , which is a geometrization of the global Kottwitz set B ⁡ ( F , G ) B(F,G) . For example, Bun G , F \Bun_{G,F} plays the role of Igusa stacks over function fields. We use Bun G , F \Bun_{G,F} to reformulate the global Langlands conjecture for G G over F F in terms of categorical local Langlands, refining conjectures of Arinkin–Gaitsgory–Kazhdan–Raskin–Rozenblyum–Varshavsky and Zhu. Finally, we verify this conjecture when G G is commutative. Along the way, we prove a GAGA theorem for smooth proper schemes over sousperfectoid spaces, which is of independent interest.



Introduction

The title of this paper pays homage to the foundational work of Fargues–Fontaine
[
24
]
. Starting from a nonarchimedean local field
F_{v}
with residue field
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
(and an auxiliary algebraically closed nonarchimedean field
K
over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
), they constructed an adic space
X_{F_{v},K}
with remarkable properties. Since its introduction, the
Fargues–Fontaine curve

X_{F_{v},K}
has played a fundamental role in arithmetic geometry over
F_{v}
. For example, work of Fargues–Scholze
[
25
]
shows that its moduli of
G
-bundles
\Bun_{G,F_{v}}
is central to the Langlands correspondence for
G
over
F_{v}
.

When
F_{v}
is the function field
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(\!(z)\!)
, the Fargues–Fontaine curve already appears in work of Hartl–Pink
[
37
]
, so we also call it the
Hartl–Pink curve
. It can be described as follows. The product
\Spa F_{v}\times\Spa K
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
exists as an adic space (in fact, it is isomorphic to the punctured open unit disk over
K
), and the absolute
q
-Frobenius automorphism
\Frob_{K}
of
K
acts freely and totally discontinuously on
\Spa F_{v}\times\Spa K
. Hence we can form the quotient adic space

(
\dagger
)

\displaystyle X_{F_{v},K}\coloneqq(\Spa F_{v}\times\Spa K)/\Frob_{K}.

The goal of this paper is to study a
global
analogue of the above constructions.

G
-bundles on the global Hartl–Pink curve

Let
F
be a global function field with field of constants
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. By viewing schemes as adic spaces, one could try to define a
global Hartl–Pink curve
by naively copying (

†

) and attempting to form

(
\ddagger
)

\displaystyle\text{``}(\Spec{F}\times\Spa K)/\Frob_{K}\text{''.}

However, two obstacles arise:

1)

It is unclear how to interpret “
\Spec{F}\times\Spa K
” in a well-behaved way.

2)

In any reasonable interpretation of “
\Spec{F}\times\Spa K
”,
\Frob_{K}
will no longer act freely and totally discontinuously.

We will primarily be interested in
G
-bundles on (

‡

), so we can circumvent 2) via descent. To circumvent 1), write
C
for the geometrically connected smooth proper curve over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
associated with
F
. Then
\Spec{F}=\varprojlim_{U}U
, where
U
runs over dense open subschemes of
C
, so formally we expect

\displaystyle\text{``}\Spec{F}\times\Spa K\text{''}=\text{``}\varprojlim_{U}U\times\Spa K\text{''.}

Now
U\times\Spa{K}

does
have a well-behaved interpretation: this product exists as an adic space and in fact is isomorphic to the analytification
U_{K}^{\an}
of
U
over
K
. Moreover, this interpretation works well in families, which leads to the following:

Definition
.

Let
G
be a connected split
1
1

1

In the introduction, we work with split
G
for simplicity. However, everything works for general connected reductive groups
G
over
F
, and we work in this generality in the body of the paper.
reductive group over
F
. For all affinoid perfectoid spaces
S=\Spa(R,R^{+})
over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
,

a)

Write
\Bun_{G,U}(S)
for the groupoid of
G
-bundles
{\mathscr{G}}
on
U_{S}^{\an}
equipped with an isomorphism
\phi:{\mathscr{G}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Frob_{S}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}
.

b)

Write
\Bun_{G,F}(S)
for the groupoid
\varinjlim_{U}\Bun_{G,U}(S)
.

Remarks
.

1)

One can recover
\Bun_{G,U}
from
\Bun_{G,F}
; see Lemma
2.6
. Therefore we will interchangeably use
\Bun_{G,U}
and
\Bun_{G,F}
.

2)

Amusingly, while we do not define
the
global Hartl–Pink curve, nonetheless we can define its moduli of
G
-bundles
\Bun_{G,F}
. This is reminiscent of the situation for
local
Hartl–Pink curves
X_{F_{v},K}
, which depend on the auxiliary
K
.

Geometry of
\Bun_{G,F}

We start with the following basic properties of
\Bun_{G,U}
.

Theorem A
.

\Bun_{G,U}
is a small Artin v-stack over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
. It is
\ell
-cohomologically smooth over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
, and its dualizing complex with
{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}
-coefficients is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{F}}_{\ell}
.

Theorem A mirrors results of Fargues–Scholze
[
25
, Theorem IV.1.19]
and Hamann–Imai
[
34
, Proposition 3.18]
in the local setting.

To state our next theorem, let us introduce the
localization map
, which plays an important role throughout this paper. Write
Z
for the closed complement
C\smallsetminus U
. For all
z
in
Z
, there is a natural map
\Spa F_{z}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}U
, so by restricting
G
-bundles along these maps and using the presentation (

†

), we get a map of v-stacks

\displaystyle\loc_{Z}:\Bun_{G,U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\prod_{z\in Z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}.

Recall that the local moduli
\Bun_{G,F_{z}}
has an open substack
\Bun_{G,F_{z}}^{\semis}

[
25
, Theorem III.4.5]
, which is the locus where the corresponding
G
-bundle on the curve
X_{F_{z},K}
is semistable. By taking its preimage under
\loc_{Z}
, we define a
global semistable locus

\Bun_{G,U}^{\semis}
. While
\Bun_{G,U}^{\semis}
is defined in terms of its local analogue
\Bun^{\semis}_{G,F_{z}}
, we prove that it admits the following intrinsic description.

Theorem B
.

\Bun_{G,F}^{\semis}
is an open substack of
\Bun_{G,F}
, and we have an isomorphism

\displaystyle\Bun^{\semis}_{G,F}\cong\coprod_{b\in B(F,G)_{\basic}}*/\underline{G_{b}(F)},

where
B(F,G)
denotes the
global Kottwitz set

[
49
]
,
B(F,G)_{\basic}
denotes its subset of
basic
elements, and
G_{b}
is the inner twist of
G
associated with
b
.

Theorem B mirrors a result of Fargues–Scholze
[
25
, Theorem III.4.5]
in the local setting. This is one incarnation of the idea that
\Bun_{G,F_{z}}
is a geometrization of the local Kottwitz set
B(F_{z},G)
, but there are other such incarnations. For example, Anschütz
[
3
, Theorem 10]
proved that the underlying topological space
\absolutevalue{\Bun_{G,F_z}}
is naturally in bijection with
B(F_{z},G)
.

We similarly regard
\Bun_{G,F}
as a geometrization of the global Kottwitz set. However, for reasons explained in the next subsection, we do
not
expect a natural bijection between
\absolutevalue{\Bun_{G,F}}
and
B(F,G)
in general. Nonetheless, we prove the following description of the
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
-points of
\Bun_{G,F}
.

Theorem C
.

\Bun_{G,F}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q})
is naturally equivalent to the groupoid quotient

\displaystyle G(F\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q})/G(F\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}),

where the action is given by
\Frob_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}
-conjugation. Consequently, the set of isomorphism classes in
\Bun_{G,F}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q})
is naturally in bijection with
B(F,G)
.

Theorem C mirrors a result of Anschütz
[
4
, Theorem 5.3]
in the local setting. Actually, we prove a generalization of Theorem C that fully describes the
reduction
of
\Bun_{G,F}
; see Theorem
2.10
. The more general Theorem
2.10
mirrors a result of Gleason–Ivanov–Zillinger
[
32
, Theorem 7.14.(1)]
in the local setting.

Relation with shtukas

It turns out that
\Bun_{G,U}
plays the role of
Igusa stacks

[
72
]
over function fields. Igusa stacks capture the geometry of Shimura varieties, so let us recall the function field analogue of the latter. Let
I
be a finite set, and for all
i
in
I
, let
V_{i}
be a representation of the Langlands dual group
\widehat{G}
. Write
V
for the representation
\boxtimes_{i\in I}V_{i}
of
\widehat{G}^{I}
. Associated with
I
and
V
, there is a moduli space
\Sht^{I}_{G,V}
of
global shtukas
bounded by
V

[
69
]
, which is a Deligne–Mumford stack that is separated and locally of finite type over
C^{I}
.

There is also a local version of
\Sht^{I}_{G,V}
. Let
\{I_{z}\}_{z\in Z}
be a partition of
I
, and for all
z
in
Z
, write
V_{z}
for the representation
\boxtimes_{i\in I_{z}}V_{i}
of
\widehat{G}^{I_{z}}
. Associated with
I_{z}
and
V_{z}
, there is a moduli space
\LocSht^{I_{z}}_{G,V_{z}}
of
local shtukas
bounded by
V_{z}

[
67
]
, which is a small v-stack over
(\Spd{\mathcal{O}}_{z})^{I_{z}}
.

We prove the following function field analogue of Scholze’s
fiber product conjecture

[
72
, Conjecture 1.1]
, which is the defining feature of Igusa stacks:

Theorem D
.

There is a natural cartesian square of small v-stacks

\textstyle{(\Sht^{I}_{G,V})^{\Diamond}|_{\prod_{z\in Z}(\Spd{\mathcal{O}}_{z})^{I_{z}}_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\HT}}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\prod_{z\in Z}\!\big(\!\LocSht^{I_{z}}_{G,V_{z}}\!\big)_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\BL}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G,U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\loc_{Z}}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\prod_{z\in Z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}},}

where
\BL
denotes the product of the
Beauville–Laszlo maps

(\LocSht^{I_{z}}_{G,V_{z}})_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}

[
25
, p. 98]
, and
\pi_{\HT}
is a function field analogue of the Hodge–Tate period map.

Actually, we prove a generalization of Theorem D that allows level structures; see Theorem
3.10
. Previously, Hartl–Viehmann
[
38
]
studied a version of the period map
\pi_{\HT}
for the function field analogue of Rapoport–Zink spaces.

Remarks
.

1)

Theorem D implies that
\Bun_{G,U}
encodes the function field analogue of
Igusa varieties
; see Proposition
4.10
. Because Igusa varieties associated with non-basic elements of the Kottwitz set can be positive-dimensional in general, this explains why we do not expect a natural bijection between
\absolutevalue{\Bun_{G,F}}
and
B(F,G)
.

2)

Gleason–Ivanov–Zillinger
[
32
, Theorem 7.14.(2)]
proved a natural description of the
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
-points of
\LocSht^{I_{z}}_{G,V_{z}}
. By combining this description with Theorem C and the aforementioned result of Anschütz
[
4
, Theorem 5.3]
, Theorem D immediately implies the
Langlands–Rapoport conjecture
for moduli spaces of global shtukas with arbitrary (in particular, colliding) legs. This generalizes results of Arasteh Rad–Hartl
[
6
, Theorem 3.21]
, who proved this when the legs are disjoint.

Relation with Langlands

In the local setting,
\Bun_{G,F_{z}}
plays a central role in the Langlands correspondence for
G
over
F_{z}
. For example, let
\Lambda
be one of
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
,
\overline{{\mathbb{Z}}}_{\ell}
, or
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{\ell}
, and write
D(\Bun_{G,F_{z}},\Lambda)
for the associated derived category of étale sheaves.
2
2

2

Strictly speaking, when
\Lambda\neq\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{\ell}
we use (a base change of) motivic sheaves
[
65
,
66
]
; see
5.1
.
Associated with
I_{z}
and
V_{z}
, Fargues–Scholze
[
25
]
constructed a
Hecke operator

\displaystyle T_{V_{z}}:D(\Bun_{G,F_{z}},\Lambda)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D(\Bun_{G,F_{z}},\Lambda),

which they used to define the automorphic-to-Galois direction of the Langlands correspondence for
G
over
F_{z}
, up to semisimplification.

Using Theorem D, we prove that applying Hecke operators to the object

\displaystyle\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda\in D\,\big(\!\prod_{z\in Z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}},\Lambda\big)=\bigotimes_{z\in Z}D(\Bun_{G,F_{z}},\Lambda)

recovers the cohomology of the moduli of global shtukas at infinite level. Write

\displaystyle\pi_{\infty Z}:(\Sht^{I}_{G,V,\infty Z})_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}U^{I}_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}

for the moduli of global shtukas with infinite level at
Z
, which has an action of
G(F_{Z})\coloneqq\prod_{z\in Z}G(F_{z})
, and write
{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
for its intersection cohomology sheaf. Forthcoming work of Eteve–Gaitsgory–Genestier–Lafforgue will endow
\pi_{\infty Z,!}{\mathcal{S}}_{V}
with the structure of a representation of the Weil group
W_{U}^{I}
on the level of derived categories.

Theorem E
.

After restricting to
\prod_{z\in Z}W_{F_{z}}^{I_{z}}
, we have a natural isomorphism

\displaystyle\pi_{\infty Z,!}{\mathcal{S}}_{V}\cong i_{1}^{*}(T_{V}(\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda)),

where
i_{1}
denotes the product of the natural open embeddings
*/\underline{G(F_{z})}\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}
, and
T_{V}
is the endofunctor
\bigotimes_{z\in Z}T_{V_{z}}
of
\bigotimes_{z\in Z}D(\Bun_{G,F_{z}},\Lambda)
.

Remarks
.

1)

If one is only interested in Theorem E after taking cohomology groups, it suffices to use work of Xue
[
71
]
instead of the forthcoming work of Eteve–Gaitsgory–Genestier–Lafforgue. For example, this is the case for the next remark.

2)

One can use Theorem E to recover all of the representation-theoretic results in
[
54
]
; see Remark
5.13
.2).

In light of Theorem E, it is natural to ask how to interpret
\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda
on the Galois side of the Langlands correspondence. Write
\LS_{\widehat{G},F_{z}}
for the moduli of continuous
\widehat{G}
-valued representations of
W_{F_{z}}
over
\Lambda

[
16
,
25
,
73
]
, which is an algebraic stack of finite type over
\Lambda
. When the order of
\pi_{0}(Z(G)_{\overline{F}})
is invertible in
\Lambda
, Fargues–Scholze
[
25
, Conjecture X.3.5]
conjecture that there is a canonical equivalence of categories

\displaystyle{\mathbb{L}}_{\psi_{z}}:D(\Bun_{G,F_{z}},\Lambda)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Ind D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\widehat{G},F_{z}})_{\Nilp},

where
\Ind D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\widehat{G},F_{z}})_{\Nilp}
is the derived category of
ind-coherent sheaves
on
\LS_{\widehat{G},F_{z}}
with
nilpotent singular support
, and
{\mathbb{L}}_{\psi_{z}}
depends on the choice of a maximal unipotent subgroup
N
of
G
along with a generic continuous character
\psi_{z}:N(F_{z})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Lambda^{\times}
.

The following conjecture was suggested by Scholze. Let
\psi:N({\mathbb{A}})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Lambda^{\times}
be a generic continuous character trivial on
N(F)
, and for all
z
in
Z
, take
\psi_{z}=\psi|_{N(F_{z})}
.

Conjecture F
.

Assume Fargues–Scholze’s conjecture, so that
\bigotimes_{z\in Z}{\mathbb{L}}_{\psi_{z}}
yields

\displaystyle\bigotimes_{z\in Z}D(\Bun_{G,F_{z}},\Lambda)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\bigotimes_{z\in Z}\Ind D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\widehat{G},F_{z}})_{\Nilp}=\Ind D^{\operatorname{qc}}_{\coh}\,\big(\!\prod_{z\in Z}\LS_{\widehat{G},F_{z}}\big)_{\Nilp}.

Under this equivalence,
\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda
corresponds to
\lres_{Z,*}(\omega_{\LS_{\widehat{G},U}})
, where
\LS_{\widehat{G},U}
denotes the moduli of continuous
\widehat{G}
-valued representations of
W_{U}
over
\Lambda

[
73
]
, and

\displaystyle\lres_{Z}:\LS_{\widehat{G},U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\prod_{z\in Z}\LS_{\widehat{G},F_{z}}

denotes the restriction map.

Remarks
.

1)

For
U=C
and
\Lambda=\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
, Conjecture F specializes to asking for a natural isomorphism
C_{c}(G(F)\backslash G({\mathbb{A}})/G({\mathbb{O}}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\cong\Gamma(\LS_{\widehat{G},C},\omega_{\LS_{\widehat{G},C}})
; see Example
6.11
. Previously, Arinkin–Gaitsgory–Kazhdan–Raskin–Rozenblyum–Varshavsky
[
8
]
conjectured this, and recently Gaitsgory–Raskin
[
29
]
proved a weak version thereof.

More generally, we use Theorem E to prove that Conjecture F implies a conjecture of Zhu
[
73
, Conjecture 4.49]
after restricting to
\prod_{z\in Z}W_{F_{z}}^{I_{z}}
; see Proposition
6.12
.

2)

Given the previous remark, we regard Conjecture F as
the
Langlands conjecture for
G
over
F
when ramification is allowed at places in
Z
. This fulfills a suggestion of Fargues–Scholze
[
26
, §7]
that the global Langlands conjecture ought to study an object that encodes automorphic forms on
G_{b}
for all
b
in
B(F,G)
.

In the number field setting, Fargues
[
23
, section 7]
has already emphasized the importance of studying the analogous object
\overline{\pi}_{\HT,!}\Lambda
, though there are more restrictions on the
b
in
B({\mathbb{Q}},G)
that appear. A more detailed study of
\overline{\pi}_{\HT,!}\Lambda
will appear in forthcoming work of Caraiani–Hamann–Zhang.

3)

Conjecture F fits well with the interpretation of Langlands as an isomorphism of
4
-dimensional topological quantum field theories
. In particular, it fits well with the recent interpretation of relative Langlands as an identification of
boundary theories

[
9
]
; we will investigate this in future work.

4)

For a version of
\Bun_{G,F}
adapted to the
global Kaletha set

[
20
]
instead of
B(F,G)
, see Appendix C, written by Peter Dillery. This builds on work of Fargues
[
22
]
that adapts
\Bun_{G,F_{z}}
to the
local Kaletha set

[
19
]
.

We conclude by proving Conjecture F in the commutative case:

Theorem G
.

When
G
is a (not necessarily split) torus, Conjecture F holds.

Proof sketches

We start with part of Theorem A. We prove that
\Bun_{G,U}
and
\Bun_{G,F}
are small v-stacks by locally choosing a Frobenius-splitting on
U
and analyzing affinoid charts of
U^{\an}_{S}
, which reduces the problem to a v-descent result of Scholze–Weinstein
[
67
, Lemma 17.1.8]
for vector bundles on perfectoid spaces.

Next, we turn to Theorem D. The
S
-points of
(\Sht^{I}_{G,V})^{\Diamond}
involve
G
-bundles on the algebraic curve
C_{R}
, while one can glue in the analytic topology to show that
S
-points of the fiber product in Theorem D involve
G
-bundles on the analytification
C^{\an}_{S}
. To reconcile this, we prove the following GAGA result in Appendix
A
:

Theorem H
.

Let
S=\Spa(R,R^{+})
be a sousperfectoid space, and let
X
be a smooth proper scheme over
R
. Then the analytification functor

\displaystyle\{\mbox{vector bundles on X}\}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\{\mbox{vector bundles on }X^{\an}_{S}\}

is an equivalence of categories.

Remark
.

While we were finalizing this paper, Theorem H was proved independently by Wang
[
70
, Theorem 4.3.1]
along very similar lines; evidently, it is of independent interest. For example, as an application we prove that algebraic stacks of Higgs bundles agree with their analytification, answering a question of Heuer–Xu
[
40
, Remark 8.1.2]
; see Theorem
A.20
. This was also proved independently by Wang.

Theorem H provides a lot of control. In addition to proving Theorem D, we use it to prove Theorem C as follows. An
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
-point of
\Bun_{G,U}
involves a
G
-bundle
{\mathscr{G}}
on
U^{\an}_{\Spa\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}
equipped with a descent datum with respect to the diagram

\displaystyle U_{\Spa{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\times_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}\Spa{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\times_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}\Spa{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}}^{\an}

\displaystyle\rightrightarrows\hskip-10.00002pt\to U_{\Spa{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\times_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}\Spa{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}}^{\an}\rightrightarrows U_{\Spa{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}}^{\an}.

A result of Gleason–Ivanov–Zillinger
[
32
, Theorem 3.15]
implies that the restriction of
{\mathscr{G}}
along
\Spa F_{z}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}U
arises uniquely from a
G
-bundle on
\breve{F}_{z}
. The latter are all trivial, so we can glue in the analytic topology to assume that
U=C
. Finally, we can apply Theorem H and a result of D. Kim
[
48
, Theorem 4.9]
to conclude that
{\mathscr{G}}
arises uniquely from a
G
-bundle on
C_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}
.

We now turn to Theorem B. By proving a description of pro-étale
\underline{{\mathscr{O}}_{U}}
-local systems analogous to Kedlaya–Liu’s description
[
47
, Theorem 8.5.12]
of pro-étale
\underline{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
-local systems, we show that
\Bun_{G,F}
has a natural open substack isomorphic to
*/\underline{G(F)}
. From here, twisting and Theorem C let us show that
\Bun_{G,F}
has an open substack isomorphic to the disjoint union in Theorem B. To prove that this is all of
\Bun^{\semis}_{G,F}
, we use Beauville–Laszlo uniformization
[
25
, Proposition III.3.1]
to lift geometric points from
\Bun^{\semis}_{G,F_{z}}
to
\LocSht^{I_{z}}_{G,V_{z}}
for some
V_{z}
. Combining this with Theorem D yields geometric points of
\Sht^{I}_{G,V}
, and we conclude using a standard nonemptiness criterion for
\Sht^{I}_{G,V}
. Similarly, we finish proving Theorem A by using the charts provided by Beauville–Laszlo uniformization and Theorem D.

In an ideal world, Theorem E would follow immediately from Theorem D. For
\Lambda=\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{\ell}
, one can successfully execute this using the étale sheaf theory of
[
63
]
, but for general
\Lambda
one has to compare the motivic sheaf theory of
[
65
]
with classical étale
\ell
-adic sheaf theories for algebraic varieties. We develop some tools for facilitating these comparisons in Appendix
B
.

Finally, we prove Theorem G by using work of Langlands
[
52
]
on his conjectures for tori
T
to prove an explicit description of
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},U}
.

Outline

In §
1
, we study vector bundles on the global Hartl–Pink curve. In §
2
, we use the Tannakian description of
G
-bundles to convert results from §
1
into part of Theorem A, part of Theorem B, and Theorem C. In §
3
, we introduce shtukas and prove Theorem D. In §
4
, we use Theorem D to finish proving Theorem A and Theorem B, as well as explain the relation with Igusa varieties. In §
5
, we state a forthcoming result of Eteve–Gaitsgory–Genestier–Lafforgue and prove Theorem E. Finally, in §
6
we discuss Conjecture F and prove Theorem G.

In Appendix
A
, we prove Theorem H, which is used throughout the paper. In Appendix
B
, we prove basic results about the (overconvergent) motivic sheaves of
[
65
,
66
]
, which we use in §
5
and §
6
. Finally, in Appendix
C
, written by Peter Dillery, we consider a version of
\Bun_{G,F}
adapted to the global Kaletha set.

Notation

Except for in Appendix
A
, all rings and stacks are classical (i.e. not derived) unless otherwise specified.

By nonarchimedean field, we mean a topological field complete with respect to a rank-
1
nonarchimedean valuation. For any ring homomorphism
A\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}B
, write
A^{\sim}
for the integral closure of the image of
A
in
B
. For any scheme
X
and affine group
G
over
X
, write
\Rep_{X}{G}
for the category of representations of
G
in vector bundles on
X
.

We view schemes as adic spaces via the fully faithful functor sending
\Spec{A}
to
\Spa(A,{\mathbb{Z}}^{\sim})
for all rings
A
, where
A
is endowed with the discrete topology
[
62
, p. 64]
. For any adic space
X
over
\Spa{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, write
X^{\Diamond}
for the associated small v-sheaf over
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
as in
[
67
, Lemma 18.1.1]
.

Unless otherwise specified, all products are over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. For any prestack
X
on

\displaystyle\{\mbox{affine schemes over }{\mathbb{F}}_{q}\},

write
\Frob_{X}:X\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}X
for its absolute
q
-Frobenius endomorphism, and write
X^{\perf}
for its limit perfection. The transition morphisms for our ind-schemes are required to be closed embeddings. Write
\Perf
for the category of affinoid perfectoid spaces over
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
, and for any small v-stack
X
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, write
\Frob_{X}:X\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}X
for its absolute
q
-Frobenius endomorphism.

We view derived categories as stable
\infty
-categories, and we view all functors between derived categories as derived functors. Write
\Sym
for the
\infty
-category of presentably closed symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-categories.

For any ring
A
, write
D(A)
for the derived category of
A
-modules, and write
D_{\perf}(A)\subseteq D(A)
for the full subcategory of perfect objects. For any derived algebraic stack
X
, write
D_{\qcoh}(X)
for its derived category of quasicoherent sheaves, and write
D_{\perf}(X)\subseteq D(X)
for the full subcategory of perfect objects. Write
X^{\cl}
for the underlying classical algebraic stack of
X
.

For any topological space
X
, write
X
for the associated condensed set as in
[
61
, Proposition 1.7]
.

1.
Vector bundles on the global Hartl–Pink curve

In this section, we introduce and study vector bundles on the global Hartl–Pink curve. They are defined using analytifications, so we start by discussing the latter in terms of adic spaces. For example, we prove v-descent for vector bundles on analytifications. This entire discussion applies to any smooth scheme over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.

We then specialize to smooth curves
U
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, which are the main case of interest in this paper. We prove a description of pro-étale
\underline{{\mathscr{O}}_{U}}
-local systems in terms of vector bundles on the global Hartl–Pink curve, analogous to Kedlaya–Liu’s description of pro-étale
\underline{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
-local systems. Finally, we prove a vector bundle version of Theorem C.

1.1.

Analytification has the following interpretation in the category of adic spaces.

Definition
.

Let
X
be a scheme. Let
S
be an adic space over
X
, and let
Y
be a scheme over
X
. Write
Y^{\an}_{S}
, if it exists, for the fiber product
Y\times_{X}S
in the category of adic spaces.

By replacing
X
and
Y
with affine open covers, the
\Spec
-global sections adjunction implies that
Y^{\an}_{S}
equals the “fiber product”
Y\times_{X}S
in the sense of
[
42
, (3.8)]
.

1.2.

We will use the following generalization of
[
54
, Lemma 4.4]
, which describes the functor of points of analytifications without needing to sheafify in the analytic topology. Let
D
be a noetherian ring. Let
S
be an adic space over
D
, and let
Y
be a quasiprojective scheme over
D
.

Lemma
.

For all adic spaces
\Spa(A,A^{+})
over
S
, morphisms
\Spa(A,A^{+})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}Y^{\an}_{S}
over
S
are equivalent to morphisms
\Spec{A}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}Y
over
D
.

Proof.

Since
D
is noetherian and
Y
is quasiprojective over
D
, there exist finitely many homogeneous polynomials
f_{1},\dotsc,f_{l}
and
g_{1},\dotsc,g_{m}
in
D[T_{0},\dotsc,T_{d}]
such that
Y
is the locus in
{\mathbb{P}}_{D}^{d}
where
f_{a}(T_{0},\dotsc,T_{d})
vanishes for all
1\leq a\leq l
and
g_{b}(T_{0},\dotsc,T_{d})
vanishes nowhere for all
1\leq b\leq m
.

By the universal property of
Y^{\an}_{S}
, morphisms
\Spa(A,A^{+})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}Y^{\an}_{S}
over
S
are equivalent to morphisms
\Spa(A,A^{+})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}Y
over
D
. The
\Spec
-global sections adjunction implies that the latter are equivalent to the data of a line bundle
{\mathscr{L}}
on
\Spa(A,A^{+})
equipped with sections
s_{0},\dotsc,s_{d}
generating
{\mathscr{L}}
such that
f_{a}(s_{0},\dotsc,s_{d})
vanishes for all
1\leq a\leq l
and
g_{b}(s_{0},\dotsc,s_{d})
vanishes nowhere for all
1\leq b\leq m
. Finally,
[
46
, Theorem 1.4.2]
shows these are equivalent to morphisms
\Spec{A}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}Y
over
D
.
∎

1.3.

The following lemma lets us compute the global sections of analytifications.

Lemma
.

Let
Y=\Spa(A,A^{+})
be a smooth affinoid adic space over a nonarchimedean field
K
, let
\Spa(R,R^{+})
be an affinoid sousperfectoid adic space over
\Spa{K}
, and write
(A\widehat{\otimes}_{K}R,(A^{+}\widehat{\otimes}_{K^{\circ}}R^{+})^{\sim})
for the base change of
(K,K^{\circ})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(A,A^{+})
to
(R,R^{+})
. Then
\Spa(A\widehat{\otimes}_{K}R,(A^{+}\widehat{\otimes}_{K^{\circ}}R^{+})^{\sim})
is a sousperfectoid adic space.

Proof.

Since
Y
is smooth over
K
,
[
43
, (2.2.8)]
implies that there is a finite rational open cover
\{Y_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}
of
Y
that are rational open subspaces of finite étale covers of closed unit polydisks over
K
. For all
1\leq i\leq m
, write
Y_{i}=\Spa(A_{i},A_{i}^{+})
. Then
A_{i}\widehat{\otimes}_{K}R
is a rational localization of a finite étale algebra over
R\langle T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}\rangle
, so
[
67
, Proposition 6.3.3]
shows that
A_{i}\widehat{\otimes}_{K}R
is sousperfectoid and hence strongly sheafy as in
[
36
, Definition 4.1]
.

Write
(A_{i}\widehat{\otimes}_{K}R,(A_{i}^{+}\widehat{\otimes}_{K^{\circ}}R^{+})^{\sim})
for the base change of
(K,K^{\circ})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(A_{i},A^{+}_{i})
to
(R,R^{+})
. Then
\{\Spa(A_{i}\widehat{\otimes}_{K}R,(A_{i}^{+}\widehat{\otimes}_{K^{\circ}}R^{+})^{\sim})\}_{i=1}^{m}
is a finite rational open cover of

\displaystyle\Spa(A\widehat{\otimes}_{K}R,(A^{+}\widehat{\otimes}_{K^{\circ}}R^{+})^{\sim}),

and its associated Čech complex is obtained by applying
-\widehat{\otimes}_{K}R
to the Čech complex associated with
\{Y_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}
. Because
A
is sheafy, the latter has cohomology
A
in zeroth degree and
0
elsewhere. The exactness of
-\widehat{\otimes}_{K}R
implies that the Čech complex associated with
\{\Spa(A_{i}\widehat{\otimes}_{K}R,(A_{i}^{+}\widehat{\otimes}_{K^{\circ}}R^{+})^{\sim})\}_{i=1}^{m}
has cohomology
A\widehat{\otimes}_{K}R
in zeroth degree and
0
elsewhere, so the result follows from
[
36
, Corollary 4.5]
3
3

3

While
[
36
]
works over
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, the proof of
[
36
, Corollary 4.5]
does not use this assumption.
.
∎

1.4.

We will analytify smooth varieties as follows. Let
Y=\Spec K[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}]/I
be a smooth affine scheme over a nonarchimedean field
K
, and let
S=\Spa(R,R^{+})
be an affinoid sousperfectoid space over
\Spa{K}
. For all pseudouniformizers
\varpi
of
K
, write
B_{S,\varpi}
for the topological ring
R\langle\varpi T_{1},\dotsc,\varpi T_{d}\rangle/I
, and write
B^{+}_{S,\varpi}
for the integral closure in
B_{S,\varpi}
of the image of
R^{+}\langle\varpi T_{1},\dotsc,\varpi T_{d}\rangle
.

Proposition
.

The pre-adic space
Y_{S,\varpi}\coloneqq\Spa(B_{S,\varpi},B^{+}_{S,\varpi})
is a sousperfectoid adic space. Moreover,
Y^{\an}_{S}
equals
\varinjlim_{\varpi}Y_{S,\varpi}
, where
\varpi
runs over pseudouniformizers of
K
and the transition morphisms are open embeddings. Consequently,
Y^{\an}_{S}
exists and is sousperfectoid.

Proof.

Write
A
for
K\langle\varpi T_{1},\dotsc,\varpi T_{d}\rangle/I
, and write
A^{+}
for the integral closure in
A
of the image of
K^{\circ}\langle\varpi T_{1},\dotsc,\varpi T_{d}\rangle
. Since
Y
is smooth over
K
, the affinoid adic space
\Spa(A,A^{+})
is smooth over
K
. Hence the first statement follows from Lemma
1.3
and
(B_{S,\varpi},B_{S,\varpi}^{+})
being the base change of
(K,K^{\circ})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(A,A^{+})
to
(R,R^{+})
. The second statement follows from the universal property of
Y^{\an}_{S}
.
∎

1.5.

Next, we specialize to smooth varieties over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. Let
Y=\Spec{\mathbb{F}}_{q}[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}]/I
be a smooth affine scheme over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, and let
S=\Spa(R,R^{+})
be an affinoid perfectoid space over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. Any pseudouniformizer
\varpi
of
R
induces a morphism

\displaystyle S\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Spa{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(\!(\varpi^{1/q^{\infty}})\!).

By applying Proposition
1.4
to
Y_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(\!(\varpi^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}
, we see that
Y^{\an}_{S}
exists and equals
\bigcup_{\varpi}Y_{S,\varpi}
, where
\varpi
runs over pseudouniformizers of
R
.

To prove v-descent for vector bundles on the analytification of
Y
, we use Frobenius to construct the following natural perfectoid cover. Note that
(\Frob_{Y})_{S}^{\an}
sends
Y_{S,\varpi^{1/q}}
to
Y_{S,\varpi}
. Write
B^{+,\perf}_{S,\varpi}
for the
\varpi
-adic completion of
\varinjlim_{i}B^{+}_{S,\varpi^{1/q^{i}}}
, where
i
runs over non-negative integers and the transition maps are given by
(\Frob_{Y})_{S}^{\an,*}
. Write
B^{\perf}_{S,\varpi}
for
B^{+,\perf}_{S,\varpi}[\textstyle\frac{1}{\varpi}]
. Finally, write
(Y^{\an}_{S})^{\perf}
, if it exists, for the limit
\varprojlim_{i}Y^{\an}_{S}
in the category of uniform analytic adic spaces, where the transition maps are given by
(\Frob_{Y})^{\an}_{S}
.

Proposition
.

The pre-adic space
Y^{\perf}_{S,\varpi}\coloneqq\Spa(B^{\perf}_{S,\varpi},B^{\perf,+}_{S,\varpi})
is affinoid perfectoid. Moreover,
Y^{\perf}_{S,\varpi}
equals the fiber product
Y_{S,\varpi}\times_{Y^{\an}_{S}}(Y^{\an}_{S})^{\perf}
in the category of uniform analytic adic spaces, so
(Y^{\an}_{S})^{\perf}=\bigcup_{\varpi}Y^{\perf}_{S,\varpi}
exists and is perfectoid. Finally, the map
B_{S,\varpi}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}B_{S,\varpi}^{\perf}
of topological
B_{S,\varpi}
-modules splits.

Proof.

For the first statement, note that
(B^{\perf}_{S,\varpi},B^{+,\perf}_{S,\varpi})
is the colimit of

\displaystyle\{(B_{S,\varpi^{1/q^{i}}},B^{+}_{S,\varpi^{1/q^{i}}})\}_{i}

in the category of uniform Tate Huber pairs. Therefore
[
63
, Proposition 3.5]
indicates that it suffices to check that
B^{\perf}_{S,\varpi}
is perfect. Because
S
is perfectoid,
\Frob_{S}
induces an isomorphism
Y_{S,\varpi}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}Y_{S,\varpi^{1/q}}
. Composing this isomorphism with
(\Frob_{Y})^{\an}_{S}:Y_{S,\varpi^{1/q}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}Y_{S,\varpi}
yields the absolute
q
-Frobenius endomorphism, which implies that
B_{S,\varpi}^{\perf}
is indeed perfect.

For the second statement, the above shows that
Y^{\perf}_{S,\varpi}
equals the limit
\varprojlim_{i}Y_{S,\varpi^{1/q^{i}}}
in the category of uniform analytic adic spaces. The universal property of
Y_{S}^{\an}
implies that the square

\textstyle{Y_{S,\varpi^{1/q}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Frob_{Y})_{S}^{\an}}
\textstyle{Y_{S,\varpi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y^{\an}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Frob_{Y})_{S}^{\an}}
\textstyle{Y^{\an}_{S}}

is cartesian, so taking
\varprojlim_{i}
in the category of uniform analytic adic spaces yields the desired result.

For the third statement, the smoothness of
Y
implies that
\Frob_{Y,*}{\mathbb{F}}_{q}[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}]/I
is a finite projective
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}]/I
-module
[
12
, Lemma 1.1.1]
4
4

4

While
[
12
]
works over an algebraically closed field, the proofs of
[
12
, Lemma 1.1.1]
and
[
12
, Proposition 1.1.6]
do not use this assumption.
. Since
Y
is also affine,
[
12
, Proposition 1.1.6]
shows that
Y
is Frobenius-split.

Note that the square

\textstyle{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}]/I\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Frob_{Y}^{*}}
\textstyle{\Frob_{Y,*}{\mathbb{F}}_{q}[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}]/I\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{S,\varpi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Frob_{Y})_{S}^{\an,*}}
\textstyle{B_{S,\varpi^{1/q}}}

is a pushout in the category of rings. Therefore applying
-\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}]/I}B_{S,\varpi}
to an
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}]/I
-module splitting of the top arrow yields a topological
B_{S,\varpi}
-module splitting of the bottom arrow. Finally, taking the completed colimit of
\{B_{S,\varpi^{1/q^{i}}}\}_{i}
yields a topological
B_{S,\varpi}
-module splitting of
B_{S,\varpi}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}B^{\perf}_{S,\varpi}
.
∎

1.6.

We now prove v-descent for vector bundles on (open subspaces of) analytifications. Let
Y
be a smooth scheme over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, and let
V_{S}
be an open subspace of
Y^{\an}_{S}
. For all affinoid perfectoid spaces
S^{\prime}
over
S
, write
V_{S^{\prime}}
for the preimage of
V_{S}
.

Theorem
.

The presheaf of categories on
\Perf_{S}
given by

\displaystyle S^{\prime}\mapsto\{\mbox{vector bundles on }V_{S^{\prime}}\}

satisfies v-descent.

Proof.

By replacing
Y
and
V_{S}
with open covers, we can assume that

\displaystyle Y=\Spec{\mathbb{F}}_{q}[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}]/I

is affine and that
V_{S}=\Spa(A,A^{+})
is affinoid. Let
S^{\prime}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}S
be an affinoid perfectoid v-cover. Since
Y^{\an}_{S}=\bigcup_{\varpi}Y_{S,\varpi}
, we see that
V_{S}
lies in
Y_{S,\varpi}
for some pseudouniformizer
\varpi
of
R
. Write
V^{\perf}_{S}
for the preimage of
V_{S}
in
Y^{\perf}_{S,\varpi}
.

Let
{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}
be a vector bundle on
V_{S^{\prime}}
with descent datum
\alpha
with respect to

\displaystyle V_{S^{\prime}\times_{S}S^{\prime}\times_{S}S^{\prime}}\rightrightarrows\hskip-10.00002pt\to V_{S^{\prime}\times_{S}S^{\prime}}\rightrightarrows V_{S^{\prime}}.

By Proposition
1.5
, pullback yields a vector bundle
\widetilde{{\mathscr{E}}}^{\prime}
on
V_{S^{\prime}}^{\perf}
with commuting descent data
\widetilde{\alpha}
with respect to the adic spaces

\displaystyle V^{\perf}_{S^{\prime}\times_{S}S^{\prime}\times_{S}S^{\prime}}\rightrightarrows\hskip-10.00002pt\to V^{\perf}_{S^{\prime}\times_{S}S^{\prime}}\rightrightarrows V^{\perf}_{S^{\prime}}

and
\beta^{\prime}
with respect to the affinoid pre-adic spaces

\displaystyle V^{\perf}_{S^{\prime}}\times_{V_{S^{\prime}}}V^{\perf}_{S^{\prime}}\times_{V_{S^{\prime}}}V^{\perf}_{S^{\prime},\varpi}\rightrightarrows\hskip-10.00002pt\to V^{\perf}_{S^{\prime}}\times_{V_{S^{\prime}}}V^{\perf}_{S^{\prime}}\rightrightarrows V^{\perf}_{S^{\prime}}.

Proposition
1.5
indicates that the
V^{\perf}_{(-)}
are perfectoid, so
[
67
, Lemma 17.1.8]
enables us to descend
(\widetilde{{\mathscr{E}}}^{\prime},\widetilde{\alpha})
and
(\beta,\widetilde{\alpha})
to a vector bundle
\widetilde{{\mathscr{E}}}
on
V^{\perf}_{S}
with descent datum
\beta
with respect to the affinoid pre-adic spaces

\displaystyle V^{\perf}_{S}\times_{V_{S}}V^{\perf}_{S}\times_{V_{S}}V^{\perf}_{S}\rightrightarrows\hskip-10.00002pt\to V^{\perf}_{S}\times_{V_{S}}V^{\perf}_{S}\rightrightarrows V^{\perf}_{S}.

Finally, Proposition
1.5
shows that the map
B_{S,\varpi}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}B^{\perf}_{B,\varpi}
of topological
B_{S,\varpi}
-modules splits, so the map
A\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}A\widehat{\otimes}_{B_{S,\varpi}}B^{\perf}_{B,\varpi}
of topological
A
-modules also splits. Hence
[
68
, Tag 08XA]
and
[
68
, Tag 08XD]
enable us to descend
(\widetilde{{\mathscr{E}}},\beta)
to a vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}
on
V_{S}
, as desired.
∎

1.7.

The Frobenius-invariants of global sections of analytifications are given as follows. Write
A
for the global sections of
Y
, endowed with the discrete topology.

Proposition
.

The natural map
\underline{A}(S)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Gamma(Y^{\an}_{S},{\mathscr{O}}_{Y^{\an}_{S}})^{\Frob_{S}^{*}=1}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

By replacing
Y
with an open cover, we can assume that

\displaystyle Y=\Spec{\mathbb{F}}_{q}[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}]/I

is affine. Let
\{e_{m}\}_{m}
be an
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-basis of
A
consisting of images of monomials. For all pseudouniformizers
\varpi
of
R
, this induces an identification
\widehat{\bigoplus}_{m}R\cdot e_{m}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}B_{S,\varpi}
via sending
T_{i}
to
\varpi T_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq d
. Under this identification,
\Frob_{S}^{*}:B_{\varpi,S}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}B_{\varpi^{q},S}
acts coordinatewise, so
\bigoplus_{m}R^{\Frob_{S}^{*}=1}\cdot e_{m}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}B_{S,\varpi}^{\Frob_{S}^{*}=1}
. Now
[
47
, Corollary 3.1.4]
5
5

5

While
[
47
, Corollary 3.1.4]
is stated for
q=p
, the proof applies verbatim for general
q
.
implies that
\Cont(\absolutevalue{S},{\mathbb{F}}_{q})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}R^{\Frob_{S}^{*}=1}
, so the quasicompactness of
\absolutevalue{S}
shows that

\displaystyle\underline{A}(S)=\Cont(\absolutevalue{S},A)=\Cont\big(\absolutevalue{S},\bigoplus_{m}{\mathbb{F}}_{q}\cdot e_{m}\big)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\bigoplus_{m}R^{\Frob_{S}^{*}=1}\cdot e_{m}.

Finally, note that the global sections of
Y^{\an}_{S}
are given by
\bigcap_{\varpi}B_{S,\varpi}
, where
\varpi
runs over pseudouniformizers of
R
. Therefore taking
\bigcap_{\varpi}
yields the claim.
∎

1.8.

Finally, we specialize to smooth curves over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, which are the main case of interest in this paper. Let
C
be a geometrically connected smooth proper curve over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. Write
F
for its function field, and write
{\mathbb{A}}
for its adele ring. Let
U
be a dense open subscheme of
C
, and write
{\mathbb{O}}_{U}
for its ring of integral adeles. When
U
equals
C
, we omit it from our notation.

For all closed points
v
of
C
, write
{\mathbb{F}}_{v}
for its residue field, fix an embedding
{\mathbb{F}}_{v}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, write
{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
for the completed local ring at
v
, and write
F_{v}
for the fraction field of
{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
. Let
A_{v}
be one of
\{{\mathcal{O}}_{v},F_{v}\}
. By applying
[
25
, Proposition II.1.1]
to
\Spa{{\mathbb{F}}_{v}}\times S
, we see that
\Spa{A_{v}}\times S
is a sousperfectoid adic space.

We define vector bundles on the
global
and
local Hartl–Pink curves
as follows.

Definition
.

a)

Write
\Bun_{U}(S)
for the category of vector bundles
{\mathscr{E}}
on
U^{\an}_{S}
equipped with an isomorphism
\phi:{\mathscr{E}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Frob^{*}_{S}{\mathscr{E}}
.

b)

Write
\Bun_{F}(S)
for the category
\varinjlim_{U}\Bun_{U}(S)
, where
U
runs over dense open subschemes of
C
.

c)

Write
\Bun_{A_{v}}(S)
for the category of vector bundles
{\mathscr{E}}
on
\Spa{A_{v}}\times S
equipped with an isomorphism
\phi:{\mathscr{E}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Frob^{*}_{S}{\mathscr{E}}
.

Theorem
1.6
indicates that
\Bun_{U}
satisfies v-descent. Proposition
1.7
implies that the transition morphisms in b) are faithful, so
\Bun_{F}
also satisfies v-descent.

1.9.

Usually, the local Hartl–Pink curve for
F_{v}
is defined using
q^{\deg{v}}
-Frobenius instead of
q
-Frobenius. We now relate these two definitions. Write
X_{S,F_{v}}
for the relative Fargues–Fontaine curve for
F_{v}
as in
[
25
, Definition I.2.2]
.

Proposition
.

The presheaf of categories
\Bun_{A_{v}}
satisfies v-descent, and its base change to
\Perf_{{\mathbb{F}}_{v}}
is naturally isomorphic to

\displaystyle S\mapsto\left\{\begin{tabular}[]{c}vector bundles ${\mathscr{E}}$ on $\Spa{A_{v}}\times_{{\mathbb{F}}_{v}}S$ equipped\\
with an isomorphism $\phi:{\mathscr{E}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}(\Frob_{S}^{\deg{v}})^{*}{\mathscr{E}}$\end{tabular}\right\}.

Consequently, the base change of
\Bun_{F_{v}}
to
\Perf_{{\mathbb{F}}_{v}}
is naturally isomorphic to

\displaystyle S\mapsto\{\mbox{vector bundles on }X_{S,F_{v}}\}.

Proof.

For the first statement, let
S^{\prime}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}S
be an affinoid perfectoid v-cover. Then
\Spa{\mathbb{F}}_{v}\times S^{\prime}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Spa{\mathbb{F}}_{v}\times S
is also an affinoid perfectoid v-cover, so the first statement follows from
[
67
, Proposition 19.5.3]
. The second statement follows from arguing as in the proof of
[
54
, Lemma 5.12]
. Finally, the third statement follows from

\displaystyle X_{S,F_{v}}=(\Spa{F_{v}}\times_{{\mathbb{F}}_{v}}S)/\Frob^{\deg{v}}_{S}.\qed

1.10.

Thanks to Artin–Schreier–Witt,
\Bun_{{\mathcal{O}}_{v}}
enjoys the following description.

Proposition
.

The presheaf of categories
\Bun_{{\mathcal{O}}_{v}}
is naturally isomorphic to

\displaystyle S\mapsto\{\mbox{pro-\'{e}tale }\underline{{\mathcal{O}}_{v}}\mbox{-local systems on }S\}.

Proof.

After replacing
(-)[\![\pi]\!]
with
-\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}{\mathbb{F}}_{v}[\![\pi]\!]
, where
\pi
denotes a uniformizer of
{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
, the proof proceeds as in
[
54
, Proposition 3.7]
and
[
54
, Theorem 3.12]
.
∎

1.11.

The following relationship between vector bundles on the global and local Hartl–Pink curves plays an important role in this paper. Write
Z
for the closed complement
C\smallsetminus U
. For all closed points
u
of
U
, we have a natural morphism of adic spaces
\Spa{{\mathcal{O}}_{u}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}U
, and for all
z
in
Z
, we have a natural morphism of adic spaces
\Spa{F_{z}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}U
. Therefore pullback yields morphisms

\displaystyle\loc_{u}:\Bun_{U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{{\mathcal{O}}_{u}}\mbox{ and }\loc_{z}:\Bun_{U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{F_{z}}.

Definition
.

a)

Write

\displaystyle\loc:\Bun_{U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\prod_{u}\Bun_{{\mathcal{O}}_{u}}\times\prod_{z}\Bun_{F_{z}}

for the morphism induced by the
\{\loc_{u}\}_{u}
and
\{\loc_{z}\}_{z}
, where
u
runs over closed points of
U
, and
z
runs over
Z
.

b)

Write
\Bun_{\mathbb{A}}
for the presheaf of categories
\varinjlim_{U}\textstyle\prod_{u}\Bun_{{\mathcal{O}}_{u}}\times\textstyle\prod_{z}\Bun_{F_{z}}
, where
U
runs over dense open subschemes of
C
.

c)

Write
\loc:\Bun_{F}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{\mathbb{A}}
for the morphism obtained by taking
\varinjlim_{U}
of a).

Proposition
1.9
implies that
\Bun_{\mathbb{A}}
satisfies v-descent.

1.12.

We will use the following lemma to reduce to the situation where
\Pic{U}=1
. Because
U
is Dedekind,
\Pic{U}=1
if and only if all vector bundles on
U
are trivial. Since
U
is a smooth curve, this implies that
U
is affine.

Lemma
.

There exists an open cover
\{C_{1},C_{2}\}
of
C
satisfying
\Pic{C_{1}}=\Pic{C_{2}}=1
and hence an open cover
\{U_{1},U_{2}\}
of
U
satisfying
\Pic{U_{1}}=\Pic{U_{2}}=1
.

Proof.

The finitude of
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
implies that
\Pic{C}
is finitely generated. Let
{\mathscr{L}}_{1},\dotsc,{\mathscr{L}}_{g}
be generators of
\Pic{C}
, and let
C_{1}
be a dense open subscheme of
C
such that
{\mathscr{L}}_{i}|_{C_{1}}
is trivial for all
1\leq i\leq g
. Then excision for class groups implies that
\Pic{C_{1}}=1
. Because
C\smallsetminus C_{1}
is finite, weak approximation shows that the order-of-vanishing map
F^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}{\mathbb{Z}}^{\oplus(C\smallsetminus C_{1})}
is surjective, so for all
1\leq i\leq g
, there exists a divisor
D_{i}
supported on
C_{1}
such that
{\mathscr{L}}_{i}\cong{\mathscr{O}}_{C}(D_{i})
. By taking
C_{2}=C\smallsetminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{g}\supp{D_{i}}
, excision for class groups again implies that
\Pic{C_{2}}=1
. Finally, the analogous statements hold for
U_{1}=U\cap C_{1}
and
U_{2}=U\cap C_{2}
by excision for class groups.
∎

1.13.

We will use the following notion of local systems on
S
with coefficients in
{\mathscr{O}}_{U}
, which is local both in
S
and in
U
. For any
{\mathscr{E}}
in
\QCoh(U)
, write
\underline{{\mathscr{E}}}
for the constant
\QCoh(U)
-valued sheaf on the site
*_{\text{pro-\'{e}t}}
induced by
{\mathscr{E}}
.

Definition
.

A
pro-étale
\underline{{\mathscr{O}}_{U}}
-local system on
S
is an
\underline{{\mathscr{O}}_{U}}
-module on the site
S_{\text{pro-\'{e}t}}
that, locally in
S_{\text{pro-\'{e}t}}
, is of the form
\underline{{\mathscr{E}}}
for some vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}
on
U
.

When
\Pic{U}=1
, note that this agrees with the notion of pro-étale
\underline{A}
-local systems, where
A
denotes the global sections of
U
endowed with the discrete topology.

1.14.

We now prove a description of pro-étale
\underline{{\mathscr{O}}_{U}}
-local systems in terms of vector bundles on the global Hartl–Pink curve; this is the global analogue of a theorem of Kedlaya–Liu
[
47
, Theorem 8.5.12]
.

We say that an object in
\Bun_{F_{v}}(S)

has slope zero
if, under the identification from Proposition
1.9
, its image in
\Bun_{F_{v}}(\Spa{\mathbb{F}}_{v}\times S)
has slope zero Harder–Narasimhan polygon as in
[
25
, p. 74]
. Note that this agrees with the usual notion when
S
lies over
{\mathbb{F}}_{v}
. Moreover, for all
({\mathscr{E}},\phi)
in
\Bun_{G,{\mathcal{O}}_{v}}(S)
, its image in
\Bun_{G,F_{v}}(S)
has slope zero.

Theorem
.

We have an exact tensor equivalence of categories

\displaystyle\{\mbox{pro-\'{e}tale }\underline{{\mathscr{O}}_{U}}\mbox{-local systems on }S\}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\left\{\begin{tabular}[]{c}objects in $\Bun_{U}(S)$ whose image in\\
$\Bun_{F_{z}}(S)$ has slope zero for all $z$ in $Z$\end{tabular}\right\}

given by
{\mathbb{L}}\mapsto({\mathbb{L}}\otimes_{\underline{{\mathscr{O}}_{U}}}{\mathscr{O}}_{U^{\an}_{S}},{\mathbb{L}}\otimes_{\underline{{\mathscr{O}}_{U}}}(\Frob^{-1}_{S})^{*})
.

Proof.

By replacing
U
with the open cover
\{U_{1},U_{2}\}
from Lemma
1.12
, we can assume that
\Pic{U}=1
. Note that the above functor preserves tensor products and duals. Hence taking internal homs reduces full faithfulness to proving that, for all
\underline{A}
-local systems
{\mathbb{L}}
on
S
, the map

\displaystyle\Hom_{\underline{A}}(\underline{A},{\mathbb{L}})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Hom_{{\mathscr{O}}_{U^{\an}_{S}}}(({\mathscr{O}}_{U^{\an}_{S}},(\Frob_{S}^{-1})^{*}),({\mathbb{L}}\otimes_{\underline{A}}{\mathscr{O}}_{U^{\an}_{S}},{\mathbb{L}}\otimes_{\underline{A}}(\Frob_{S}^{-1})^{*}))

is a bijection. By replacing
S
with a pro-étale cover and using Theorem
1.6
, we can assume that
{\mathbb{L}}=\underline{A}
. Then the result follows from Proposition
1.7
.

For essential surjectivity, let
({\mathscr{E}},\phi)
be an object in
\Bun_{U}(S)
. By full faithfulness and pro-étale descent, it suffices to prove that there exists an affinoid perfectoid pro-étale cover
S^{\prime}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}S
such that
({\mathscr{E}},\phi)|_{S^{\prime}}
is trivial. Since
\loc_{z}({\mathscr{E}},\phi)
has slope zero for all
z
in
Z
,
[
25
, Theorem II.2.19]
yields an affinoid perfectoid pro-étale cover
S^{\prime}=\Spa(R^{\prime},R^{\prime+})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}S
such that, for all
z
in
Z
, the base change
\loc_{z}({\mathscr{E}},\phi)|_{S^{\prime}}
is trivial. Now
\{U^{\an}_{S^{\prime}}\}\cup\{\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z}\times S^{\prime}\}_{z}
is an open cover of
C^{\an}_{S^{\prime}}
, so we can glue
({\mathscr{E}},\phi)|_{S^{\prime}}
with the trivial object in
\Bun_{{\mathcal{O}}_{z}}(S^{\prime})
for all
z
in
Z
to obtain an object
({\mathscr{E}}^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
in
\Bun_{C}(S^{\prime})
. Theorem
A.19
shows that
({\mathscr{E}}^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
is the analytification of a vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime\alg}
on
C_{R^{\prime}}
equipped with an isomorphism
\phi^{\prime\alg}:{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime\alg}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Frob_{R^{\prime}}^{*}{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime\alg}
. After replacing
\Spec{R^{\prime}}
with a clopen cover,
[
50
, Lemme 3 du paragraphe I.3]
indicates that
({\mathscr{E}}^{\prime\alg},\phi^{\prime\alg})
is isomorphic to
({\mathscr{E}}^{\alg}|_{R^{\prime}},\id_{{\mathscr{E}}^{\alg}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}(\Frob_{R^{\prime}}^{-1})^{*})
for some vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}^{\alg}
on
C
. Finally,
\Pic{U}=1
implies that
{\mathscr{E}}^{\alg}|_{U}
is trivial.
∎

1.15.

Let
B
be a perfect
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-algebra, endowed with the discrete topology. We conclude this section by proving that, in many situations, vector bundles on
U_{B}
are equivalent to vector bundles on “
U_{\Spd B}
”.

What do we mean by “
U_{\Spd B}
”? Note that
\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Spd{B}
is surjective and representable in perfectoid spaces, so we have a natural diagram of perfectoids

(
\star
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\times_{\Spd{B}}\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\times_{\Spd{B}}\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\\
&\rightrightarrows\hskip-10.00002pt\to\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\times_{\Spd{B}}\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\rightrightarrows\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}.\end{split}

Theorem
.

Assume that each connected component of
\Spec{B}
is a valuation ring. Then analytification induces an exact tensor equivalence from

\displaystyle\{\mbox{vector bundles on }U_{B}\}

to vector bundles on
U_{\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}}^{\an}
with descent datum with respect to

(
\star\star
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&U_{\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\times_{\Spd{B}}\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\times_{\Spd{B}}\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}}^{\an}\\
&\rightrightarrows\hskip-10.00002pt\to U_{\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\times_{\Spd{B}}\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}}^{\an}\rightrightarrows U_{\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}}^{\an}.\end{split}

Proof.

For full faithfulness, let
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
and
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
be vector bundles on
U_{B}
, write
({\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{1},\alpha_{1})
and
({\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2},\alpha_{2})
for the corresponding vector bundles on
U_{\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}}^{\an}
with descent data with respect to (

⋆
⁣
⋆

‣
Theorem
), and let
f^{\prime}:{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{1}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{2}
be a morphism of vector bundles compatible with the descent data. By replacing
U
with an open cover, we can assume that
U=\Spec{A}
is affine. Write
A={\mathbb{F}}_{q}[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}]/I
. For all affinoid perfectoid spaces
S=\Spa(R,R^{+})
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, Lemma
1.3
shows that

\displaystyle U_{S,1}\coloneqq\Spa(R\langle T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}\rangle/I,(R^{+}\langle T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}\rangle)^{\sim})

is an open subspace of
U^{\an}_{S}
. For all affinoid perfectoid spaces
S^{\prime}
over
S
, note that the preimage of
U_{S,1}
in
U_{S^{\prime}}^{\an}
equals
U_{S^{\prime},1}
, so restricting (

⋆
⁣
⋆

‣
Theorem
) to
U_{\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)},1}
yields

\displaystyle U_{\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\times_{\Spd{B}}\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\times_{\Spd{B}}\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)},1}

\displaystyle\rightrightarrows\hskip-10.00002pt\to U_{\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\times_{\Spd{B}}\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)},1}\rightrightarrows U_{\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)},1}.

Since
I
is finitely generated, for all positive integers
m
, we have

\displaystyle B(\!(t_{1}^{1/q^{\infty}},\dotsc,t_{m}^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)\langle T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}\rangle/I=\big(B[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{d}]/I\big)(\!(t_{1}^{1/q^{\infty}},\dotsc,t_{m}^{1/q^{\infty}})\!),

so taking global sections yields

\displaystyle(A\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}B)(\!(t_{1}^{1/q^{\infty}},t_{2}^{1/q^{\infty}},t_{3}^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)\leftleftarrows\hskip-10.00002pt\leftarrow(A\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}B)(\!(t_{1}^{1/q^{\infty}},t_{2}^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)\leftleftarrows(A\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}B)(\!(t_{1}^{1/q^{\infty}})\!).

Therefore
[
48
, Theorem 4.9]
indicates that the restriction of
f^{\prime}
to
U_{\Spa B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!),1}
arises uniquely from a morphism
f:{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
of vector bundles on
U_{B}
. Since morphisms of vector bundles on
U^{\an}_{S}
are determined by their restrictions to
U_{S,1}
, this yields the desired result.

For essential surjectivity, let
{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}
be a vector bundle on
U^{\an}_{\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}}
with descent datum
\alpha
with respect to (

⋆
⁣
⋆

‣
Theorem
). For all
z
in
Z
, pullback yields a vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}_{z}
on
\Spa{F_{z}}\times\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}
with descent datum
\alpha_{z}
with respect to the evaluation of
\Spa{F_{z}}\times(-)
on (

⋆

‣
1.15
), and applying
[
32
, Theorem 3.15]
and
[
32
, Remark 3.14]
to
{\mathbb{F}}_{v}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}B
shows that
({\mathscr{E}}_{z}^{\prime},\alpha_{z})
arises uniquely from a vector bundle
{\mathscr{E}}_{z}
on
\Spec{F_{z}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}B}
. After replacing
\Spec{B}
by a clopen cover,
[
45
, Theorem 6.1]
indicates that
{\mathscr{E}}_{z}
is trivial. Now
\{U^{\an}_{(-)}\}\cup\{\Spa{{\mathcal{O}}_{z}}\times(-)\}_{z}
is an open cover of
C^{\an}_{(-)}
, so we can glue
({\mathscr{E}}^{\prime},\alpha)
to the trivial vector bundle on
\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z}\times\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}
with descent datum with respect to the evaluation of
\Spa{{\mathcal{O}}_{z}}\times(-)
on (

⋆

‣
1.15
). This yields a vector bundle
\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}}^{\prime}
on
C^{\an}_{\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}}
with descent datum
\widehat{\alpha}
with respect to

\displaystyle C_{\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\times_{\Spd{B}}\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\times_{\Spd{B}}\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}}^{\an}

\displaystyle\rightrightarrows\hskip-10.00002pt\to C_{\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\times_{\Spd{B}}\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}}^{\an}\rightrightarrows C_{\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}}^{\an}.

Theorem
A.19
shows that this corresponds to a vector bundle
\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}}^{\prime\alg}
on
C_{B(\!(t_{1}^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}
with, for all positive integers
n
, a descent datum
\widehat{\alpha}_{n}
with respect to

(
\star\star\star
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&C_{B[\![t_{1}^{1/q^{\infty}},t_{2}^{1/q^{\infty}},t_{3}^{1/q^{\infty}}]\!]\langle t_{1}^{n}/t_{2}t_{3},t_{2}^{n}/t_{1}t_{3},t_{3}^{n}/t_{1}t_{2}\rangle[1/t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}]}\\
&\rightrightarrows\hskip-10.00002pt\to C_{B[\![t_{1}^{1/q^{\infty}},t_{2}^{1/q^{\infty}}]\!]\langle t_{1}^{n}/t_{2},t_{2}^{n}/t_{1}\rangle[1/t_{1}t_{2}]}\rightrightarrows C_{B(\!(t_{1}^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}.\end{split}

Let
\{C_{1},C_{2}\}
be an affine open cover of
C
. For all
i
in
\{1,2\}
, write
A_{i}
for the global sections of
C_{i}
, and note that
A_{i}
is free over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. Hence restricting (

⋆
⋆
⋆

‣
1.15
) to
(C_{i})_{B(\!(t_{1}^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}
, taking global sections, and taking
\varprojlim_{n}
yields

\displaystyle A_{i}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}B(\!(t_{1}^{1/q^{\infty}},t_{2}^{1/q^{\infty}},t_{3}^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)\leftleftarrows\hskip-10.00002pt\leftarrow A_{i}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}B(\!(t_{1}^{1/q^{\infty}},t_{2}^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)\leftleftarrows A_{i}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}B(\!(t_{1}^{1/q^{\infty}})\!).

Therefore
[
48
, Theorem 4.9]
indicates that the restriction of
(\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}}^{\prime},\widehat{\alpha})
to
(C_{i})_{(-),1}
arises uniquely from a vector bundle
\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}}_{i}
on
(C_{i})_{B}
. Now
\{(C_{1})_{(-),1},(C_{2})_{(-),1}\}
is an open cover of
C_{(-)}^{\an}
and
\{(C_{1})_{B},(C_{2})_{B}\}
is an open cover of
C_{B}
, so
(\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}}^{\prime},\widehat{\alpha})
arises uniquely from a vector bundle
\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}}
on
C_{B}
. Finally, restrict
\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}}
to
U_{B}
.
∎

2.
G
-bundles

In this section, we introduce
G
-bundles on the global Hartl–Pink curve. After defining the moduli stack
\Bun_{G,F}
thereof, we define its
localization map
to a product of local moduli stacks
\Bun_{G,F_{z}}
, which plays an important role in this paper. For example, we use the localization map to define the global semisimple locus
\Bun_{G,F}^{\semis}
in
\Bun_{G,F}
. Using results from §
1
, we prove part of Theorem A, part of Theorem B (though we postpone the official statement of Theorem B to §
4
), and Theorem C.

2.1.

Let
G
be a parahoric group scheme over
C
as in
[
57
, Definition 2.18]
. We also write
G
for its base change to
U
,
F
,
{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
, or
F_{v}
for any closed point
v
of
C
.

We have the following standard Tannakian description of
G
-torsors. Let
Y
be a sousperfectoid space over
C
, and let
{\mathscr{G}}
be an étale
G
-torsor on
Y
. For all
V
in
\Rep_{C}{G}
, write
{\mathscr{G}}(V)
for the locally free
{\mathscr{O}}_{Y,\et}
-module
{\mathscr{G}}\times^{G}(V\otimes_{{\mathscr{O}}_{C}}{\mathscr{O}}_{Y,\et})
, which arises uniquely from a vector bundle
{\mathscr{G}}(V)
on
Y
by
[
47
, Theorem 8.2.22 (d)]
.

Proposition
.

The above induces an equivalence of categories between

a)

étale
G
-torsors on
Y
,

b)

exact tensor functors
\Rep_{C}{G}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\{\mbox{vector bundles on }Y\}
.

Moreover, when
Y=\Spa(T,T^{+})
is affinoid, all étale
G
-torsors on
Y
are representable by adic spaces, and the above are naturally equivalent to

c)

étale
G
-torsors on
\Spec{T}
,

d)

exact tensor functors
\Rep_{C}{G}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\{\mbox{finite projective }T\mbox{-modules}\}
.

Proof.

By
[
13
, Lemma 3.1]
,
{\mathscr{O}}_{G}
is a filtered colimit of objects in
\Rep_{C}{G}
. Hence the result follows from the proof of
[
55
, Proposition 6.10]
.
∎

2.2.

We now define the stack of
G
-bundles on the global and local Hartl–Pink curves. Let
A_{v}
be one of
\{{\mathcal{O}}_{v},F_{v}\}
.

Definition
.

a)

Write
\Bun_{G,U}(S)
for the groupoid of
G
-torsors
{\mathscr{G}}
on
U^{\an}_{S}
equipped with an isomorphism
\phi:{\mathscr{G}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Frob_{S}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}
.

b)

Write
\Bun_{G,F}(S)
for the groupoid
\varinjlim_{U}\Bun_{G,U}(S)
, where
U
runs over dense open subschemes of
C
.

c)

Write
\Bun_{G,A_{v}}(S)
for the groupoid of
G
-torsors
{\mathscr{G}}
on
\Spa{A_{v}}\times S
equipped with an isomorphism
\phi:{\mathscr{G}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Frob_{S}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}
.

Using Proposition
2.1
, Theorem
1.6
indicates that
\Bun_{G,U}
is a v-stack. Proposition
1.7
implies that the transition morphisms in b) are faithful, so
\Bun_{G,F}
is also a v-stack. Finally, Proposition
1.9
indicates that
\Bun_{G,A_{v}}
is a v-stack, and the proof of
[
25
, Proposition III.1.3]
implies that all of these v-stacks are small.

2.3 Example
.

We claim that
\Bun_{G,C}
is naturally isomorphic to the constant stack over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
associated with the groupoid

(
\heartsuit
)

\displaystyle\coprod_{\alpha\in\ker^{1}(F,G)}G_{\alpha}(F)\backslash G_{\alpha}({\mathbb{A}})/G({\mathbb{O}}),

where
\ker^{1}(F,G)
denotes the kernel of the localization map

\displaystyle(\loc_{v})_{v}:H^{1}(F,G)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\prod_{v}H^{1}(F_{v},G),

and
G_{\alpha}
over
F
denotes the inner twist of
G
associated with
\alpha
. To see this, write
{\mathcal{B}}_{G}
for the smooth algebraic stack over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
of
G
-bundles on
C
. Then Theorem
A.19
identifies
S
-points of
\Bun_{G,C}
with
\Spec{R}
-points of the algebraic stack
({\mathcal{B}}_{G})^{\Frob_{{\mathcal{B}}_{G}}}
of
\Frob_{{\mathcal{B}}_{G}}
-fixed points of
{\mathcal{B}}_{G}
. By
[
69
, Lemma 3.3 b)]
, the latter is naturally isomorphic to the constant stack over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
associated with the groupoid
{\mathcal{B}}_{G}({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
, and
[
51
, Remarque 12.2]
identifies
{\mathcal{B}}_{G}({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
with (

♡

‣
2.3
). Since its automorphism groups are finite, the claim follows from the natural equivalence
(\Spec{R})_{\text{f\'{e}t}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}S_{\text{f\'{e}t}}
.

2.4.

In a manner analogous to Proposition
1.9
, the trivial locus in the stack of
G
-bundles on the Fargues–Fontaine curve descends to our setting as follows. Write
\Bun^{1}_{G,F_{v}}
for the substack of
\Bun_{G,F_{v}}(S)
whose
S
-points consist of objects such that, for all geometric points
\overline{s}
of
S
, its image in
\Bun_{G,F_{v}}(\overline{s})
is trivial. Consider the morphism
*\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{G,F_{v}}
corresponding to the trivial object for all
S
, which factors through a morphism
*\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun^{1}_{G,F_{v}}
. One can identify
\underline{G(F_{v})}
with
*\times_{\Bun_{G,F_{v}}^{1}}*
as group v-sheaves, so descent yields a morphism
*/\underline{G(F_{v})}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun^{1}_{G,F_{v}}
.

Proposition
.

The substack
\Bun^{1}_{G,F_{v}}\subseteq\Bun_{G,F_{v}}
is open, and
*/\underline{G(F_{v})}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun^{1}_{G,F_{v}}
is an isomorphism. Moreover,
\Bun_{G,{\mathcal{O}}_{v}}
is naturally isomorphic to
*/\underline{G({\mathcal{O}}_{v})}
. Finally, the base change of
\Bun_{G,F_{v}}
to
{\mathbb{F}}_{v}
is naturally isomorphic to the stack of
G
-bundles on the Fargues–Fontaine curve for
F_{v}
as in
[
25
, Definition III.1.3]
.

Proof.

After replacing
(-)[\![\pi]\!]
with
-\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}{\mathbb{F}}_{v}[\![\pi]\!]
, where
\pi
denotes a uniformizer of
{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
, the first statement follows from the proof of
[
25
, Theorem III.2.4]
, and the second statement follows from the proof of
[
54
, Proposition 3.8]
and
[
54
, Theorem 3.12]
. The third statement follows from Proposition
1.9
.
∎

2.5.

The following
localization maps
play an important role in this paper. For all closed points
u
in
U
and
z
in
Z
, pullback yields morphisms

\displaystyle\loc_{u}:\Bun_{G,U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{G,{\mathcal{O}}_{u}}\mbox{ and }\loc_{z}:\Bun_{G,U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}.

Definition
.

a)

Write

\displaystyle\loc:\Bun_{G,U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\prod_{u}\Bun_{G,{\mathcal{O}}_{u}}\times\prod_{z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}

for the morphism induced by the
\{\loc_{u}\}_{u}
and
\{\loc_{z}\}_{z}
, where
u
runs over closed points of
U
, and
z
runs over
Z
.

b)

Write
\Bun_{G,{\mathbb{A}}}
for the prestack
\varinjlim_{U}\textstyle\prod_{u}\Bun_{G,{\mathcal{O}}_{u}}\times\textstyle\prod_{z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}
on
\Perf_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
, where
U
runs over dense open subschemes of
C
.

c)

Write
\loc:\Bun_{G,F}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{G,{\mathbb{A}}}
for the morphism obtained by taking
\varinjlim_{U}
of a).

Proposition
2.4
implies that the transition morphisms in b) are faithful, so
\Bun_{G,{\mathbb{A}}}
is a small v-stack.

2.6.

When studying
\Bun_{G,U}
, one has the following mechanism for changing
U
.

Lemma
.

For all dense open subschemes
U^{\prime}
of
U
, the square

\textstyle{\Bun_{G,U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\loc}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\prod_{u}\Bun_{G,{\mathcal{O}}_{u}}\times\prod_{z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G,U^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\loc}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\prod_{u^{\prime}}\Bun_{G,{\mathcal{O}}_{u^{\prime}}}\times\prod_{z^{\prime}}\Bun_{G,F_{z^{\prime}}}}

is cartesian, where
u
runs over closed points of
U^{\prime}
, and
z^{\prime}
runs over
C\smallsetminus U^{\prime}
. Similarly, the square

\textstyle{\Bun_{G,U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\loc}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\prod_{u}\Bun_{G,{\mathcal{O}}_{u}}\times\prod_{z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G,F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\loc}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G,{\mathbb{A}}}}

is cartesian.

Proof.

Note that
\{U^{\prime\an}_{S}\}\cup\{\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{c}\times S\}_{c}
is an open cover of
U^{\an}_{S}
, where
c
runs over
U\smallsetminus U^{\prime}
. Therefore the first statement follows from gluing, and the second statement follows from taking
\varinjlim_{U^{\prime}}
of the first statement.
∎

2.7.

We
use
the semistable locus in the stack of
G
-bundles on the local Hartl–Pink curve to define a global analogue as follows. Write
\Bun^{\semis}_{G,F_{v}}(S)
for the full subcategory of objects in

equals the semistable locus as in
[

, Section III.4.2]
.

Definition
.

a)

Write
\Bun_{G,U}^{\semis}
for the preimage of
\prod_{u}\Bun_{G,{\mathcal{O}}_{u}}\times\prod_{z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}^{\semis}
in

under

.

on

.

As in Definition

, Lemma

.

2.8.

The stack of
G
-bundles on the global Hartl–Pink curve also has a trivial locus in the following sense. Write
\Bun^{1}_{G,U}
for the substack of
\Bun_{G,U}
whose
S
-points consist of objects such that, for all geometric points
\overline{s}
of
S
, its image in
\Bun_{G,U}(\overline{s})
is trivial. Consider the morphism
*\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{G,U}
corresponding to the trivial object for all
S
, which factors through a morphism
*\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{G,U}^{1}
. Proposition
1.7
identifies
\underline{G(A)}
with
*\times_{\Bun_{G,U}^{1}}*
as group v-sheaves, so descent yields a morphism

\displaystyle*/\underline{G(A)}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{G,U}^{1}.

Theorem
.

The substack
\Bun^{1}_{G,U}\subseteq\Bun_{G,U}
is open, and
*/\underline{G(A)}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{G,U}^{1}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Let
({\mathscr{G}},\phi)
be an object in
\Bun_{G,U}(S)
. We claim that

\displaystyle\big\{\overline{s}\in\absolutevalue{S}\mid\mbox{the image of }({\mathscr{G}},\phi)\mbox{ in }\Bun_{G,U}(\overline{s})\mbox{ is trivial}\big\}

is an open subset of
\absolutevalue{S}
. To see this, by replacing
S
with a pro-étale cover, we can assume that
S
is strictly totally disconnected. Note that the above subset lies in

\displaystyle\big\{\overline{s}\in\absolutevalue{S}\mid\mbox{the image of }({\mathscr{G}},\phi)\mbox{ in }\Bun_{G,F_{z}}(\overline{s})\mbox{ is trivial for all }z\mbox{ in }Z\big\},

which is an open subset of
\absolutevalue{S}
by
[
25
, Theorem III.2.4]
. Therefore, by replacing
S
with an open subspace, we can assume that the image of
({\mathscr{G}},\phi)
in
\Bun_{G,F_{z}}(\overline{s})
is trivial for all
z
in
Z
and geometric points
\overline{s}
of
S
. For all
V
in
\Rep_{C}{G}
, this implies that the image of
({\mathscr{G}}(V),\phi(V))
in
\Bun_{F_{z}}(S)
has slope zero for all
z
in
Z
, so Theorem
1.14
shows that
({\mathscr{G}},\phi)
corresponds to an exact tensor functor

\displaystyle\Rep_{C}{G}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\{\mbox{pro-\'{e}tale }\underline{{\mathscr{O}}_{U}}\mbox{-local systems on }S\}.

Let
\{U_{1},U_{2}\}
be the open cover of
U
from Lemma
1.12
. For all
i
in
\{1,2\}
, write
A_{i}
for the global sections of
U_{i}
, and write
A_{12}
for the global sections of
U_{1}\cap U_{2}
. Because
\Pic{U_{i}}=1
, rank-
n

\underline{{\mathscr{O}}_{U_{i}}}
-local systems are equivalent to
\underline{\GL_{n}(A_{i})}
-torsors. Hence
[
25
, Lemma III.2.6]
implies that
\underline{{\mathscr{O}}_{U_{i}}}
-local systems on
S
are trivial, so
({\mathscr{G}},\phi)
corresponds to two exact tensor functors

\displaystyle\Big\{\Rep_{C}{G}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\{\mbox{finite free }\underline{A_{i}}(S)\mbox{-modules}\}\Big\}_{i}

along with an isomorphism between them after postcomposing with

\displaystyle\{\mbox{finite free }\underline{A_{i}}(S)\mbox{-modules}\}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\{\mbox{finite free }\underline{A_{12}}(S)\mbox{-modules}\}.

Since
A_{i}
is discrete, we have
\Spec\underline{A_{i}}(S)=\Spec\Cont(\pi_{0}(\absolutevalue{S}),A_{i})=\pi_{0}(\absolutevalue{S})\times U_{i}
. Therefore Proposition
2.1
and gluing show that
({\mathscr{G}},\phi)
corresponds to an étale
G
-torsor
{\mathscr{F}}
on
\pi_{0}(\absolutevalue{S})\times U
.

For all
\overline{s}
in
\absolutevalue{S}
, note that
\absolutevalue{\ov{s}}\times U\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\textstyle\varprojlim_{N}\big(N\times U\big)
, where
N
runs over compact neighborhoods of the image of
\overline{s}
in
\pi_{0}(\absolutevalue{S})
, and the transition morphisms are affine. Therefore if
{\mathscr{F}}|_{\absolutevalue{\ov{s}}\times U}
is trivial, then
{\mathscr{F}}|_{N\times U}
is trivial for some compact neighborhood
N
of the image of
\overline{s}
in
\pi_{0}(\absolutevalue{S})
. Hence the image of
({\mathscr{G}},\phi)
in
\Bun_{G,U}(\widetilde{N})
is trivial, where
\widetilde{N}
denotes the preimage of
N
in
S
. As
\overline{s}
varies, this yields the claim.

The claim yields the first statement. For the second statement, the above shows that
*\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun^{1}_{G,U}
is a pro-étale cover, so descent yields the desired result.
∎

2.9.

Let us recall some notation on the Kottwitz set. In this subsection, we work over
\Spd\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
. Let
K
be one of
\{F,F_{v}\}
, write
B(K,G)
for the Kottwitz set as in
[
49
, Subsection 10.2]
, and write
B(K,G)_{\basic}
for its subset of basic elements as in
[
49
, Definition 10.2]
. For all
b
in
B(K,G)
, write
G_{b}
for the associated connected reductive group over
K
as in
[
49
, Subsection 2.6]
. Write
\loc_{v}:B(F,G)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}B(F_{v},G)
for the localization map as in
[
49
, (10.7)]
, and recall that
B(F,G)
is naturally isomorphic to the set of
\Frob_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}
-conjugacy classes in
G(F\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q})

[
44
, Theorem 12.2]
.

Similarly to the local setting
[
25
, Corollary III.4.3]
, in the global setting we can pass between the trivial locus and other basic loci by twisting as follows. Let
b
be an element in
G(U_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}})
whose image in
B(F,G)
is basic. Write
\Bun^{b}_{G,U}
for the substack of
\Bun_{G,U}
whose
S
-points consist of objects such that, for all geometric points
\overline{s}
of
S
, its image in
\Bun_{G,U}(\overline{s})
is isomorphic to
(G,(\Frob_{S}^{-1})^{*}\circ b)
. Twisting
G
by
\ad{b}
over
U_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}
yields a parahoric group scheme
G_{b}
over
U
, and because
b
is basic,
[
33
, (5.2)]
shows that the generic fiber of
G_{b}
agrees with the above notion.

Proposition
.

This induces a natural isomorphism
\Bun_{G,U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Bun_{G_{b},U}
such that the image of
\Bun_{G,U}^{b}
equals
\Bun_{G_{b},U}^{1}
.

Proof.

For all objects
({\mathscr{G}},\phi)
in
\Bun_{G,U}(S)
, we have a natural isomorphism

\displaystyle\underline{\Isom}({\mathscr{G}},G)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\underline{\Isom}(\Frob^{*}_{S}{\mathscr{G}},\Frob_{S}^{*}G)=\Frob_{S}^{*}\underline{\Isom}({\mathscr{G}},G),

where the left arrow denotes precomposing with
\phi^{-1}:\Frob_{S}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}{\mathscr{G}}
and postcomposing with
(\Frob_{S}^{-1})^{*}\circ b:G\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Frob^{*}_{S}G
. Since
(G_{b})_{U_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}}=G_{U_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}}
and we work over
\Spd\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
, we see
\underline{\Isom}(-,G)
induces the desired isomorphism
\Bun_{G,U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Bun_{G_{b},U}
.
∎

2.10.

For any small v-stack
X
, write
X^{\red}
its
reduction
, i.e. the perfect v-stack given by
\Spec{B}\mapsto\Hom(\Spd{B},X)
for all perfect
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebras

[
