, Proposition 3.7]
. We conclude by proving the following generalization of Theorem C.

Theorem
.

The perfect v-stack
\Bun_{G,U}^{\red}
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
is the v-sheafification of

\displaystyle\Spec{B}\mapsto\left\{\begin{tabular}[]{c}$G$-torsors ${\mathscr{G}}$ on $U_{B}$ equipped with\\
an isomorphism $\phi:{\mathscr{G}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Frob_{B}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}$\end{tabular}\right\}.

Moreover, when each connected component of
\Spec{B}
is a valuation ring, no sheafification is needed.

Proof.

By using
[
11
, Lemma 6.2]
and taking perfections, we see that
\Spec{B}
as in the second statement form a basis for the v-topology on
\{\mbox{affine perfect schemes}\}
. Hence it suffices to prove that the restriction of
\Bun^{\red}_{G,U}
to such
\Spec{B}
is naturally equivalent to (
Theorem
). Because
\Spa{B(\!(t^{1/q^{\infty}})\!)}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Spd{B}
is surjective and representable in perfectoid spaces, this follows from Proposition
2.1
and Theorem
1.15
.
∎

3.
Shtukas and the fiber product conjecture

Our goal in this section is to prove Theorem D. First, we recall the algebraic stack of global shtukas, and we prove that its associated v-stack takes a simple form. Next, we recall the v-stack of local shtukas. Finally, we define the function field analogue of the Hodge–Tate period map, and we prove Theorem D.

3.1.

We start with some more notation concerning
G
over
F
. Fix a separable closure
\overline{F}
of
F
, and write
W_{F}
for the associated absolute Weil group of
F
. Then global class field theory yields an isomorphism
F^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}W_{F}^{\ab}
that sends uniformizers to geometric Frobenii. Let
T
be a maximal subtorus of
G
over
F
, let
B\subseteq G_{\overline{F}}
be a Borel subgroup containing
T_{\overline{F}}
, and write
X_{*}^{+}(T)
for the associated set of dominant cocharacters of
T_{\overline{F}}
. Write
\pi_{1}(G)
for the cocharacter group of
T_{\overline{F}}
quotiented by the subgroup generated by the coroots.

3.2.

Let us recall the stack of global shtukas. Let
I
be a finite set, and let
\mu_{\bullet}=(\mu_{i})_{i\in I}
be in
X_{*}^{+}(T)^{I}
. For all
i
in
I
, write
C_{i}
for the normalization of
C
in the field of definition of
\mu_{i}
. For all affine schemes
\Spec{B}
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
and morphisms

\displaystyle x:\Spec{B}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}C_{i}

over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, write
\Gamma_{x}\subseteq C_{B}
for the graph of the corresponding morphism
\Spec{B}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}C
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. Let
N
be a finite closed subscheme of
C
, and write
N_{i}
for the preimage of
N
in
C_{i}
.

Definition
.

Write
\Sht_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}^{I}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}}
for the prestack on
\{\mbox{affine schemes over }{\mathbb{F}}_{q}\}
whose
\Spec{B}
-points parametrize data consisting of

i)

for all
i
in
I
, a morphism
x_{i}:\Spec{B}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
,

ii)

a
G
-torsor
{\mathscr{G}}
on
C_{B}
,

iii)

an isomorphism of
G
-torsors

\displaystyle\phi:{\mathscr{G}}|_{C_{B}\smallsetminus\sum_{i\in I}\Gamma_{x_{i}}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Frob_{B}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}|_{C_{B}\smallsetminus\sum_{i\in I}\Gamma_{x_{i}}}

whose relative position along
\{\Gamma_{x_{i}}\}_{i\in I}
is bounded by
\mu_{\bullet}
,

iv)

an isomorphism of
G
-torsors
\psi:{\mathscr{G}}|_{N_{B}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}G
such that the square

\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}|_{N_{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\phi)_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\Frob_{B}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}|_{N_{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Frob_{B}^{*})^{-1}}
\textstyle{\Frob_{B}^{*}G}

commutes.

Write
\Sht_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}^{I,\circ}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}}
for the open substack of
\Sht_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}^{I}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}}
whose
\Spec{B}
-points consist of
(\{x_{i}\}_{i\in I},{\mathscr{G}},\phi,\psi)
such that the isomorphism
\phi
has relative position along
\{\Gamma_{x_{i}}\}_{i\in I}
equal to
\mu_{\bullet}
.

Recall that
\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}}
is a Deligne–Mumford stack that is separated and locally of finite type over
\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}

[
54
, Proposition 5.6]
, and its open substack
\Sht^{I,\circ}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}}
is smooth over
\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}
.

For all finite closed subschemes
N^{\prime}\supseteq N
of
C
, write
K^{N^{\prime}}_{N}
for the kernel of
G({\mathscr{O}}_{N^{\prime}})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}G({\mathscr{O}}_{N})
. Recall that
\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N^{\prime}}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N^{\prime}_{i}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}^{\prime}}
is finite Galois with group
K^{N^{\prime}}_{N}

[
54
, Proposition 5.5]
.

3.3.

For convenience, we package the Harder–Narasimhan stratification for global shtukas into a single parameter
t
as follows. By
[
5
, Proposition 2.2(b)]
, there exists an
\SL_{h}
-bundle
{\mathscr{V}}
on
C
along with a closed embedding
\iota:G^{\ad}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\underline{\Aut}({\mathscr{V}})
of group schemes over
C
such that
\underline{\Aut}({\mathscr{V}})/G^{\ad}
satisfies
[
5
, (2.1)]
. Let
t
be a positive integer.

Definition
.

Write
\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}}
for the open substack of

\displaystyle\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}}

whose
\Spec{B}
-points consist of
(\{x_{i}\}_{i\in I},{\mathscr{G}},\phi,\psi)
such that the
\SL_{h}
-torsor
\iota_{*}({\mathscr{G}}^{\ad})
on
C_{B}
has Harder–Narasimhan polygon bounded by
t2\rho^{\vee}
, where
2\rho^{\vee}
denotes the sum of positive coroots in
\SL_{h}
.

Recall that, when
\deg{N}
is large enough,
\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}}
is a disjoint union of quasiprojective schemes over
\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}

[
54
, Proposition 5.6]
.

3.4.

We now convert the moduli of global shtukas into a v-stack.

Definition
.

Write
(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})^{\Diamond}
and
(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})^{\Diamond}
for the prestack on
\Perf_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
whose
S
-points are given by

\displaystyle(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})(R)\mbox{ and }(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})(R),

respectively.

3.5 Proposition
.

1)

When
\deg{N}
is large enough,
(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})^{\Diamond}
equals
(-)^{\Diamond}
of the adic space
\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}}
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.

2)

For any increasing family
\{N_{a}\}_{a\geq 1}
of finite closed subschemes of
C
such that
\deg{N_{a}}\to\infty
as
a\to\infty
, the morphism

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{a\geq 1}(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N_{a}}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus(N_{a})_{i}})^{\Diamond}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\varprojlim_{a\geq 1}\big(\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus(N_{a})_{i}\big)^{\Diamond}

is separated, representable in locally spatial diamonds, and locally of finite
\dimtrg
.

3)

The prestack
(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})^{\Diamond}
is a small v-stack,

\displaystyle(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})^{\Diamond}\subseteq(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})^{\Diamond}

is an open substack, and for all finite closed subschemes
N^{\prime}\supseteq N
of
C
,

\displaystyle(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N^{\prime}}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N^{\prime}_{i}})^{\Diamond}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}^{\prime}})^{\Diamond}

is finite Galois with group
K^{N^{\prime}}_{N}
.

Proof.

Part 1) follows from Lemma
1.2
and
3.3
. For part 2), it follows from part 1) that, for all positive integers
t
, the morphism

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{a\geq 1}(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N_{a}}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus(N_{a})_{i}})^{\Diamond}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\varprojlim_{a\geq 1}\big(\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus(N_{a})_{i}\big)^{\Diamond}

is separated, representable in locally spatial diamonds, and locally of finite
\dimtrg
. Recall that
\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}}
is the increasing union
\bigcup_{t\geq 1}\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}}
. Since the preimage of
\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N^{\prime}_{i}}
in
\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N^{\prime}}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N^{\prime}_{i}}
equals

\displaystyle\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N^{\prime}}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N^{\prime}_{i}}

and
(-)^{\Diamond}
preserves open embeddings, taking
\bigcup_{t\geq 1}
yields the desired result.

For part 3), let
v
be a closed point of
C\smallsetminus N^{\prime}
, and let
t
be a positive integer. For all positive integers
m
, combining the natural equivalence
(\Spec{R})_{\text{f\'{e}t}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}S_{\text{f\'{e}t}}
with
3.2
implies that
S
-points of
(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus(N+v)_{i}})^{\Diamond}
parametrize
K^{N+mv}_{N}
-torsors
S^{\prime}
on
S
equipped with a
K^{N+mv}_{N}
-equivariant morphism

\displaystyle S^{\prime}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N+mv}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus(N+v)_{i}})^{\Diamond}.

By letting
m
be large, part 1) indicates that
(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus(N+v)_{i}})^{\Diamond}
is a small v-stack, the morphism

\displaystyle(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N^{\prime}}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus(N^{\prime}+v)_{i}})^{\Diamond}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus(N^{\prime}+v)_{i}})^{\Diamond}

is finite Galois with group
K^{N^{\prime}}_{N}
, and varying
t
yields open embeddings. Hence taking
\bigcup_{t\geq 1}
shows the desired statements after restricting to
\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus(N+v)_{i}
. Finally, because
\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}
is covered by open subspaces of the form
\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus(N+v)_{i}
, taking
\bigcup_{v}
yields the desired result.
∎

3.6.

Conversely, we can recover the moduli of global shtukas (up to perfection) from its associated v-stack:

Corollary
.

1)

When
N
is large enough,
(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})^{\Diamond,\red}
is naturally isomorphic to the perfect scheme
(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})^{\perf}
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.

2)

The perfect v-stack
(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})^{\Diamond,\red}
is naturally isomorphic to

\displaystyle(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})^{\perf}.

Proof.

For part 1), Proposition
3.5
.1) shows that
(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})^{\Diamond}
equals
(-)^{\Diamond}
of the adic space
(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})^{\perf}
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.
6
6

6

We warn that this is
not
glued from
\Spa{B}
as
\Spec{B}
runs over affine open subspaces of the perfect scheme
(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})^{\perf}
. Instead, this is glued from
\Spa(B,{\mathbb{Z}}^{\sim})
.
Therefore the desired result follows from
[
31
, Theorem 2.32]
.

For part 2), let
v
be a closed point of
C
. For all perfect
{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-algebras
B
, the functor
\Spd
induces a natural equivalence
(\Spec{B})_{\text{f\'{e}t}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}(\Spd{B})_{\text{f\'{e}t}}

[
48
, Corollary 5.4]
, so Proposition
3.5
.3) implies that, for all positive integers
m
, the
\Spec{B}
-points of
(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus(N+v)_{i}})^{\Diamond,\red}
parametrizes
K^{N+mv}_{N}
-torsors
\Spec{B^{\prime}}
on
\Spec{B}
equipped with a
K^{N+mv}_{N}
-equivariant morphism

\displaystyle\Spec{B^{\prime}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N+mv}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus(N+v)_{i}})^{\Diamond,\red}.

By letting
m
be large, part 1) and
3.2
indicate that
(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus(N+v)_{i}})^{\Diamond,\red}
is naturally isomorphic to
(\Sht^{I,\leq t}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus(N+v)_{i}})^{\perf}
. Finally, taking
\bigcup_{v}\bigcup_{t\geq 1}
yields the desired result.
∎

3.7.

3.8.

In the local and global settings, shtukas induce bundles on the Hartl–Pink curve in the following way. Recall that choosing a uniformizer of
{\mathcal{O}}_{z}
identifies
\Spa{{\mathcal{O}}_{z}}\times_{{\mathbb{F}}_{z}}S
with the open unit disk over
S
. For all objects
(\{x_{i}\}_{i\in I_{z}},{\mathscr{G}},\phi)
in

\displaystyle(\LocSht^{I_{z}}_{G,\mu_{z}}|_{\prod_{i\in I_{z}}\Spd{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}})(S),

the quasicompactness of
\absolutevalue{S}
shows that there exists a closed disk
D\subseteq\Spa{{\mathcal{O}}_{z}}\times_{{\mathbb{F}}_{z}}S
over
S
centered at the origin such that
D
contains
\Gamma_{x_{i}}
for all

in
I_{z}
. Hence
\phi
restricts to an isomorphism
{\mathscr{G}}|_{\Spa{F_{z}}\times_{{\mathbb{F}}_{z}}S\smallsetminus D}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}(\Frob_{S}^{\deg{z}})^{*}{\mathscr{G}}|_{\Spa{F_{z}}\times_{{\mathbb{F}}_{z}}S\smallsetminus D}
. Via continuation by Frobenius and Proposition
2.4
, this corresponds to an object in
\Bun_{G,F_{z}}(S)
, so altogether this construction yields a morphism

\displaystyle\LocSht^{I_{z}}_{G,\mu_{z}}|_{\prod_{i\in I_{z}}\Spd{{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}.

Write
\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}
for
\prod_{z\in Z}\big(\prod_{i\in I_{z}}\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}\big)
, and write
\prod_{i\in I}^{\circ}\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}
for its open subspace
\big(\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}\big)\times_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}}\big(\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}\big)
. Form the fiber product

\textstyle{(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}^{\circ}\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}})^{\Diamond}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\prod_{i\in I}\!^{\circ}\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}})^{\Diamond}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\big(\displaystyle\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}\big)^{\Diamond}.}

By arguing as above for all
z
in
Z
, we also obtain a morphism

\displaystyle(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}^{\circ}\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}})^{\Diamond}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{G,U}.

3.9.

We now describe the analogue of the Hodge–Tate period map for global shtukas. Write
n_{v}
for the multiplicity of
v
in
N
, and write
K_{v}^{n_{v}}
for the kernel of
G({\mathcal{O}}_{v})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}G({\mathscr{O}}_{n_{v}v})
. Write
\LocSht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod^{\circ}_{i\in I}\Spd{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}}
for the product

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in Z\\
n_{z}=0\end{subarray}}\LocSht^{I_{z}}_{G,\mu_{z}}|_{\prod_{i\in I_{z}}\Spd{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}}\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in Z\\
n_{z}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\LocSht^{I_{z}}_{G,\mu_{z},n_{z}}|_{\prod_{i\in I_{z}}\Spd{F_{z_{i}}}},

and write
K_{N\cap U}
for the kernel of
G({\mathbb{O}}_{U})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}G({\mathscr{O}}_{N\cap U})
.

Let
(\{x_{i}\}_{i\in I},{\mathscr{G}},\phi,\psi)
be an object in
(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}^{\circ}\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}})^{\Diamond}(S)
. For all
z
in
Z
, arguing as in the proof of
[
54
, Lemma 5.12]
shows that

\displaystyle(\{x_{i}\}_{i\in I_{z}},{\mathscr{G}}|_{\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z}},\phi|_{\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z}})

corresponds to an object in
(\LocSht^{I_{z}}_{G,\mu_{z}}|_{\prod_{i\in I_{z}}\Spd{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}})(S)
. If
n_{z}
is positive, then
(\psi)_{n_{z}z}
corresponds to a lift of this object to
(\LocSht^{I_{z}}_{G,\mu_{z},n_{z}}|_{\prod_{i\in I_{z}}\Spd{F_{z_{i}}}})(S)
, so altogether we obtain a morphism

\displaystyle(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}^{\circ}\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}})^{\Diamond}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\LocSht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod^{\circ}_{i\in I}\Spd{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}}

over
\prod^{\circ}_{i\in I}\Spd{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}
. For all closed points

of

, Proposition
2.4
indicates that

\displaystyle({\mathscr{G}}|_{\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{u}},\phi|_{\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{u}})

corresponds to an object in

. If

lies in

, then
(\psi)_{n_{u}u}
corresponds to a lift of this object to

, so altogether we also obtain a morphism

\displaystyle(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}^{\circ}\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}})^{\Diamond}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}*/\underline{K_{N\cap U}}.

3.10.

Finally, we arrive at Theorem D.

Theorem
.

The square of small v-stacks

\textstyle{(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}^{\circ}\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}})^{\Diamond}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast/\underline{K_{N\cap U}}\times\LocSht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod^{\circ}_{i\in I}\Spd{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G,U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\loc}
\textstyle{\ast/\underline{G({\mathbb{O}}_{U})}\times\displaystyle\prod_{z\in Z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}}

is cartesian.

Proof.

For all
z
be in
Z
and closed disks
D_{z}\subseteq\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z}\times_{{\mathbb{F}}_{z}}S
over
S
centered at the origin, write
D_{z}^{\cup}
for the closed subspace

\displaystyle\coprod_{r\in{\mathbb{Z}}/\!\deg{z}}\Frob_{S}^{-r}(D_{z})\subseteq\coprod_{r\in{\mathbb{Z}}/\!\deg{z}}\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z}\times_{{\mathbb{F}}_{z},r}S\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z}\times S,

where
\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z}\times_{{\mathbb{F}}_{z},r}S
denotes the product of
\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z}
and

\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Spa{\mathbb{F}}_{z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Frob_{{\mathbb{F}}_{z}}^{r}}
\textstyle{\Spa{\mathbb{F}}_{z}}

over
{\mathbb{F}}_{z}
, and the isomorphism is from
[
54
, Lemma 5.11]
. Then

\displaystyle\Big\{U^{\an}_{S}\smallsetminus\bigcup_{z\in Z}D_{z}^{\cup}\Big\}\cup\{\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z}\times S\}_{z\in Z}

is an open cover of
C^{\an}_{S}
, so we can glue an object in
\Bun_{G,U}(S)
to an object in
(\ast/\underline{K_{N\cap U}}\times\LocSht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod^{\circ}_{i\in I}\Spd{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}})(S)
to obtain

i)

an
S
-point
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
\prod_{i\in I}^{\circ}\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}
,

ii)

a
G
-torsor
{\mathscr{G}}^{\an}
on
C^{\an}_{S}
,

iii)

an isomorphism of
G
-torsors

\displaystyle\phi^{\an}:{\mathscr{G}}|_{C^{\an}_{S}\smallsetminus\sum_{i\in I}\Gamma_{x_{i}}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Frob_{S}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}^{\an}|_{C^{\an}_{S}\smallsetminus\sum_{i\in I}\Gamma_{x_{i}}}

whose relative position along
\{\Gamma_{x_{i}}\}_{i\in I}
is bounded by
\mu_{\bullet}
,

iv)

an isomorphism of
G
-torsors
\psi^{\an}:{\mathscr{G}}^{\an}|_{N_{S}^{\an}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}G
such that the square

\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}^{\an}|_{N^{\an}_{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\phi^{\an})_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{\an}}
\textstyle{\Frob_{S}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}^{\an}|_{N^{\an}_{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{\an}}
\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Frob_{S}^{-1})^{*}}
\textstyle{\Frob_{S}^{*}G}

commutes.

Finally, Proposition
2.1
and Theorem
A.19
show that the above corresponds to an object in
(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}^{\circ}\Spa{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}})^{\Diamond}(S)
.
∎

4.
Geometric consequences

In this section, we harvest some of the fruits of Theorem D. We start by deducing an algebraic analogue of Theorem D via taking reductions, and we explain how this implies the Langlands–Rapoport conjecture for global shtukas. Next, we use the charts provided by Theorem D and Beauville–Laszlo uniformization to prove Theorem A and Theorem B. Finally, we conclude by explaining the relation with Igusa varieties.

4.1.

For all
z
in
Z
, write
\prod_{i\in I_{z}}z_{i}
for the product of the
z_{i}
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{z}
. Let us recall the following algebraic analogues of
\Bun_{G,F_{z}}
and
\LocSht^{I_{z}}_{G,\mu_{z}}|_{\prod_{i\in I_{z}}\Spd{\mathcal{O}}_{z_{i}}}
.

Definition
.

a)

Write
{\mathscr{I}}\!\mathrm{soc}_{G,F_{z}}
for the prestack on
\{\mbox{affine perfect schemes over }{\mathbb{F}}_{q}\}
whose
\Spec{B}
-points parametrize data consisting of
G
-torsors
{\mathscr{G}}
on
\Spec F_{z}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}B
equipped with an isomorphism
\phi:{\mathscr{G}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Frob_{B}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}
.

b)

Write
{\mathscr{L}}\!\mathrm{ocSht}^{I_{z}}_{G,\mu_{z}}|_{\prod_{i\in I_{z}}z_{i}}
for the prestack on
\{\mbox{affine perfect schemes over }{\mathbb{F}}_{z}\}
whose
\Spec{B}
-points parametrize data consisting of

i)

for all
i
in
I_{z}
, a morphism

over
{\mathbb{F}}_{z}
,

ii)

a
G
-torsor
{\mathscr{G}}
on
\Spec{{\mathcal{O}}_{z}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{z}}B}
,

iii)

an isomorphism of
G
-torsors
\phi:{\mathscr{G}}|_{\Spec{F_{z}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{z}}B}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}(\Frob_{B}^{\deg{z}})^{*}{\mathscr{G}}|_{\Spec{F_{z}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{z}}B}}
whose relative position along

is bounded by
\mu_{z}
,

Note that

and

imply that

and
{\mathscr{L}}\!\mathrm{ocSht}^{I_{z}}_{G,\mu_{z}}|_{\prod_{i\in I_{z}}z_{i}}
are perfect v-stacks.

4.2.

Write
\prod_{i\in I}z_{i}
for
\prod_{z\in Z}\big(\prod_{i\in I_{z}}z_{i}\big)
. By taking reductions, we recover the following algebraic analogue of Theorem
3.10
:

Theorem
.

Assume that
N
and
Z
are disjoint. Then there is a natural cartesian square of perfect v-stacks

\textstyle{(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}z_{i}})^{\perf}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast/\underline{K_{N}}\times\displaystyle\prod_{z\in Z}{\mathscr{L}}\!\mathrm{ocSht}^{I_{z}}_{G,\mu_{z}}|_{\prod_{i\in I_{z}}z_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G,U}^{\red}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast/\underline{G({\mathbb{O}}_{U})}\times\displaystyle\prod_{z\in Z}{\mathscr{I}}\!\mathrm{soc}_{G,F_{z}}.}

Proof.

Because
(-)^{\red}
preserves limits, base changing the top arrow of Theorem
3.10
to
\prod_{i\in I}z_{i}
and taking
(-)^{\red}
yields a cartesian square of perfect v-stacks. The classifying stacks are preserved by
[
48
, Corollary 5.4]
, the top left is identified by Corollary
3.6
.2), the top right is identified by
[
32
, Theorem 7.14.(2)]
, and the bottom right is identified by
[
32
, Theorem 7.14.(1)]
.
∎

4.3 Remarks
.

1)

By evaluating Theorem
4.2
on
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
and using the description of
\Bun_{G,U}^{\red}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q})
from Theorem
2.10
, we immediately deduce the Langlands–Rapoport conjecture for moduli spaces of global shtukas with arbitary (in particular, colliding) legs.

2)

If one takes the v-sheafification of (
Theorem
) as the definition of
\Bun_{G,U}^{\red}
, one can directly prove Theorem
4.2
using Beauville–Laszlo gluing. However, without using Theorem
2.10
, it is unclear why no sheafification is needed when evaluating
\Bun^{\red}_{G,U}
on
\Spec{B}
for which each connected component is a valuation ring.

4.4.

Next, we recall some facts about affine Grassmannians.
For the rest of this paper, we work over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
.
For all
z
in
Z
, write
\Gr_{G}
for the
B_{\dR}
-affine Grassmannian over
\Spd{\breve{F}_{z}}
as in
[
67
, Definition 20.2.1]
.
8
8

8

While
[
67
]
works over
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, the definition of the
B_{\dR}
-affine Grassmannian and its basic properties hold over any nonarchimedean local field. Indeed, this is implicitly used in
[
25
]
.
When
I_{z}=\{i\}
is a singleton, recall from
[
67
, p. 184]
the closed affine Schubert variety
\Gr_{G,\mu_{z}}|_{\Spd{\breve{F}_{z_{i}}}}
in
\Gr_{G}|_{\Spd{\breve{F}_{z_{i}}}}
and the open affine Schubert variety
\Gr_{G,\mu_{z}}^{\circ}|_{\Spd{\breve{F}_{z_{i}}}}
in
\Gr_{G,\mu_{z}}|_{\Spd{\breve{F}_{z_{i}}}}
. The proof of
[

, Proposition 23.3.1]
shows that

is naturally isomorphic to

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}\LocSht^{I_{z}}_{G,\mu_{z},m}|_{\Spd{\breve{F}_{z_{i}}}}.

The proof of
[
25
, Proposition VI.2.3]
implies that

\displaystyle\Gr_{G}|_{\Spa{\mathbb{C}}_{z}}=\bigcup_{\mu_{z}\in X_{*}^{+}(T)}\Gr_{G,\mu_{z}}|_{\Spa{\mathbb{C}}_{z}},

[
25
, Proposition III.3.1]
indicates that the natural morphism
\Gr_{G}|_{\Spa{\breve{F}_{z}}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}
is surjective in the pro-étale topology, and the proof of
[
25
, Theorem IV.1.19]
shows

\displaystyle\coprod_{\mu_{z}\in X_{*}^{+}(T)}\big(\Gr_{G,\mu_{z}}^{\circ}|_{\Spd\breve{F}_{z_{i}}}\big)\big/\underline{G(F_{z})}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}\times\Spd\breve{F}_{z}

is a v-cover that is separated, representable in locally spatial diamonds, and cohomologically smooth.

4.5.

Write
\loc_{Z}:\Bun_{G,U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\textstyle\prod_{z\in Z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}
for the morphism
(\loc_{z})_{z\in Z}
, which plays an important role in §

and §

. It enjoys the following finitude properties.

Proposition
.

When
Z
is nonempty,
\loc_{Z}:\Bun_{G,U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\textstyle\prod_{z\in Z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}
is compactifiable, representable in locally spatial diamonds, and locally of finite
\dimtrg
. Consequently,
\Bun_{G,U}
is an Artin v-stack.

When

is empty, Example
2.3
shows that

is
not
representable in locally spatial diamonds but that
\Bun_{G,C}
remains an Artin v-stack.

Proof.

Let

be a positive integer. In Theorem

, take

to be a singleton for all

in

, and take

to be

. Then taking

yields a cartesian square

\textstyle{\displaystyle\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},mZ}|_{\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\breve{F}_{z_{i}}}})^{\Diamond}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}\LocSht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},mZ}|_{\prod_{i\in I}\Spd{\breve{F}_{z_{i}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G,U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\loc_{Z}}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\prod_{z\in Z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}.}

Proposition
3.5
.2) indicates that
\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},mZ}|_{\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\breve{F}_{z_{i}}}})^{\Diamond}
is a locally spatial diamond that is separated and locally of finite
\dimtrg
over

. By

and

is also a locally spatial diamond that is separated and locally of finite
\dimtrg
over

, so the top arrow is separated, representable in locally spatial diamonds by

\dimtrg
.

Next,

Finally,

[

.
∎

4.6.

At this point, we can finish the proof of Theorem A. For any finite set
Q
of closed points of
C
, write
{\mathcal{O}}_{Q}
for

, and write

. Write

in

for the sum of all positive roots, and write

Proposition
.

The Artin v-stack
\Bun_{G,U}
is cohomologically smooth over
\Spd\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
, and its dualizing complex over

When

. When

is nonempty, take

to be a singleton for all

in

. Then

\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\Big[\displaystyle\prod_{z\in Z}\Gr^{\circ}_{G,\mu_{z}}|_{\Spd\breve{F}_{z_{i}}}\Big]\Big/\underline{G(F_{Z})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\Bun_{G,U}\times\displaystyle\prod_{z\in Z}\Spd\breve{F}_{z_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\loc_{Z}}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\prod_{z\in Z}\big(\Bun_{G,F_{z}}\times\Spd\breve{F}_{z_{i}}\big).}

Now

indicates that

is separated, representable in locally spatial diamonds, and cohomologically smooth, and the proof of

, Theorem IV.1.19]
indicates that

is isomorphic to

. Hence the same holds for

.

Because

, Proposition

implies

(
\circ
)

\displaystyle\Big[\displaystyle\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}(\Sht^{I,\circ}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},mZ}|_{\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\breve{F}_{z_{i}}}})^{\Diamond}\Big]\Big/\underline{G(F_{Z})}

admits a separated, representable in locally spatial diamonds, and cohomologically smooth v-cover from a locally spatial diamond

4.6
) over

.

Next,

4.7.

We will use the following analogue of (perfect) Igusa varieties for shtukas. For all

4.8 Proposition
.

When

♡

‣

Proof.

\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}|_{\Spec F_{z}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{z}}B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\phi^{\deg{z}})_{F_{z}}}
\scriptstyle{(\psi_{z})_{F_{z}}}
\textstyle{(\Frob_{B}^{\deg{z}})^{*}{\mathscr{G}}|_{\Spec F_{z}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{F}}_{z}}B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\psi_{z})_{F_{z}}}
\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Frob_{B}^{*})^{-\deg{z}}\circ b_{z}}
\textstyle{G}

commutes.

Next, write
{\mathcal{B}}_{G,\infty Z}
for the prestack on

4.9.

Similarly to Definition

4.10.

As suggested by Theorem

,

\textstyle{{\mathscr{G}}|_{\Spa F_{z}\times_{{\mathbb{F}}_{z}}S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\phi^{\deg{z}})_{F_{z}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{z}^{\an}}
\textstyle{(\Frob_{S}^{\deg{z}})^{*}{\mathscr{G}}|_{\Spa F_{z}\times_{{\mathbb{F}}_{z}}S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{z}^{\an}}
\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Frob_{S}^{*})^{-\deg{z}}}
\textstyle{G.}

Note that everything is independent of
R^{+}
, so we can assume that
R^{+}
equals
R^{\circ}
. Because products of points as in
[
31
, Definition 1.2]
form a basis for the v-topology
[
31
, Example 1.1]
, we can assume that
S
is a product of points. Then
[
32
, Corollary 1.9]
implies that the above corresponds to an object in
(\Ig^{b_{\bullet}}_{G,\mu_{\bullet}})^{\Diamond}(S)
.
∎

4.11.

Let us recall some more notation concerning the Kottwitz set. Let
K
be one of
\{F,F_{v}\}
, write
{\mathbb{X}}_{K}
for the
W_{K}
-module given by

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{K}\coloneqq\begin{cases}{\mathbb{Z}}&\mbox{if }K=F_{v},\\
\Div(\overline{F})_{0}\mbox{ as in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{HK21}{}{}, p.~75]}}&\mbox{if }K=F,\end{cases}

and write
\kappa:B(K,G)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(\pi_{1}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{X}}_{K})_{W_{K}}
for the Kottwitz map as in
[
49
, Subsection 11.5]
. Recall that
\kappa
restricts to a bijection
B(K,G)_{\basic}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}(\pi_{1}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{X}}_{K})_{W_{K}}

[
49
, Proposition 13.1, Proposition 15.1]
.

Recall the global semistable locus from Definition
2.7
, which was defined
in terms of
its local analogue. We now prove that it admits the following
intrinsic
description; this is Theorem B. For all
b
in
B(F,G)_{\basic}
, write
\Bun^{b}_{G,F}
for the substack of
\Bun_{G,F}
whose
S
-points consist of objects such that, for all geometric points
\overline{s}
of
S
, its image in
\Bun_{G,F}(\overline{s})
is isomorphic to
(G,(\Frob^{*}_{S})^{-1}\circ b)
.

Theorem
.

The substack
\Bun^{b}_{G,F}\subseteq\Bun_{G,F}
is open and isomorphic to
*/\underline{G_{b}(F)}
. Moreover, the open substack
\Bun^{\semis}_{G,F}\subseteq\Bun_{G,F}
equals
\coprod_{b\in B(F,G)_{\basic}}\Bun^{b}_{G,F}
.

Proof.

Theorem
2.8
implies that
\Bun^{1}_{G,F}\subseteq\Bun_{G,F}
is open and naturally isomorphic to
*/\underline{G(F)}
. Proposition
2.9
yields an isomorphism
\Bun_{G,F}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Bun_{G_{b},F}
of v-stacks such that the image of
\Bun^{b}_{G,F}
equals
\Bun^{1}_{G_{b},F}
, so applying the above to
G_{b}
shows that
\Bun^{b}_{G,F}\subseteq\Bun_{G,F}
is open and isomorphic to
*/\underline{G_{b}(F)}
. Theorem
2.10
identifies the isomorphism classes in
\Hom_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}(\Spd\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q},\Bun_{G,F})
with
B(F,G)
, which indicates that the
\Bun^{b}_{G,F}
are disjoint for distinct
b
. Hence
\coprod_{b\in B(F,G)_{\basic}}\Bun^{b}_{G,F}
is an open substack of
\Bun_{G,F}
, and it evidently lies in
\Bun^{\semis}_{G,F}
.

For the reverse inclusion, let
\overline{s}=\Spa(K,K^{+})
be a geometric point of
\Bun^{\semis}_{G,U}
, where
U
is some dense open subscheme of
C
. Then
[
25
, Theorem III.4.5]
implies that, for all
z
in
Z
, the image of
\overline{s}
in
\Bun_{G,F_{z}}
lies in
\Bun^{b_{z}}_{G,F_{z}}
for some
b_{z}
in
B(F_{z},G)_{\basic}
. Take
I_{z}=\{i\}
to be a singleton, and take
\mu_{z}
to be a lift of
\kappa(b_{z})
to
X_{*}^{+}(T)
. Then
4.4
and the proof of
[
60
, Proposition A.9]
9
9

9

While
[
60
, Appendix A]
works over
p
-adic local fields and assumes that the cocharacter is minuscule, this is not used in the proof of
[
60
, Proposition A.9]
.
indicate that the image of
\overline{s}
in
\Bun_{G,F_{z}}
lifts to a
\Spa(K,K^{+})
-point of
\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}\LocSht^{I_{z}}_{G,\mu_{z},mz}|_{\Spd{F_{z_{i}}}}
. As
z
varies, the cartesian square from the proof of Proposition
4.5
shows that
\overline{s}
lifts to a
\Spa(K,K^{+})
-point of
\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}(\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},mZ}|_{\prod_{i\in I}\Spa F_{z_{i}}})^{\Diamond}
, which yields a
\Spec{K}
-point
({\mathscr{G}},\phi)
of
\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},\varnothing}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}}
.

Recall that the algebraic stack
{\mathcal{B}}_{G}
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
of
G
-bundles on
C
satisfies
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{G})=\pi_{1}(G)_{W_{F}}
. Since absolute
q
-Frobenius induces the identity on topological spaces,
{\mathscr{G}}
and
\Frob_{K}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}
have the same image in
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{G})
. Now
\phi
exhibits
\Frob^{*}_{K}{\mathscr{G}}
as a modification of
{\mathscr{G}}
of relative position
\sum_{z\in Z}\mu_{z}
, so the image of
\Frob_{K}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}
in
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{G})
equals the image of
{\mathscr{G}}
in
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{B}}_{G})
plus the image of
\sum_{z\in Z}\mu_{z}
in
\pi_{1}(G)_{W_{F}}
. Therefore the image of
\sum_{z\in Z}\mu_{z}
in
\pi_{1}(G)_{W_{F}}
is trivial. By
[
49
, Proposition 15.6]
, this shows that there exists a
b
in
B(F,G)_{\basic}
such that
\loc_{z}(b)=b_{z}
for all
z
in
Z
and
\loc_{u}(b)=1
for all closed points
u
of
U
.

Let
U^{\prime}
be a dense open subscheme of
U
such that
b
has a representative in
G(U^{\prime}_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}})
, and fix such a representative. By setting
b_{c}=1
for all
c
in
U\smallsetminus U^{\prime}
, we may replace
U
with
U^{\prime}
. Then Proposition
2.9
yields an isomorphism
\Bun_{G,U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Bun_{G_{b},U}
such that, for all
z
in
Z
, the image of
\overline{s}
in
\Bun_{G_{b},F_{z}}
lies in
\Bun^{1}_{G_{b},F_{z}}
. After using fpqc descent to extend
G_{b}
to a parahoric group scheme over
C
, Proposition
2.4
implies that
\Bun_{G_{b},{\mathcal{O}}_{z}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun^{1}_{G_{b},F_{z}}
is surjective in the pro-étale topology for all

in
Z
. Hence the image of
\overline{s}
in
\Bun_{G_{b},F_{z}}
lifts to a
\Spa(K,K^{+})
-point of
\Bun_{G_{b},{\mathcal{O}}_{z}}
, and as

varies, Lemma
2.6
shows that
\overline{s}
lifts to a
\Spa(K,K^{+})
-point
({\mathscr{G}}_{b},\phi_{b})
of
\Bun_{G_{b},C}
.

Proposition
2.1
and Theorem
A.19
indicate that
({\mathscr{G}}_{b},\phi_{b})
is the analytification of a
G_{b}
-torsor
{\mathscr{G}}^{\alg}_{b}
on
C_{K}
equipped with an isomorphism
\phi^{\alg}_{b}:{\mathscr{G}}^{\alg}_{b}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Frob_{K}^{*}{\mathscr{G}}^{\alg}_{b}
. Applying
[

, Lemma 3.3 b)]
to

shows that
({\mathscr{G}}^{\alg}_{b},\phi^{\alg}_{b})
is isomorphic to

\displaystyle(\overline{{\mathscr{G}}}^{\alg}_{b},\id_{\overline{{\mathscr{G}}}^{\alg}_{b}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}(\Frob_{K}^{-1})^{*})

for some
G_{b}
-torsor
\overline{{\mathscr{G}}}^{\alg}_{b}
on

. Write
b^{\prime}
for the image of
(\overline{{\mathscr{G}}}^{\alg}_{b})_{F}
under

\displaystyle H^{1}(F,G_{b})\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}B(F,G_{b})_{\basic}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}B(F,G)_{\basic},

where the left arrow denotes the injection from
[
49
, (10.5)]
, and the right arrow denotes the bijection from
[
33
, Lemma 5.3]
. After shrinking

such that

has a representative in

, we see that
\overline{s}
lies in
\Bun_{G,U}^{b^{\prime}}
, as desired.
∎

5.
Sheaf-theoretic consequences

Our goal in this section is to prove Theorem E, which heavily relies on material from Appendix
B
. We start by briefly recalling the theory of motivic sheaves and explaining why we need it. Next, we explain a general mechanism for constructing (derived) Hecke actions, which we use in both §
5
and §
6
. We then state a forthcoming result of Eteve–Gaitsgory–Genestier–Lafforgue, which is necessary to even formulate Theorem E.

At this point, we can prove Theorem E. As an application, we explain how Theorem E can be used to recover all of the representation-theoretic results of
[
54
]
; for this, it suffices to use work of Xue instead of the forthcoming result of Eteve–Gaitsgory–Genestier–Lafforgue.

5.1.

We begin by recalling the sheaf theories that we will use. Let
L/{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
be a finite extension containing a fixed
\sqrt{q}
. Write
{\mathcal{O}}_{L}
for the ring of integers of
L
, and write
{\mathbb{F}}_{\lambda}
for the residue field of
{\mathcal{O}}_{L}
. Let
\Lambda
be one of
\{L,{\mathcal{O}}_{L},{\mathbb{F}}_{\lambda}\}
.

Recall from
B.3
the
{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}]
-linear
6
-functor formalism
D_{\mot}(-)
on

\displaystyle\{\mbox{small v-stacks over }\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}\},

and recall from
B.4
the
\Lambda
-linear
6
-functor formalism

\displaystyle D(-,\Lambda)\coloneqq D_{\mot}(-)\otimes_{D_{\mot}(*)}D(\Lambda).

We use (this base change of) the theory of motivic sheaves because it enjoys
!
-pushforwards and encodes “non-completed” sheaves even when
\Lambda\neq{\mathbb{F}}_{\lambda}
(e.g. see Corollary
B.4
). However, to facilitate comparisons with classical étale
\ell
-adic sheaf theories for algebraic varieties, we will also use the various
3
-functor formalisms from Definition
B.5
, Proposition
B.7
, and
B.8
.

If one is only interested in the
\Lambda={\mathbb{F}}_{\lambda}
case, one can instead take

\displaystyle D(-,{\mathbb{F}}_{\lambda})\coloneqq D_{\et}(-,{\mathbb{F}}_{\lambda})

and just use the étale sheaf theory of
[
63
]
. In fact, since all the sheaves that we use are overconvergent as in
[
25
, Proposition IV.2.4]
, the theory of motivic sheaves specializes to this when
\Lambda={\mathbb{F}}_{\lambda}
; see
B.3
.

5.2.

We will use the following mechanism for constructing actions of (derived) endomorphism algebras on both the automorphic and spectral sides. Let
({\mathcal{C}},E)
be a geometric setup as in
[
41
, Definition 2.1.1]
, and let
D(-)
be a
\Lambda
-linear
3
-functor formalism on
({\mathcal{C}},E)
. Consider a diagram in
{\mathcal{C}}

\textstyle{Y_{O}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{O}}
\scriptstyle{j_{Y}}
\textstyle{O\times X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{j_{X}}
\textstyle{O\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{Y_{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{F}}
\textstyle{F\times X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{p_{2}}
\textstyle{F}
\textstyle{X,}

where the squares are cartesian, and the morphisms are all
!
-able. Write
f
for the morphism
Y_{O}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}X
, and let
A
be an object in
D(Y_{F})
.

Lemma
.

The object
f_{!}j_{Y}^{*}A
in
D(X)
is naturally a module for the
{\mathbb{E}}_{1}
-algebra
\End_{D(F)}(j_{!}\mathbf{1})
over
\Lambda
.

Proof.

Since
f=p_{2}\circ g_{F}\circ j_{Y}
, the projection formula and proper base change yield

\displaystyle f_{!}j_{Y}^{*}A

\displaystyle=p_{2,!}g_{F,!}j_{Y,!}j_{Y}^{*}A=p_{2,!}g_{F,!}(A\otimes j_{Y,!}\mathbf{1})=p_{2,!}g_{F,!}(A\otimes j_{Y,!}g_{O}^{*}p_{1}^{*}\mathbf{1})

\displaystyle=p_{2,!}g_{F,!}(A\otimes g_{F}^{*}p_{1}^{*}j_{!}\mathbf{1})=p_{2,!}(p_{1}^{*}j_{!}\mathbf{1}\otimes g_{F,!}A).

Because
j_{!}\mathbf{1}
is naturally a module for the
{\mathbb{E}}_{1}
-algebra
\End_{D(F)}(j_{!}\mathbf{1})
over
\Lambda
, the same holds for
f_{!}j_{Y}^{*}A
by applying the functor
p_{2,!}(p_{1}^{*}-\otimes g_{F,!}A)
.
∎

5.3.

For places in
U
, we have the following (derived) Hecke algebra action on
\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda
. Let
Q
be a finite nonempty set of closed points of
U
, and write

\displaystyle\loc_{Q}:\Bun_{G,U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}*/\underline{G({\mathcal{O}}_{Q})}

for the morphism
(\loc_{u})_{u\in Q}
. Then Lemma
2.6
implies that the square

\textstyle{\Bun_{G,U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\loc_{Q},\loc_{Z})}
\textstyle{\ast/\underline{G({\mathcal{O}}_{Q})}\times\displaystyle\prod_{z\in Z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast/\underline{G(F_{Q})}\times_{\prod_{u\in Q}\Bun_{G,F_{u}}}\Bun_{G,U\smallsetminus Q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast/\underline{G(F_{Q})}\times\displaystyle\prod_{z\in Z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}}

is cartesian. Proposition
4.5
implies that the bottom arrow is
!
-able, so the same holds for the top arrow. Therefore Lemma
5.2
and Corollary
B.4
endow
\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda
in
D(\prod_{z\in Z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}},\Lambda)
with the structure of a module for the
{\mathbb{E}}_{1}
-algebra

\displaystyle\End_{G(F_{Q})}(\cInd_{G({\mathcal{O}}_{Q})}^{G(F_{Q})}\Lambda)=\bigotimes_{u\in Q}\End_{G(F_{u})}(\cInd^{G(F_{u})}_{G({\mathcal{O}}_{u})}\Lambda)

over
\Lambda
, which we remind the reader can have cohomology in nonzero degrees when
\Lambda\neq L
. Taking the colimit over
Q
shows that
\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda
is naturally a module for the
{\mathbb{E}}_{1}
-algebra
\bigotimes_{u}\End_{G(F_{u})}(\cInd^{G(F_{u})}_{G({\mathcal{O}}_{u})}\Lambda)
over
\Lambda
, where
u
runs over closed points of
U
.

5.4.

Let us recall some notation on Langlands dual groups.
For the rest of this paper, assume that
G
is reductive over
U
.
Write
W_{U}
for the absolute Weil group of
U
with respect to
\overline{F}
, write
\widetilde{F}/F
for the finite Galois extension such that
\Gal(\widetilde{F}/F)
equals the image of the
W_{U}
-action on
X_{*}^{+}(T)
, and write
\widetilde{C}
for the normalization of
C
in
\widetilde{F}
. Write
\widetilde{U}
for the preimage of
U
in
\widetilde{C}
, and write
\widetilde{Z}
for the closed complement
\widetilde{C}\smallsetminus\widetilde{U}
. Write
\widehat{G}
for the dual group of
G
over
\Lambda
, and write
\prescript{L}{}{G}
for
\widehat{G}\rtimes\underline{\Gal(\widetilde{F}/F)}
. Similarly, for all closed points
v
of
C
, write
\widetilde{F}_{v}/F_{v}
for the finite Galois extension such that
\Gal(\widetilde{F}_{v}/F_{v})
equals the image of the
W_{F_{v}}
-action on
X_{*}^{+}(T)
, and write
\prescript{L}{}{G}_{v}
for
\widehat{G}\rtimes\underline{\Gal(\widetilde{F}_{v}/F_{v})}
.

Using
\prescript{L}{}{G}
, we re-index stacks of global shtukas as follows. Recall from
3.2
the Deligne–Mumford stack
\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{\prod_{i\in I}C_{i}\smallsetminus N_{i}}
. Let
\dot{V}
be an object in
\Rep_{\Lambda}(\prescript{L}{}{G})^{I}
, write
\widetilde{N}
for the preimage of
N
in
\widetilde{C}
, and note that the disjoint union

\displaystyle\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu_{\bullet}\in X_{*}^{+}(T)^{I}\\
\text{arising\,in\,}\dot{V}|_{\widehat{T}^{I}}\end{subarray}}\Sht^{I}_{G,\mu_{\bullet},N}|_{(\widetilde{C}\smallsetminus\widetilde{N})^{I}}

naturally descends to a Deligne–Mumford stack
\Sht^{I}_{G,\dot{V},N}|_{(C\smallsetminus N)^{I}}
that is separated and locally of finite type over
(C\smallsetminus N)^{I}
. Write
\pi_{N}:\Sht^{I}_{G,\dot{V},N}|_{(C\smallsetminus N)^{I}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(C\smallsetminus N)^{I}
for the structure morphism.

5.5.

Briefly, write
\Sht
for
\Sht^{I}_{G,\dot{V},N}|_{(C\smallsetminus N)^{I}_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}}
. Using
3.2
, Proposition
3.5
, and finite étale descent, we can extend the
\Lambda
-linear
3
-functor formalisms from Proposition

and
B.8
to the mildly stacky setting of
\pi_{N}:\Sht\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(C\smallsetminus N)^{I}_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}
. More precisely, we have a natural diagram

\textstyle{\Ind D_{\mot}^{(C\smallsetminus N)^{I}_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}\backslash\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\mathbb{C}}_{z_{i}}}\big((\Sht|_{\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\mathbb{C}}_{z_{i}}})^{\Diamond}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Upsilon}
\scriptstyle{r_{\ell,(\Sht|_{\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\mathbb{C}}_{z_{i}}})^{\Diamond}}}
\textstyle{D_{\mot}\big((\Sht|_{\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\mathbb{C}}_{z_{i}}})^{\Diamond}\big)}
\textstyle{\Ind D_{\et}^{(C\smallsetminus N)^{I}_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}\backslash\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\mathbb{C}}_{z_{i}}}\big((\Sht|_{\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\mathbb{C}}_{z_{i}}})^{\Diamond},\Lambda\big)}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces D(\Sht,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})}
\scriptstyle{\rho}

such that
!
-pushforward along
\pi_{N}
yields a morphism of diagrams to

\textstyle{\Ind D_{\mot}^{(C\smallsetminus N)^{I}_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}\backslash\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\mathbb{C}}_{z_{i}}}\big(\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\mathbb{C}}_{z_{i}}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Upsilon}
\scriptstyle{r_{\ell,\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\mathbb{C}}_{z_{i}}}}
\textstyle{D_{\mot}\big(\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\mathbb{C}}_{z_{i}}\big)}
\textstyle{\Ind D_{\et}^{(C\smallsetminus N)^{I}_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}\backslash\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\mathbb{C}}_{z_{i}}}\big(\prod_{i\in I}\Spa{\mathbb{C}}_{z_{i}},\Lambda\big)}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces D((C\smallsetminus N)^{I},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}).}
\scriptstyle{\rho}

5.6.

We now state a forthcoming result of Eteve–Gaitsgory–Genestier–Lafforgue. Roughly, it asserts that the cohomology of stacks of global shtukas satisfies the conclusion of Drinfeld’s lemma even on the level of derived categories. On the level of cohomology groups, this is already known by work of Xue; see Remark
5.7
below.

For any separated finite type scheme
X
over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
, write
D_{\lis}(X,\Lambda)\subseteq D_{\cons}(X,\Lambda)
for the full subcategory of lisse objects. Since
\Frob_{U\smallsetminus N}
is a universal homeomorphism, the functor

\displaystyle(\Frob_{U\smallsetminus N})_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}^{*}:D_{\cons}((U\smallsetminus N)_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}},\Lambda)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D_{\cons}((U\smallsetminus N)_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}},\Lambda)

restricts to an equivalence
(\Frob_{U\smallsetminus N})_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}^{*}:D_{\lis}((U\smallsetminus N)_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}},\Lambda)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}D_{\lis}((U\smallsetminus N)_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}},\Lambda)
.

Write
{\mathcal{S}}_{\dot{V}}
for the object in
D_{\cons}(\Sht^{I}_{G,\dot{V},N}|_{(U\smallsetminus N)^{I}_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}},\Lambda)
associated with
\dot{V}
via geometric Satake. Unless otherwise specified, all Lurie tensor products are over
D(\Lambda)
.

Theorem

(Eteve–Gaitsgory–Genestier–Lafforgue)
.

The object
\pi_{N,!}{\mathcal{S}}_{\dot{V}}
in

\displaystyle D((U\smallsetminus N)_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}^{I},\Lambda)

lies in the image of the fully faithful external tensor product functor

\displaystyle\Big[\Ind D_{\lis}((U\smallsetminus N)_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}},\Lambda)\Big]^{\otimes I}\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}D((U\smallsetminus N)_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}^{I},\Lambda)

and naturally lifts to an object of
\Big[\big(\Ind D_{\lis}((U\smallsetminus N)_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}},\Lambda)\big)^{(\Frob_{U\smallsetminus N})_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}^{*}}\Big]^{\otimes I}
.

5.7 Remark
.

Work of Xue
[
71
, Theorem 3.2.3]
10
10

10

While
[
71
, Theorem 3.2.3]
assumes that
G
is split, its proof does not use this. See
[
71
, §4]
.
already implies the first statement in Theorem
5.6
, as well as the second statement after taking cohomology groups
[
71
, Corollary 3.2.6]
. In particular, if one is only interested in Theorem
5.11
below after taking cohomology groups, one does not need to appeal to Theorem
5.6
. For example, this is the case for Corollary
5.12
below.

5.8.

For places in
U
, we have the following (derived) Hecke algebra action on the cohomology of stacks of global shtukas. Let
Q
be a finite set of closed points of
U
. Write

\displaystyle\pi_{\infty(Q\cup Z)}:\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}\Sht^{I}_{G,\dot{V},m(Q\cup Z)}|_{(U\smallsetminus Q)_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}^{I}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(U\smallsetminus Q)_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}^{I}

for the structure morphism, and recall that
\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}\Sht^{I}_{G,\dot{V},m(Q\cup Z)}|_{(U\smallsetminus Q)_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}^{I}}
has a natural

5.12.

As an application of Theorem
5.11
, we can give a quick proof that the excursion operators constructed by V. Lafforgue and Xue agree with those constructed by Fargues–Scholze. We proved this originally in
[
54
, Theorem 6.13]
.

Recall from Example
2.3
the set
\ker^{1}(F,G)
. For any finite set
I
, object
\dot{V}
in
\Rep_{\Lambda}(\prescript{L}{}{G})^{I}
, morphism
x:\mathbf{1}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\dot{V}|_{\Delta(\widehat{G})}
, morphism
\xi:\dot{V}|_{\Delta(\widehat{G})}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\mathbf{1}
, and element
\gamma_{\bullet}
in
W_{F}
, recall that work of V. Lafforgue and Xue defines a
\Lambda
-linear endomorphism
S_{I,\dot{V},x,\xi,\gamma_{\bullet}}
of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\coprod_{\alpha\in\ker^{1}(F,G)}G_{\alpha}(F)\backslash G({\mathbb{A}}),\Lambda)

[
71
, Proposition 2.2.1]
.

Write
{\mathfrak{z}}(G(F_{v}),\Lambda)
for the Bernstein center of
G(F_{v})
over
\Lambda
, and write
V
for the restriction of
\dot{V}
to
(\prescript{L}{}{G}_{v})^{I}
. When
\gamma_{\bullet}
lies in
W_{F_{v}}
, recall that work of Fargues–Scholze defines an element
{\mathfrak{z}}_{I,V,x,\xi,\gamma_{\bullet}}
of
{\mathfrak{z}}(G(F_{v}),\Lambda)

[
25
, Theorem VIII.4.1]
.

Corollary
.

The element
{\mathfrak{z}}_{I,V,x,\xi,\gamma_{\bullet}}
acts on
C_{c}^{\infty}(\coprod_{\alpha\in\ker^{1}(F,G)}G_{\alpha}(F)\backslash G({\mathbb{A}}),\Lambda)
via
S_{I,\dot{V},x,\xi,\gamma_{\bullet}}
.

Proof.

After shrinking
U
, we can assume that
v
lies in
Z
. Take
I_{z}
to be empty for all
z
in
Z\smallsetminus v
, so that
I_{v}=I
. The counit
i_{1,!}i^{*}_{1}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\id
induces a commutative diagram

\textstyle{i^{*}_{1}T_{\mathbf{1}}i_{1,!}i^{*}_{1}\!\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x}
\textstyle{i^{*}_{1}T_{V}i_{1,!}i^{*}_{1}\!\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{\bullet}}
\textstyle{i^{*}_{1}T_{V}i_{1,!}i^{*}_{1}\!\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{i^{*}_{1}T_{\mathbf{1}}i_{1,!}i^{*}_{1}\!\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i^{*}_{1}T_{\mathbf{1}}\!\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x}
\textstyle{i^{*}_{1}T_{V}\!\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{\bullet}}
\textstyle{i^{*}_{1}T_{V}\!\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{i^{*}_{1}T_{\mathbf{1}}\!\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda.}

Because
T_{\mathbf{1}}
is naturally isomorphic to the identity, the left and right arrows are isomorphisms, and the corners are identified with
i^{*}_{1}\!\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda
. By using Lemma
2.6
and arguing as in Example
2.3
, we see that
H^{0}(i^{*}_{1}\!\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda)
is naturally isomorphic to
C_{c}^{\infty}(\coprod_{\alpha\in\ker^{1}(F,G)}G_{\alpha}(F)\backslash G({\mathbb{A}})/G({\mathbb{O}}_{U}),\Lambda)
. After applying
H^{0}(-)
to the above diagram, the top arrow equals the action of
{\mathfrak{z}}_{I,V,x,\xi,\gamma_{\bullet}}
by construction, and the bottom arrow equals
S_{I,\dot{V},x,\xi,\gamma_{\bullet}}
by Theorem
5.11
. This indicates that the restrictions of
{\mathfrak{z}}_{I,V,x,\xi,\gamma_{\bullet}}
and
S_{I,\dot{V},x,\xi,\gamma_{\bullet}}
to
C_{c}^{\infty}(\coprod_{\alpha\in\ker^{1}(F,G)}G_{\alpha}(F)\backslash G({\mathbb{A}})/G({\mathbb{O}}_{U}),\Lambda)
coincide. Finally, taking
\bigcup_{Z}
yields the desired result.
∎

5.13 Remarks
.

1)

The proof of Corollary
5.12
only relies on Theorem
5.11
after taking cohomology groups. In particular, one does not need to appeal to Theorem
5.6
; see Remark
5.7
.

2)

The main ingredient for the representation-theoretic results in
[
54
]
is
[
54
, Theorem 6.13]
. Therefore one can use Corollary
5.12
to prove all the representation-theoretic results in
[
54
]
; see the proof of
[
54
, Theorem 6.16]
.

6.
Relation with Langlands duality

In this section, we discuss the role that
\Bun_{G,F}
plays in the Langlands correspondence. First, we recall the moduli of Galois representations and their derived categories of ind-coherent sheaves, which are the main players on the Galois side. By restricting Galois representations to decomposition groups, we obtain a map
\lres_{Z}
analogous to
\loc_{Z}
; we study the pushforward of the coherent dualizing complex
\omega
along
\lres_{Z}
.

Next, we recall the spectral action of Fargues–Scholze, which enables us to state their categorical Langlands conjecture. We can then state Conjecture F, and we explain how Conjecture F implies conjectures of Arinkin–Gaitsgory–Kazhdan–Raskin–Rozenblyum–Varshavsky and Zhu. Finally, we prove Conjecture F when
G
is commutative; this is Theorem G.

6.1.

We start by recalling the stacks of Galois representations that we will use.
For the rest of this paper, assume that
\ell\geq 3
.

11

\displaystyle\{\mbox{derived qcqs schemes almost of finite type over }\Lambda\},

where we use the notation of Gaitsgory–Rozenblyum
[
30
, p. 273]
for the associated functors instead of the notation of
[

, Definition A.5.6]
. Then
[

, Theorem 3.4.11]
extends this to a
6
-functor formalism on

\displaystyle\{\mbox{derived algebraic stacks over }\Lambda\}.

Let
D
be one of
\{U\neq C,F_{v}\}
. Then
6.1
implies that the value of
\IndCoh
on
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},D}
is naturally equivalent to
\Ind D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},D})
.

6.3.

When restricted to bounded below objects, there is no difference between ind-perfect and ind-coherent objects on
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},D}
. More precisely, applying
\Ind
to the inclusion
D_{\perf}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},D})\subseteq D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},D})
yields a fully faithful functor

\displaystyle\Xi:\Ind D_{\perf}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},D})\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}\Ind D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},D}).

Write
\Psi:\Ind D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},D})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Ind D_{\perf}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},D})
for its right adjoint. Then arguing as in the proof of
[
28
, Proposition 1.2.4]
shows that
\Psi
restricts to an equivalence

\displaystyle\bigcup_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\big[\Ind D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},D})\big]^{\geq n}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\bigcup_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\big[\Ind D_{\perf}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},D})\big]^{\geq n},

which we use to identify
D^{\operatorname{qc}}_{\coh}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},D})
with its image under
\Psi
.

6.4.

Restriction induces the following morphisms between our stacks of Galois representations. For all closed points
u
of
U
and
z
in
Z
, write

\displaystyle\lres_{u}:\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},{\mathcal{O}}_{u}}\mbox{ and }\lres_{z}:\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{z}}

for the resulting morphisms over
\Lambda
. Write
\lres_{Z}:\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\textstyle\prod_{z\in Z}\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{z}}
for the morphism
(\lres_{z})_{z\in Z}
, where all products are over
\Lambda
. By

, the value of
\IndCoh
on
\prod_{z\in Z}\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{z}}
is naturally equivalent to

\displaystyle\bigotimes_{z\in Z}\Ind D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{z}}).

6.5.

For places in
U
, we have the following (derived)
spectral
Hecke algebra action on
\lres_{Z,*}(\omega_{\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U}})
. Let

be a finite nonempty set of closed points of
U
, and write

\displaystyle\lres_{Q}:\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\textstyle\prod_{u\in Q}\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},{\mathcal{O}}_{u}}

for the morphism
(\lres_{u})_{u\in U}
. Then
[

, Lemma 3.34]
implies that the square

\textstyle{\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\lres_{Q},\lres_{Z})}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\big(\prod_{u\in Q}\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},{\mathcal{O}}_{u}}\big)\times\big(\prod_{z\in Z}\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{z}}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U\smallsetminus Q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\big(\displaystyle\prod_{u\in Q}\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{u}}\big)\times\big(\prod_{z\in Z}\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{z}}\big)}

is derived cartesian. Moreover,

implies that bottom arrow is

with the structure of a module for the
{\mathbb{E}}_{1}
-algebra

\displaystyle\End_{D_{\coh}(\prod_{u\in Q}\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{u}})}({\mathscr{O}}_{\prod_{u\in Q}\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},{\mathcal{O}}_{u}}})=\bigotimes_{u\in Q}\End_{D_{\coh}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{u}})}({\mathscr{O}}_{\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},{\mathcal{O}}_{u}}})

over

6.6.

Write
D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{v}})_{\Nilp}\subseteq D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{v}})
for the full subcategory of objects with nilpotent singular support as in
[
25
, VIII.2.2.2]
. It is
\Ind D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{v}})_{\Nilp}
that appears in the categorical local Langlands conjecture, so we need to check that
\lres_{Z,*}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U})
lies in this subcategory.

Proposition
.

The object
\lres_{Z,*}(\omega_{\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U}})
in
\bigotimes_{z\in Z}\Ind D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{z}})
lies in

\displaystyle\bigotimes_{z\in Z}\Ind D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{z}})_{\Nilp}.

Proof.

When
Z
is empty, this condition is also empty. When
Z
is nonempty,
[
73
, Theorem 3.29]
implies that
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U}^{\square}
is quasismooth over
\Lambda
, so
[
7
, Corollary 2.2.8]
12
12

12

While
[
7
]
works over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, the proof of
[
7
, Corollary 2.2.8]
does not use this assumption.
shows that its dualizing complex
\omega_{\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U}^{\square}}
is a shift of a line bundle. By smooth descent, the same holds for
\omega_{\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U}}
, so the result follows from
6.3
.
∎

6.7.

The local automorphic and spectral sides are related by the following
spectral action
of Fargues–Scholze. Write
Z_{G}
for the center of
G
over
F
, and
for the rest of this paper, assume that
\pi_{0}((Z_{G})_{\overline{F}})
is invertible in
\Lambda
.
Then
[

, Theorem 6.1]
and
6.1
show that there is a natural
\Lambda
-linear action of

on

. Write
V_{v}
for the vector bundle on
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{v}}
associated with the object
V_{v}
in
\Rep_{\Lambda}(\prescript{L}{}{G})^{I}
, so that by construction
V_{v}
in
D_{\perf}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{v}})
acts on
D(\Bun_{G,F_{v}},\Lambda)^{\omega}
via

.

By restricting to

and applying

, the above naturally extends to a

-linear colimit-preserving action of

on

.

6.8.

We now use the spectral action to state the categorical local Langlands conjecture of Fargues–Scholze.
For the rest of this paper, assume that
G
is quasisplit over
F
.
Then we can choose
B
to be a Borel subgroup of
G
over
F
and
T
to be a maximal subtorus of
B
over
F
. Write
N
for the unipotent radical of

, and let

be a continuous homomorphism trivial on

such that, for all closed points

of

, its restriction
\psi_{v}
to

is generic.

Consider the colimit-preserving functor
a_{\psi_{v}}:\Ind D_{\perf}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{v}})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D(\Bun_{G,F_{v}},\Lambda)
given by acting as in
6.7
on

. Denote its right adjoint by

\displaystyle c_{\psi_{v}}:D(\Bun_{G,F_{v}},\Lambda)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Ind D_{\perf}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{v}}).

Conjecture

(
[
25
, Conjecture X.3.5]
)
.

The functor

restricts to an equivalence

\displaystyle D(\Bun_{G,F_{v}},\Lambda)^{\omega}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{v}})_{\Nilp}.

Consequently, applying
\Ind
yields an equivalence

\displaystyle{\mathbb{L}}_{\psi_{v}}:D(\Bun_{G,F_{v}},\Lambda)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Ind D_{\coh}^{\operatorname{qc}}(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},F_{v}})_{\Nilp}.

Conjecture
6.8
is known when
G
is a torus
[
75
, Theorem 6.4.1]
.

6.11 Example
.

Assume that
Z
is empty. Then Example
2.3
identifies
\Bun_{G,C}
with the constant stack over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
associated with the groupoid (

♡

‣
2.3
), so

\displaystyle\loc_{\varnothing,!}\!\Lambda=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\ker^{1}(F,G)}\Gamma_{c}(\underline{G_{\alpha}(F)\backslash G({\mathbb{A}})/G({\mathbb{O}})},\Lambda),

where we remind the reader that

can have cohomology in nonzero degrees when

[
71
, Remark 0.0.3]
. On the other hand, by definition

Altogether, we see that Conjecture

specializes to

, Conjecture 24.8.6]
when

, and

6.12.

More generally, Conjecture

implies the following formula for the cohomology of stacks of global shtukas for

of

, which is a conjecture of Zhu
[
73
, Conjecture 4.49]
. Let

is basic, and recall from

over

. Since

is an inner twist of

over

, we have compatible identifications
\prescript{L}{}{G}=\prescript{L}{}{G}_{b}
and
\prescript{L}{}{G}_{v}=\prescript{L}{}{G}_{b,v}
. Write

\displaystyle\pi_{\infty Z}^{G_{b}}:\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}\Sht^{I}_{G_{b},\dot{V},mZ}|_{(U\smallsetminus Q)^{I}_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(U\smallsetminus Q)_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}^{I}

for the structure morphism.

Recall the open embedding
i_{b}:*/\underline{G_{b}(F_{z})}\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}

[
25
, Theorem III.4.5]
, so that taking the product over all

in
Z
yields an open embedding

\displaystyle i_{b}:*/\underline{G_{b}(F_{Z})}\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}\textstyle\prod_{z\in Z}\Bun_{G,F_{z}}.

For all

in
Z
, let
K_{z}
be a compact open subgroup of

. Write
K

, and consider the structure morphism

\displaystyle\pi_{K}^{G_{b}}:\Big[\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}\Sht^{I}_{G_{b},\dot{V},mZ}|_{(U\smallsetminus Q)^{I}_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}}\Big]\Big/\underline{K}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(U\smallsetminus Q)_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}^{I}.

Write
\dot{V}
for the vector bundle on
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U}
associated with the object
\dot{V}
in
\Rep_{\Lambda}(\prescript{L}{}{G})^{I}

{\mathbb{D}}(-)
for the Grothendieck–Serre dual.

Proposition
.

Assume Conjecture
6.10
. Then the image of
\pi_{K,!}^{G_{b}}{\mathcal{S}}_{\dot{V}}
in

\displaystyle\bigotimes_{z\in Z}D_{\qcoh}\big((\Spec\Lambda)/\!\WD_{F_{z}}\big)^{\bigotimes I_{z}}

is isomorphic as modules for
\bigotimes_{u}\End_{G(F_{u})}(\cInd_{G({\mathcal{O}}_{u})}^{G(F_{u})}\Lambda)
to

\displaystyle\Gamma\big(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U},\dot{V}\otimes\lres_{Z}^{!}(\boxtimes_{z\in Z}{\mathbb{D}}({\mathbb{L}}_{\psi_{z}}(i_{b,!}\cInd_{K_{z}}^{G_{b}(F_{z})})))\big).

Proof.

Because

is basic, Proposition

and Theorem

identify

\displaystyle\pi_{K,!}^{G_{b}}{\mathcal{S}}_{\dot{V}}=(\pi_{\infty Z,!}^{G_{b}}{\mathcal{S}}_{\dot{V}})^{K}

\displaystyle=\Hom_{G_{b}(F_{Z})}\!\big(\cInd_{K}^{G_{b}(F_{Z})}\Lambda,\pi_{\infty Z,!}^{G_{b}}{\mathcal{S}}_{\dot{V}}\big)

\displaystyle=\Hom_{G_{b}(F_{Z})}\!\big(\cInd_{K}^{G_{b}(F_{Z})}\Lambda,i_{b}^{*}(T_{V}(\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda))\big)

\displaystyle=\Hom\!\big(i_{b,!}\cInd_{K}^{G_{b}(F_{Z})}\Lambda,T_{V}(\loc_{Z,!}\!\Lambda)\big).

By Conjecture
6.10
and the projection formula, this is isomorphic to

\displaystyle\Hom\!\big(\boxtimes_{z\in Z}{\mathbb{L}}_{\psi_{z}}(i_{b,!}\cInd_{K_{z}}^{G_{b}(F_{z})}\Lambda),V\otimes\lres_{Z,*}(\omega_{\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U}})\big)

\displaystyle=\,

Finally, applying Grothendieck–Serre duality yields

\displaystyle\Hom\!\big(\lres_{Z,!}({\mathbb{D}}(\dot{V}\otimes\omega_{\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U}})),\boxtimes_{z\in Z}{\mathbb{D}}({\mathbb{L}}_{\psi_{z}}(i_{b,!}\cInd_{K_{z}}^{G_{b}(F_{z})}))\big)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\Hom\!\big(\lres_{Z,!}(\dot{V}^{\vee}),\boxtimes_{z\in Z}{\mathbb{D}}({\mathbb{L}}_{\psi_{z}}(i_{b,!}\cInd_{K_{z}}^{G_{b}(F_{z})}))\big)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\Gamma\big(\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{G},U},\dot{V}\otimes\lres_{Z}^{!}(\boxtimes_{z\in Z}{\mathbb{D}}({\mathbb{L}}_{\psi_{z}}(i_{b,!}\cInd_{K_{z}}^{G_{b}(F_{z})})))\big).\qed

.

6.14.

In this rest of this section, our goal is to prove Conjecture
6.10
when
G=T
is a torus. We start by recalling work of Langlands
[
52
]
on his conjectures for tori. Write
W_{\widetilde{F}/F}\coloneqq W_{F}/\overline{[W_{\widetilde{F}},W_{\widetilde{F}}]}
for the relative Weil group of
\widetilde{F}/F
, so that global class field theory gives a natural short exact sequence of locally profinite groups

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{F}^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}_{\widetilde{F}}^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{\widetilde{F}/F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gal(\widetilde{F}/F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1.}

Write
{\mathbb{T}}
for the locally profinite group
\big[T(\widetilde{F})\backslash T({\mathbb{A}}_{\widetilde{F}})\big]^{\Gal(\widetilde{F}/F)}
. Then the long exact sequence for group cohomology yields a natural short exact sequence

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T(F)\backslash T({\mathbb{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{T}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ker^{1}(F,T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}

of locally profinite groups. Recall that the transfer map induces an isomorphism

\displaystyle H_{1}(W_{\widetilde{F}/F},X_{*}(T))\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}H_{1}(\widetilde{F}^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}_{\widetilde{F}}^{\times},X_{*}(T))^{\Gal(\widetilde{F}/F)}={\mathbb{T}}

[
52
, p. 233]
, and for all
\Gal(\widetilde{F}/F)
-stable open subgroups
P
of
\widetilde{F}^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}_{\widetilde{F}}^{\times}
, write

\displaystyle\Theta(P)\coloneqq\ker\big[H_{1}(W_{\widetilde{F}/F},X_{*}(T))\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}H_{1}(W_{\widetilde{F}/F}/P,X_{*}(T))\big].

Lemma
.

Under the identification
H_{1}(W_{\widetilde{F}/F},X_{*}(T))\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}{\mathbb{T}}
, the subgroup
\Theta(P)
corresponds to the image of
P\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}X_{*}(T)\subseteq T(\widetilde{F})\backslash T({\mathbb{A}}_{\widetilde{F}})
under the norm map.

Proof.

The five term exact sequence for group homology identifies
\Theta(P)
with the image of
H_{1}(P,X_{*}(T))=P\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}X_{*}(T)
under the corestriction map. Postcomposing the corestriction map with the transfer map yields the norm map, as desired.
∎

6.15.

Assume that
Z
is nonempty. We will stratify
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},U}
by bounding ramification as follows. Let
K
be a
\Gal(\widetilde{F}/F)
-stable compact open subgroup of
\widetilde{F}_{\widetilde{Z}}^{\times}
, and write
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},K}^{\square}
for the presheaf on
\{\mbox{affine schemes over }\Lambda\}
whose
\Spec{A}
-points consist of
1
-cocycles
W_{\widetilde{F}/F}/{\mathbb{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}^{\times}K\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\widehat{T}(A)
. Since
W_{\widetilde{F}/F}/{\mathbb{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}^{\times}K
is finitely generated,
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},K}^{\square}
is an affine scheme of finite type over
\Lambda
. Write
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},K}
for the algebraic stack
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},K}^{\square}\!/\widehat{T}
over
\Lambda
.

For any finitely generated abelian group
M
, write
\underline{\Hom}(M,{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
for its Cartier dual over
\Lambda
. Note that
\underline{\Hom}(M,{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
is a flat affine scheme of finite type over
\Lambda
.

Proposition
.

The algebraic stack
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},K}
is naturally a gerbe over

\displaystyle\underline{\Hom}\big({\mathbb{T}}/{\mathbb{O}}_{U}^{\times}\!\Nm_{\widetilde{F}/F}(K\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}X_{*}(T)),{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\big)

banded by
\widehat{T}^{\Gal(\widetilde{F}/F)}
.

Proof.

Write
H^{\mathrm{pre}}
for the presheaf on
\{\mbox{affine schemes over }\Lambda\}
whose
\Spec{A}
-points consist of the set-theoretic quotient

\displaystyle\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},K}^{\square}(A)\big/\widehat{T}(A)=H^{1}\big(W_{\widetilde{F}/F}/{\mathbb{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}^{\times}K,\widehat{T}(A)\big),

and write
H
for its fpqc-sheafification. Using the presentation
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},K}=\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},K}^{\square}\!/\widehat{T}
, we see the inertia stack of
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},K}
is naturally isomorphic to
\widehat{T}^{\Gal(\widetilde{F}/F)}\times\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},K}
over
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},K}
. Therefore
[
68
, Tag 06QJ]
shows that
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},K}
is naturally a gerbe over
H
banded by
\widehat{T}^{\Gal(\widetilde{F}/F)}
.

Since
X^{*}(T)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A^{\times}=\widehat{T}(A)
, the natural map

\displaystyle H^{1}\big(W_{\widetilde{F}/F}/{\mathbb{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}^{\times}K,X^{*}(T)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A^{\times}\big)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Hom\big(H_{1}\big(W_{\widetilde{F},F}/{\mathbb{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}^{\times}K,X_{*}(T)\big),A^{\times}\big)

yields a morphism
\iota^{\mathrm{pre}}:H^{\mathrm{pre}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\underline{\Hom}(H_{1}(W_{\widetilde{F},F}/{\mathbb{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}^{\times}K,X_{*}(T)),{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
. When
A^{\times}
is divisible, the above map is an isomorphism, so
[
75
, Lemma 4.1.1]
implies that
\iota^{\mathrm{pre}}
fpqc-sheafifies to an isomorphism
\iota:H\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\underline{\Hom}(H_{1}(W_{\widetilde{F},F}/{\mathbb{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}^{\times}K,X_{*}(T)),{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
. Finally, Lemma
6.14
shows that the transfer map yields an isomorphism

\displaystyle H_{1}\big(W_{\widetilde{F},F}/{\mathbb{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}^{\times}K,X_{*}(T)\big)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}{\mathbb{T}}\big/{\mathbb{O}}_{U}^{\times}\!\Nm_{\widetilde{F}/F}(K\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}X_{*}(T)).\qed

6.16.

Assume that
Z
is nonempty. Using work of Langlands, we can prove the following explicit description of
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},U}
.

Theorem
.

The derived algebraic stack
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},U}
is classical and equals

\displaystyle\bigcup_{K}\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},K},

where
K
runs over
\Gal(\widetilde{F}/F)
-stable pro-
p
open subgroups of
\widetilde{F}_{\widetilde{Z}}^{\times}
and the transition morphisms are clopen embeddings.

Proof.

Recall that the
\Spec{A}
-points of
\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},U}^{\square,\cl}
consist of
1
-cocycles
\varphi:W_{U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\widehat{T}(A)
that are continuous with respect to the ind-
\ell
-adic topology on

as in
[

. Because

is a homomorphism and
\widehat{T}(A)
is abelian,
\varphi
factors through
W_{U}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}W_{\widetilde{F}/F}/{\mathbb{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}^{\times}
. Since
\widetilde{F}^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}_{\widetilde{F}}^{\times}/{\mathbb{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}^{\times}
is locally pro-
p
, there exists a
\Gal(\widetilde{F}/F)
-stable pro-
p
open subgroup
K
of
\widetilde{F}^{\times}_{\widetilde{Z}}
such that

factors through

. This shows that

\displaystyle\LS^{\square,\cl}_{\prescript{L}{}{T},U}=\bigcup_{K}\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},K}^{\square},

where
K
runs over
\Gal(\widetilde{F}/F)
-stable pro-
p
open subgroups of
\widetilde{F}_{\widetilde{Z}}^{\times}
, and the proof of
[

, Theorem VIII.1.3]
implies that the transition morphisms are clopen embeddings. Quotienting by
\widehat{T}
yields an analogous description

\displaystyle\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},U}^{\cl}=\bigcup_{K}\LS_{\prescript{L}{}{T},K}.

Because the finitely generated abelian group
{\mathbb{T}}/{\mathbb{O}}_{U}^{\times}\!\Nm_{\widetilde{F}/F}(K\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}X_{*}(T))
has rank
\operatorname{dim}\widehat{T}^{\Gal(\widetilde{F}/F)}
, its Cartier dual

over

over

. Finally,

over

is classical.
∎

6.17.
