Appendix A
GAGA over sousperfectoid spaces

Our goal in this section is to prove Theorem H. Actually, we prove a generalization of Theorem H over any complete Tate Huber pair, though stating it requires the analytic geometry of Clausen–Scholze
[
62
]
; see Theorem
A.18
. While we were finalizing this paper, a generalization of Theorem
A.18
was proved independently by Wang
[
70
, Theorem 4.4.4]
along very similar lines.

The proof of Theorem
A.18
closely follows Clausen–Scholze’s proof of GAGA for complex analytic spaces
[
14
, Theorem 13.10]
. Namely, given an abstract formalism of “analytic loci”, first we use discrete Huber pairs to prove a GAGA theorem for
all
solid quasi-coherent sheaves. Next, we prove the analogue of Grauert’s coherence theorem in our setting. Finally, we use this to prove that the previous GAGA theorem restricts to an equivalence on pseudocoherent objects.

We conclude by applying Theorem H to prove that the analytification of algebraic stacks of bundles agrees with analytic stacks of bundles, answering a question of Heuer–Xu
[
40
, Remark 8.1.2]
. This was also proved independently by Wang
[
70
]
.

Notation

For any (animated) condensed ring
{\mathcal{A}}
, write
D_{\cond}({\mathcal{A}})
for its derived category of condensed
{\mathcal{A}}
-modules, and recall that there is a natural fully faithful functor
D({\mathcal{A}}(*))\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}D_{\cond}({\mathcal{A}})
.

A.1.

We start with some notation on
analytic rings
. Write
\AnRing
for the
\infty
-category of (normalized animated) analytic rings as in
[
62
, Definition 12.11]
, and for any analytic ring
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
, write
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})\subseteq D_{\cond}({\mathcal{A}})
for the full subcategory of complete objects as in
[
62
, Remark 12.5]
. Recall that
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})\subseteq D_{\cond}({\mathcal{A}})
determines the analytic ring structure
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
on the underlying condensed ring
{\mathcal{A}}
.

Recall that
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})\mapsto D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
yields a functor
D(-):\AnRing\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Sym
.

Lemma
.

The functor
D(-):\AnRing\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Sym
preserves pushouts.

Proof.

Consider a pushout square in
\AnRing

\textstyle{({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathcal{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathcal{B}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathcal{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\mathcal{D}},{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathcal{D}}).}

Then
[
62
, Proposition 12.12]
shows that we have a pullback square

\textstyle{D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathcal{A}})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces D({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{M}}_{B})}
\textstyle{D({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathcal{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces D({\mathcal{D}},{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathcal{D}}),}

so the result follows from passing to adjoints.
∎

A.2.

Next, let us recall the theory of quasi-coherent modules for analytic rings. For any
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
in
\AnRing
, write
\AnSpec({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
for the corresponding object of
\AnRing^{\mathrm{op}}
. Endow
\AnRing^{\mathrm{op}}
with the topology generated by finite families of jointly conservative steady localizations, as in
[
62
, p. 95]
. Consider the following classes of morphisms
\AnSpec({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{N}})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\AnSpec({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
in
\AnRing^{\mathrm{op}}
:

(
I
)

the functor
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{N}})
is an open embedding as in
[
14
, p. 61]
,

(
P
)

({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{N}})
is induced from
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
along
{\mathcal{A}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}{\mathcal{B}}
as in
[
62
, Proposition 12.8]
.

Write
E
for the class of morphisms in
\AnRing^{\mathrm{op}}
of the form
p\circ i
for
p
in
P
and
i
in
I
. Then the proof of
[
59
, Lemma 3.2.5]
13
13

13

While
[
59
, Lemma 3.2.5]
restricts to the full subcategory of
\AnRing^{\mathrm{op}}
consisting of solid affinoids as in
[
59
, Definition 2.6.6]
, this hypothesis is not used in the proof of
[
59
, Lemma 3.2.5]
.
shows that

\bullet

I
and
P
form a suitable decomposition of
E
as in
[
56
, Definition A.5.9]
,

\bullet

the functor
D(-):\AnRing\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Sym
satisfies the conditions in
[
41
, Proposition A.5.10]
with respect to
I
and
P
,

so
[
41
, Proposition A.5.10]
endows
\AnSpec({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})\mapsto D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
with the structure of a 6-functor formalism on
\AnRing^{\mathrm{op}}
with
E
as its class of
!
-able morphisms. By
[
62
, Proposition 12.18]
, this
6
-functor formalism is sheafy as in
[
41
, Definition 3.4.1]
.

Write
\{\mbox{analytic stacks}\}
for the
\infty
-category of sheaves of anima on
\AnRing^{\mathrm{op}}
. Then
[
41
, Theorem 3.4.11]
extends the above
6
-functor formalism on
\AnRing^{\mathrm{op}}
to a sheafy
6
-functor formalism on
\{\mbox{analytic stacks}\}
. Note that the
\infty
-category of analytic spaces as in
[
62
, Definition 13.5]
is a full subcategory of
\{\mbox{analytic stacks}\}
.

A.3.

Nuclear
modules enjoy the following compatibility with induced analytic ring structures. Let
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
be an analytic ring, let
{\mathcal{B}}
be an algebra in
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
, and write
({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{N}})
for the analytic ring induced from
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
along
{\mathcal{A}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}{\mathcal{B}}
as in
[
62
, Proposition 12.8]
. Then Lemma
A.1
implies that
D({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{N}})
is naturally equivalent to
\Mod_{\mathcal{B}}(D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}}))
, so the image of
-\otimes_{({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{N}})}-
in
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
is given by
-\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}-
.

Lemma
.

Assume that
{\mathcal{B}}
in
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
is nuclear as in
[
14
, Definition 8.5 (1)]
. Then an object
C
of
D({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{N}})
is nuclear if and only if its image in
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
is nuclear.

Proof.

Recall that
C
in
D({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{N}})
is nuclear if and only if, for all extremally disconnected
S
, the natural morphism

\displaystyle(\underline{\Hom}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{N}}[S],{\mathcal{B}})\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}C)(*)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\underline{\Hom}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{N}}[S],C)(*)

is an isomorphism. Adjunction shows that
\underline{\Hom}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{N}}[S],-)=\underline{\Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{M}}[S],-)
, and because
{\mathcal{B}}
in
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
is nuclear,
[
1
, Proposition 5.35]
shows that
\underline{\Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{M}}[S],{\mathcal{B}})
is naturally isomorphic to
\underline{\Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{M}}[S],{\mathcal{A}})\otimes_{({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})}{\mathcal{B}}
. Therefore the above becomes

\displaystyle(\underline{\Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{M}}[S],{\mathcal{A}})\otimes_{({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})}C)(*)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\underline{\Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{M}}[S],C)(*),

and this is an isomorphism for all extremally disconnected
S
if and only if the image of
C
in
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
is nuclear.
∎

A.4.

When the analytic rings are all nuclear over a base, we can check nuclearity on a cover as follows. Let
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
be an analytic ring, let
{\mathcal{B}}
be a nuclear algebra in
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
, and write
({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{N}})
for the analytic ring induced from
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
along
{\mathcal{A}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}{\mathcal{B}}
as in
[
62
, Proposition 12.8]
.

Let
\{{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{r}
be a finite family of nuclear algebras in
D({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{N}})
, and write
({\mathcal{B}}_{j},{\mathcal{N}}_{j})
for the analytic ring induced from
({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{N}})
along
{\mathcal{B}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
as in
[
62
, Proposition 12.8]
for all
1\leq j\leq r
. Assume that
\big\{({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{N}})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}({\mathcal{B}}_{j},{\mathcal{N}}_{j})\big\}_{j=1}^{r}
is a family of jointly conservative steady localizations.

Proposition
.

An object
C
of
D({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{N}})
is nuclear if and only if
C\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
in
D({\mathcal{B}}_{j},{\mathcal{N}}_{j})
is nuclear for all
1\leq j\leq r
.

Proof.

Since the functor
D({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{N}})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D({\mathcal{B}}_{j},{\mathcal{N}}_{j})
is symmetric monoidal, it preserves trace-class morphisms as in
[
14
, Definition 8.1]
. It also preserves colimits, so it preserves basic nuclear objects as in
[
14
, Definition 8.5 (2)]
and hence nuclear objects by
[
14
, Theorem 8.6 (2)]
.

Conversely, assume that
C\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
in
D({\mathcal{B}}_{j},{\mathcal{N}}_{j})
is nuclear for all
1\leq j\leq r
. Then Lemma
A.3
indicates that the image of
C\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
in
D({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{N}})
is nuclear. Descent shows that
C
is naturally isomorphic to a finite limit with terms of the form
C\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\bigotimes_{j\in J}{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
for nonempty subsets
J
of
\{1,\dotsc,r\}
, and because the
C\otimes_{\mathcal{B}}\bigotimes_{j\in J}{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
are nuclear,
[
14
, Theorem 8.6 (1)]
implies that
C
is also nuclear.
∎

A.5.

In the following situation, we can check
pseudocompactness
on a cover. Let
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
be an analytic ring. Let
\big\{({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}({\mathcal{A}}_{j},{\mathcal{M}}_{j})\big\}_{j=1}^{r}
be a finite family of jointly conservative steady localizations such that, for all
1\leq j\leq r
, the functor
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D({\mathcal{A}}_{j},{\mathcal{M}}_{j})
is the open embedding as in
[
14
, p. 61]
corresponding to an idempotent algebra
{\mathcal{I}}_{j}
in
D^{\leq 0}({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
.

Proposition
.

An object
C
of
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
is pseudocompact as in
[
14
, Definition 9.9]
if and only if its image in
D({\mathcal{A}}_{j},{\mathcal{M}}_{j})
is pseudocompact for all
1\leq j\leq r
.

Proof.

Because
D({\mathcal{A}}_{j},{\mathcal{M}}_{j})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
is left
t
-exact and preserves direct sums, if
C
is pseudocompact, then its image in
D({\mathcal{A}}_{j},{\mathcal{M}}_{j})
is pseudocompact.

Conversely, assume that the image of
C
in
D({\mathcal{A}}_{j},{\mathcal{M}}_{j})
is pseudocompact for all
1\leq j\leq r
. Since the composition
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D({\mathcal{A}}_{j},{\mathcal{M}}_{j})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
is given by
\underline{\Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(\fib({\mathcal{A}}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}{\mathcal{I}}_{j}),-)
and
{\mathcal{I}}_{j}
lies in
D^{\leq 0}({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
, we see that
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D({\mathcal{A}}_{j},{\mathcal{M}}_{j})
sends
D^{\leq 0}({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
to
D^{\leq 1}({\mathcal{A}}_{j},{\mathcal{M}}_{j})
. Descent shows that
\Hom_{\mathcal{A}}(C,-)
is naturally isomorphic to a finite limit with terms of the form

\displaystyle\Hom_{\bigotimes_{j\in J}({\mathcal{A}}_{j},{\mathcal{M}}_{j})}(C\otimes_{({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})}\textstyle\bigotimes_{j\in J}({\mathcal{A}}_{j},{\mathcal{M}}_{j}),-\otimes_{({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})}\bigotimes_{j\in J}({\mathcal{A}}_{j},{\mathcal{M}}_{j}))

for nonempty subsets
J
of
\{1,\dotsc,r\}
. The above shows that
-\otimes_{({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})}\bigotimes_{j\in J}({\mathcal{A}}_{j},{\mathcal{M}}_{j})
sends
D^{\leq 0}({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
to
D^{\leq(\#J)}\big(\!\bigotimes_{j\in J}({\mathcal{A}}_{j},{\mathcal{M}}_{j})\big)
. Because
C\otimes_{({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})}\bigotimes_{j\in J}({\mathcal{A}}_{j},{\mathcal{M}}_{j})
is pseudocompact, this implies that
C
is also pseudocompact.
∎

A.6.

Next, let us recall the analytic rings associated with complete Huber pairs. For any complete Huber pair
(A,A^{+})
, write
(A,A^{+})_{\blacksquare}
for the associated analytic ring as in
[
62
, Proposition 13.16]
, write
(A,A^{+})_{\blacksquare}[-]
for its functor of measures, and write
D_{\blacksquare}(A,A^{+})
for
D((A,A^{+})_{\blacksquare})
. When
A^{+}
equals
A^{\circ}
, we omit it from our notation. By
[
1
, Proposition 3.29]
, we see that
D_{\blacksquare}(A,A^{+})
is naturally equivalent to
\Mod_{A}(D_{\blacksquare}(A^{+}))
.

Lemma
.

We have the following pushout squares in
\AnRing
:

\textstyle{({\mathbb{Z}}[X,Y],{\mathbb{Z}})_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\mathbb{Z}}[X,Y],{\mathbb{Z}}[X])_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\mathbb{Z}}[X,Y],{\mathbb{Z}}[Y])_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}[X,Y]_{\blacksquare},}

\textstyle{({\mathbb{Z}}[XY],{\mathbb{Z}})_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}[XY]_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\mathbb{Z}}[X,Y],{\mathbb{Z}})_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\mathbb{Z}}[X,Y],{\mathbb{Z}}[XY])_{\blacksquare},}
\textstyle{({\mathbb{Z}}[X+Y],{\mathbb{Z}})_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}[X+Y]_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\mathbb{Z}}[X,Y],{\mathbb{Z}})_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\mathbb{Z}}[X,Y],{\mathbb{Z}}[X+Y])_{\blacksquare}.}

Proof.

This follows from combining
[
62
, Proposition 12.12]
with the characterization of
(-,-)_{\blacksquare}
from
[
1
, Proposition 3.32]
.
∎

A.7.

The following analytic rings correspond to the complements of the closed
14
14

14

“Closed” in the sense of being defined by a non-strict inequality; our closed unit disks are actually open in the affine line, so their complements in the affine line are actually closed.
unit disk centered at the origin and at infinity. Recall that
{\mathbb{Z}}(\!(T^{-1})\!)
is an idempotent algebra in

[

, Observation 8.7]
, and the corresponding open embedding as in

[

\textstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}[\![U]\!]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}[T]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{UT-1}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}[\![U]\!]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}[T]}

is a projective resolution of

[

Recall that

Lemma
.

{\mathbb{Z}}[\![T]\!]
is also an idempotent algebra in

, and we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{Z}}(\!(T^{-1})\!)\otimes_{({\mathbb{Z}}[T],{\mathbb{Z}})_{\blacksquare}}{\mathbb{Z}}[\![T]\!]=0.

Proof.

By
[

, Example 6.4]
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{Z}}[\![T]\!]\otimes_{({\mathbb{Z}}[T]_{\blacksquare},{\mathbb{Z}})}{\mathbb{Z}}[\![T]\!]=({\mathbb{Z}}[\![T_{1}]\!]\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\blacksquare}}{\mathbb{Z}}[\![T_{2}]\!])/(T_{1}-T_{2})={\mathbb{Z}}[\![T_{1},T_{2}]\!]/(T_{1}-T_{2})={\mathbb{Z}}[\![T]\!].

Next, using the above projective resolution of

\textstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}[\![U]\!]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}[\![T]\!].}

After solidification,

, Example 6.4]
indicates that both terms become
{\mathbb{Z}}[\![U,T]\!]
. Because

is invertible in

, the resulting complex is indeed exact.
∎

A.8.

Let
(R,R^{+})
be a complete Huber pair.

Lemma
.

We have a pushout square in
\AnRing

\textstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}[T]_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(R\langle T\rangle,R^{+}\langle T\rangle)_{\blacksquare}.}

Proof.

Write
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
for the pushout. Since
[
1
, Lemma 3.8]
indicates that
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\blacksquare}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}{\mathbb{Z}}[T]_{\blacksquare}
is steady, we see that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle=R\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\blacksquare}}{\mathbb{Z}}[T]_{\blacksquare}

by
[
62
, p. 84]
,

\displaystyle=R\langle T\rangle

by
[
1
, Proposition 3.14]
.

Next,
[
62
, Proposition 12.12]
indicates that
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})\subseteq D_{\cond}(R\langle T\rangle)
is the full subcategory of objects whose images in
D_{\cond}({\mathbb{Z}}[T])
and
D_{\cond}(R)
lie in
D_{\blacksquare}({\mathbb{Z}}[T])
and
D_{\blacksquare}(R,R^{+})
, respectively. Because
R^{+}\langle T\rangle
is topologically generated by
{\mathbb{Z}}[T]
and
R^{+}
,
[
1
, Proposition 3.32]
implies that
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
equals
D_{\blacksquare}(R\langle T\rangle,R^{+}\langle T\rangle)
.
∎

A.9.

One can use analytic rings to define the following version of analytification; remarkably, it still remembers algebraic functions. For any
R
-algebra
A
, write
A^{\alg}
for the pushout in
\AnRing

\textstyle{(R_{\disc},R^{+}_{\disc})_{{\blacksquare}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(A_{\disc},(R^{+})_{\disc}^{\sim})_{{\blacksquare}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\alg}.}

Since
A_{\disc}
is nuclear in
D(R_{\disc},R^{+}_{\disc})
,
[
62
, Proposition 13.14]
shows that the top arrow is steady. Therefore underlying condensed ring of
A^{\alg}
is
A
, by
[
62
, p. 84]
.

For any
f
in
A
, write
A_{\absolutevalue{f}\leq 1}
and
A_{\absolutevalue{f}\geq 1}
for the following pushouts in
\AnRing
:

\textstyle{({\mathbb{Z}}[T],{\mathbb{Z}})_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}[T]_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\alg}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{\absolutevalue{f}\leq 1},}
\textstyle{({\mathbb{Z}}[T],{\mathbb{Z}})_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{({\mathbb{Z}}[T^{\pm 1}],{\mathbb{Z}}[T^{-1}])_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\alg}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{\absolutevalue{f}\geq 1}.}

Because the top arrows are steady localizations, the same holds for the bottom arrows.

A.10.

The following analytic ring corresponds to the closure of the closed unit disk centered at the origin. For the rest of this section, assume that
R
is Tate.

Proposition
.

The object
R\langle T\rangle
in
D_{\blacksquare}(R,R^{+})
is nuclear as in
[
14
, Definition 8.5 (1)]
. Moreover, the analytic ring induced from
(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}
along
R\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}R\langle T\rangle
as in
[
62
, Proposition 12.8]
is naturally isomorphic to
(R\langle T\rangle,R^{+}+T\cdot R^{\circ\circ}\langle T\rangle)_{\blacksquare}
, and the induced morphism
R[T]^{\alg}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(R\langle T\rangle,R^{+}+T\cdot R^{\circ\circ}\langle T\rangle)_{\blacksquare}
is a steady localization.

Proof.

Since
R
is Tate, the first statement follows from
[
2
, Lemma 4.4]
. For the second statement, write
({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
for the analytic ring induced form
(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}
along
R\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}R\langle T\rangle
as in
[
62
, Proposition 12.8]
. Because
R\langle T\rangle
lies in
D_{\blacksquare}(R,R^{+})
, we see that
{\mathcal{A}}=R\langle T\rangle
. Next,
[
62
, Proposition 12.12]
indicates that
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})\subseteq D_{\cond}(R\langle T\rangle)
is the full subcategory of objects whose image in
D_{\cond}(R)
lies in
D_{\blacksquare}(R,R^{+})
. Since
R^{+}+T\cdot R^{\circ\circ}\langle T\rangle
is topologically generated by
R^{+}
,
[
1
, Proposition 3.32]
implies that
D({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
equals
D_{\blacksquare}(R\langle T\rangle,R^{+}+T\cdot R^{\circ\circ}\langle T\rangle)
, as desired.

For the third statement,
[
62
, Proposition 13.14]
and the first statement imply

\displaystyle(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(R\langle T\rangle,R^{+}+T\cdot R^{\circ\circ}\langle T\rangle)_{\blacksquare}

is steady, so
[
62
, Proposition 12.15]
shows that

\displaystyle R[T]^{\alg}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(R\langle T\rangle,R^{+}+T\cdot R^{\circ\circ}\langle T\rangle)_{\blacksquare}

is also steady. Finally, Lemma
A.8
implies that the composition

\displaystyle R[T]^{\alg}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(R\langle T\rangle,R^{+}+T\cdot R^{\circ\circ}\langle T\rangle)_{\blacksquare}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(R\langle T\rangle,R^{+}\langle T\rangle)_{\blacksquare}

is a localization. Because the right arrow is a localization, the same holds for the left arrow.
∎

A.11.

For any
R
-algebra
A
and
f
in
A
, write
\overline{A}_{\absolutevalue{f}\leq 1}
for the pushout in
\AnRing

\textstyle{R[T]^{\alg}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{(R\langle T\rangle,R^{+}+T\cdot R^{\circ\circ}\langle T\rangle)_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\alg}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{A}_{\absolutevalue{f}\leq 1}.}

The following analytic spaces correspond the loci in
\AnSpec A^{\alg}
where
f
is analytic and where
f
is analytically invertible, respectively. Choose a pseudouniformizer
\varpi
of
R
, form the colimits in
\{\mbox{analytic stacks}\}

\displaystyle\an(A,f)\coloneqq\varinjlim_{n\geq 0}\AnSpec A_{\absolutevalue{\vpi^nf}\leq 1}\mbox{ and }D^{\an}(f)\coloneqq\varinjlim_{n\geq 0}\AnSpec A_{\absolutevalue{\vpi^{-n}f}\geq 1},

and note that they do not depend on the choice of
\varpi
. Moreover, the factorization

\displaystyle(R\langle\varpi T\rangle,R^{+}\langle\varpi T\rangle)_{\blacksquare}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(R\langle T\rangle,R^{+}+T\cdot R^{\circ\circ}\langle T\rangle)_{\blacksquare}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(R\langle T\rangle,R^{+}\langle T\rangle)_{\blacksquare}

indicates that
\an(A,f)
is naturally isomorphic to
\varinjlim_{n\geq 0}\AnSpec\overline{A}_{\absolutevalue{\vpi^nf}\leq 1}
.

A.12.

The analytic loci satisfy the following basic properties.

Proposition
.

1)

For any
r
in
R
, we have
\an(R,r)=\AnSpec R^{\alg}
.

2)

Write
\an(R[X,Y],X)\cap\an(R[X,Y],Y)
for the fiber product in
\{\mbox{analytic stacks}\}

\textstyle{\an(R[X,Y],X)\cap\an(R[X,Y],Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\an(R[X,Y],X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\an(R[X,Y],Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\AnSpec R[X,Y]^{\alg}.}

Then
\an(R[X,Y],X)\cap\an(R[X,Y],Y)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\AnSpec R[X,Y]^{\alg}
factors through

\displaystyle\an(R[X,Y],XY)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\AnSpec R[X,Y]^{\alg}\mbox{ and }\an(R[X,Y],X+Y)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\AnSpec R[X,Y]^{\alg}.

3)

The composition
\an(R[T^{\pm 1}],T^{-1})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\AnSpec R[T^{\pm 1}]^{\alg}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\AnSpec R[T]^{\alg}
is naturally isomorphic to
D^{\an}(T)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\AnSpec R[T]^{\alg}
over
\AnSpec R[T]^{\alg}
.

4)

\big\{\an(R[T],T),D^{\an}(T)\big\}
forms a cohomologically étale (as in
[
64
, Definition 6.12]
) steady localization cover of
\AnSpec R[T]^{\alg}
.

5)

Write
D^{\an}(X)\cup D^{\an}(Y)
for the pushout in
\{\mbox{analytic stacks}\}

\textstyle{D^{\an}(X)\cap D^{\an}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D^{\an}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D^{\an}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D^{\an}(X)\cup D^{\an}(Y).}

Then
D^{\an}(X+Y)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\AnSpec R[X,Y]^{\alg}
factors through

\displaystyle D^{\an}(X)\cup D^{\an}(Y)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\AnSpec R[X,Y]^{\alg}.

Proof.

For part 1), it suffices to show that
R_{\absolutevalue{r}\leq 1}=R^{\alg}
when
r
lies in
R^{\circ\circ}
. By
A.7
, the functor
D(R[T]^{\alg})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D(R[T]_{\absolutevalue{T}\leq 1})
is the open embedding as in
[
14
, p. 61]
corresponding to the idempotent algebra
{\mathbb{Z}}(\!(T^{-1})\!)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\blacksquare}}(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}
in
D(R[T]^{\alg})
. The descriptions of
{\mathbb{Z}}(\!(T)\!)
and
{\mathbb{Z}}[\![U]\!]
from
A.7
show that
{\mathbb{Z}}(\!(T^{-1})\!)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\blacksquare}}(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}
is given by the complex

\textstyle{\displaystyle\frac{(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}[{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{\infty\}]}{R[\{\infty\}]}\otimes_{R}R[T]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{UT-1}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\frac{(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}[{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{\infty\}]}{R[\{\infty\}]}\otimes_{R}R[T].}

Since
r
lies in
R^{\circ\circ}
, multiplication by
Ur-1
is invertible on
(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}[{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{\infty\}]/R[\{\infty\}]
. Therefore, after base changing the above complex along the
R
-algebra morphism
R[T]\mapsto R
that sends
T\mapsto r
, the resulting complex is indeed exact.

For part 2), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle R[X,Y]^{\alg}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}R[X,Y]_{\absolutevalue{X}\leq 1}\otimes_{R[X,Y]^{\alg}}R[X,Y]_{\absolutevalue{Y}\leq 1}

factors through
R[X,Y]^{\alg}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}R[X,Y]_{\absolutevalue{XY}\leq 1}
and
R[X,Y]^{\alg}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}R[X,Y]_{\absolutevalue{X+Y}\leq 1}
. This follows from applying
-\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\blacksquare}}(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}
to Lemma
A.6
and the morphisms

\displaystyle({\mathbb{Z}}[X,Y],{\mathbb{Z}}[XY])_{\blacksquare}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}{\mathbb{Z}}[X,Y]_{\blacksquare}\mbox{ and }({\mathbb{Z}}[X,Y],{\mathbb{Z}}[X+Y])_{\blacksquare}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}{\mathbb{Z}}[X,Y]_{\blacksquare}.

For part 3), note that
T
is invertible in
({\mathbb{Z}}[T^{\pm 1}],{\mathbb{Z}}[T^{-1}])_{\blacksquare}
, and
T\mapsto T^{-1}
induces an isomorphism
({\mathbb{Z}}[T^{\pm 1}],{\mathbb{Z}}[T])_{\blacksquare}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}({\mathbb{Z}}[T^{\pm 1}],{\mathbb{Z}}[T^{-1}])_{\blacksquare}
. Applying
-\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\blacksquare}}(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}
to this shows that
R[T]^{\alg}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}R[T]_{\absolutevalue{T}\geq 1}
factors through
R[T]^{\alg}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}R[T^{\pm 1}]^{\alg}
and that the analytic rings
R[T]_{\absolutevalue{T}\geq 1}
and
R[T^{\pm 1}]_{\absolutevalue{T^{-1}}\leq 1}
are naturally isomorphic over
R[T^{\pm 1}]^{\alg}
. This implies the desired result.

For part 4),
[
59
, Lemma 3.2.9]
indicates that it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\big\{R[T]_{\absolutevalue{T}\leq 1},R[T]_{\absolutevalue{T}\geq 1}\big\}

forms a family of jointly conservative steady localizations of
R[T]^{\alg}
such that, after applying
D(-)
, the resulting functors are open embeddings as in
[
14
, p. 61]
. This follows from applying
-\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\blacksquare}}(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}
to Lemma
A.7
.

For part 5), Lemma
A.7
shows that
{\mathbb{Z}}[\![T]\!]
is an idempotent algebra in
D_{\blacksquare}({\mathbb{Z}}[T],{\mathbb{Z}})
, and
A.7
implies that
D_{\blacksquare}({\mathbb{Z}}[T],{\mathbb{Z}})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D_{\blacksquare}({\mathbb{Z}}[T^{\pm 1},T^{-1}])
is the open embedding as in
[
14
, p. 61]
corresponding to
{\mathbb{Z}}[\![T]\!]
. Hence applying
-\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\blacksquare}}(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}
to the morphism
{\mathbb{Z}}[\![X+Y]\!]\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}{\mathbb{Z}}[\![X,Y]\!]
of idempotent algebras in
D_{{\blacksquare}}({\mathbb{Z}}[X,Y],{\mathbb{Z}})
yields the desired result.
∎

A.13.

Using discrete Huber pairs, one can prove the following GAGA theorem for
all
(solid) quasi-coherent sheaves as in work of Clausen–Scholze
[
14
]
. Let
A
be a finitely generated
R
-algebra, and let
A^{+}\subseteq A
be the integral closure of a finitely generated
R
-subalgebra. If
A^{+}
equals the integral closure of the
R
-subalgebra generated by
f_{1},\dotsc,f_{m}
, write
\an(A,A^{+})
for the intersection
\bigcap_{i=1}^{m}\an(A,f_{i})
in
\{\mbox{analytic stacks}\}
over
\AnSpec A^{\alg}
. By Proposition
A.12
.1), Proposition
A.12
.2), and proof of
[
14
, Lemma 6.11]
, this does not depend on the choice of
f_{1},\dotsc,f_{m}
.

Theorem
.

The assignment
\Spa(A_{\disc},A^{+}_{\disc})\mapsto\an(A,A^{+})
sends rational covers to steady localization covers. Consequently, the assignments
\Spec{A}\mapsto\an(A,R^{\sim})
and
\Spec{A}\mapsto\an(A,A)
naturally glue to functors

\displaystyle(-)^{\alg}\mbox{ and }(-)^{\an}:\{\mbox{schemes locally of finite type over }R\}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\{\mbox{analytic stacks}\},

respectively, along with a natural transformation
(-)^{\an}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}(-)^{\alg}
. Moreover,
(-)^{\an}
sends Zariski covers to cohomologically étale steady localization covers. Finally, for any proper scheme
X
over
R
, the morphism
X^{\an}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}X^{\alg}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The first statement follows from the description of rational covers of

\displaystyle\Spa(A_{\disc},A^{+}_{\disc})

from
[
14
, Lemma 6.12]
and Proposition
A.12
. The second statement follows from the first statement and the fact that the assignments
\Spec{A}\mapsto\Spa(A_{\disc},R^{\sim}_{\disc})
and
\Spec{A}\mapsto\Spa(A_{\disc},A_{\disc})
send rational covers to rational covers. The third statement follows from Proposition
A.12
.4). Finally, the fourth statement follows from the valuative criterion for properness.
∎

Remark
.

By
[
62
, Proposition 13.6]
, the functors
(-)^{\alg}
and
(-)^{\an}
are valued in the full subcategory of analytic spaces as in
[
62
, Definition 13.5]
.

A.14.

We will later need the notion of
pseudocoherent
sheaves on schemes, as well as its relation with finite presentation. For any scheme
X
, write
D_{\pc}(X)
for the full subcategory of
D_{\operatorname{qc}}(X)
consisting of pseudocoherent objects as in
[
68
, Tag 08CB]
. When
X=\Spec{A}
is affine, recall from
[
68
, Tag 08E7]
that this is equivalent to the full subcategory
D_{\pc}(A)\subseteq D(A)
of pseudocoherent objects as in
[
68
, Tag 064Q]
.

Lemma
.

Let
A
be a finitely generated
R
-algebra. Then
A
is a finitely presented
R
-algebra if and only if, for all
R
-algebra surjections
R[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}]\twoheadrightarrow A
, the object
A
lies in
D_{\pc}(R[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}])
.

Proof.

Let
R[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}]\twoheadrightarrow A
be an
R
-algebra surjection with
A
lying in

\displaystyle D_{\pc}(R[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}]).

Then
[
68
, Tag 064T]
implies that
A
is a finitely presented
R
-algebra.

Conversely, assume that
A
is a finitely presented
R
-algebra, and let

\displaystyle\alpha:R[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}]\twoheadrightarrow A

be an
R
-algebra surjection. By
[
68
, Tag 00R2]
,
\ker\alpha
is finitely generated. Therefore noetherian approximation yields a noetherian ring
R_{0}
, a surjection

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}:R_{0}[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}]\twoheadrightarrow A_{0}

of
R_{0}
-algebras, and a ring homomorphism
R_{0}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}R
such that the base change of
(A_{0},\alpha_{0})
to
R
is isomorphic to
(A,\alpha)
. Because
R_{0}[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}]
is noetherian,
A_{0}
lies in
D_{\pc}(R_{0}[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}])
. Hence
A
lies in
D_{\pc}(R[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}])
.
∎

A.15.

By construction, the analytifications from Theorem
A.13
are covered by analytic rings of the following form. For any finitely generated
R
-algebra
A
with generators
\underline{f}=(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{m})
, write
A_{\absolutevalue{\ul{f}}\leq 1}
and
\overline{A}_{\absolutevalue{\ul{f}}\leq 1}
for these pushouts in
\AnRing
:

\textstyle{({\mathbb{Z}}[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}],{\mathbb{Z}})_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underline{f}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{Z}}[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}]_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\alg}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{\absolutevalue{\ul{f}}\leq 1},}

\textstyle{R[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}]^{\alg}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underline{f}}
\textstyle{(R\langle T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}\rangle,R^{+}+(T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m})\cdot R^{\circ\circ}\langle T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}\rangle)_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\alg}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{A}_{\absolutevalue{\ul{f}}\leq 1}.}

For any scheme
X

. We say that an object of

Finally, write

A.16.

For the analytifications from Theorem

,

-pullback of quasi-coherent sheaves commutes with arbitrary direct sums of uniformly bounded below objects. More precisely, for any scheme

Proposition
.

Assume that

Proof.

Let

with

. Write

\textstyle{(R[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}]_{\disc},R^{+}_{\disc})_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{R[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}]_{\absolutevalue{\ul{T}}\leq 1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{(A_{\disc},(R^{+})^{\sim}_{\disc})_{\blacksquare}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{\absolutevalue{\ul{f}}\leq 1}.}

\displaystyle R[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}]_{\absolutevalue{\ul{T}}\leq 1}=(R\langle T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}\rangle,R^{+}\langle T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}\rangle)_{\blacksquare}

using Lemma

, so

(
\Bumpeq
)

\displaystyle R\langle T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}\rangle\otimes_{R[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}]}A

lies in

. By

, Theorem 5.50]
, (

‣
A.16
) yields a pseudocompact object of

. Because (

≎

‣
A.16
) also lies in

Hence we can assume that
A=R[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}]
. Consider the morphisms

\textstyle{\AnSpec A_{\absolutevalue{\ul{T}}\leq 1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{\AnSpec A^{\alg}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\AnSpec(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare},}

and note that the restriction of
\pi
to

is

. We claim that

is naturally isomorphic to
j^{*}p^{*}[m]
. First,

. Since

\textstyle{M[T_{j}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{M(\!(T_{j}^{-1})\!)/M[T_{j}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}

implies that

\displaystyle j^{!}\underline{\Hom}_{R}(A,M)

\displaystyle=j^{!}M(\!(T_{1}^{-1},\dotsc,T_{m}^{-1})\!)/M[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}]=j^{!}M[T_{1},\dotsc,T_{m}][m]

\displaystyle=j^{!}p^{*}M[m]=j^{*}p^{*}M[m].

Finally, the desired result follows immediately from the claim.
∎

A.17.

As a consequence, we prove the following finitude result for proper pushforwards, which is the analogue of Grauert’s coherence theorem in our setting.

Corollary
.

For any proper scheme
X
of finite presentation over
R
, the functor

\displaystyle\pi_{*}:D(X^{\an})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D_{\blacksquare}(R,R^{+})

sends
D_{\pc}(X^{\an})
to
D_{\pc}(R)
.

Proof.

Theorem
A.13
indicates that
X^{\an}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}X^{\alg}
. Because
X^{\alg}
is covered by finitely many objects of the form
\AnSpec({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
, it is quasicompact, so the same holds for
X^{\an}
. Therefore
A.15
shows that
X^{\an}
is covered by finitely many objects of the form
\AnSpec\overline{A}_{\absolutevalue{\ul{f}}\leq 1}
. Since
X
is separated over
R
, the intersection of these objects over
X^{\an}
are also of this form, so Lemma
A.3
and
[
14
, Theorem 8.6 (1)]
imply that
\pi_{*}
sends nuclear objects of
D(X^{\an})
to nuclear objects of
D_{\blacksquare}(R,R^{+})
as in
[
14
, Definition 8.5 (1)]
.

We claim that
\pi_{*}
is naturally isomorphic to
\pi_{!}
. To see this, note that Proposition
A.10
indicates that
(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\overline{A}_{\absolutevalue{\ul{f}}\leq 1}
is induced as in
[
62
, Proposition 12.8]
. Hence
[
41
, Lemma 4.5.5]
implies that
\AnSpec\overline{A}_{\absolutevalue{\ul{f}}\leq 1}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\AnSpec(R,R^{+})_{\blacksquare}
is cohomologically prim as in as in
[
41
, Definition 4.5.1.(b)]
with trivial codualizing complex, and applying
[
41
, Remark 4.4.11.(iii)]
to the cover of
X^{\an}
by such objects shows that the same holds for
\pi
. This yields the claim.

Let
C
be a pseudocompact object of
D(X^{\an})
, and let
d
be an integer. Then Proposition
A.16
shows there exists an integer
e
such that, for all families
(M_{\alpha})_{\alpha}
of objects of
D^{\geq d}_{{\blacksquare}}(R,R^{+})
, the family
(\pi^{!}M_{\alpha})_{\alpha}
lies in
D^{\geq e}(X^{\an})
and satisfies

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\alpha}\pi^{!}M_{\alpha}=\pi^{!}\big(\bigoplus_{\alpha}M_{\alpha}\big).

Because
C
is pseudocompact, the claim indicates that

\displaystyle\Hom(\pi_{*}C,\bigoplus_{\alpha}M_{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\Hom(C,\pi^{!}\bigoplus_{\alpha}M_{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\Hom(C,\bigoplus_{\alpha}\pi^{!}M_{\alpha})=\bigoplus_{\alpha}\Hom(C,\pi^{!}M_{\alpha})=\bigoplus_{\alpha}\Hom(\pi_{*}C,M_{\alpha}).

Therefore
\pi_{*}C
is pseudocompact, as desired.
∎

A.18.

Using Corollary
A.17
, we prove that Theorem
A.13
restricts to an equivalence between subcategories of pseudocoherent objects as follows. For any scheme
X
locally of finite type over
R
, Theorem
A.13
shows that
X^{\alg}
is glued from objects of the form
\AnSpec A^{\alg}
along covers induced from Zariski covers. Hence
A.14
indicates that the full subcategory
D_{\pc}(A)\subseteq D(A)\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}D(A^{\alg})
naturally glues to a full subcategory of
D(X^{\alg})
that is naturally equivalent to
D_{\pc}(X)
.

Consider the functor
D(X^{\alg})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D(X^{\an})
. When
X=\Spec{A}
is affine,
[
1
, Theorem 5.50]
and
[
14
, Theorem 8.6 (1)]
imply that this sends
D_{\pc}(A)
to
D_{\pc}((\Spec{A})^{\an})
, so in general this sends
D_{\pc}(X)
to
D_{\pc}(X^{\an})
.

Theorem
.

For any proper scheme
X
of finite presentation over
R
, the resulting functor
D_{\pc}(X)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D_{\pc}(X^{\an})
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Full faithfulness follows from Theorem
A.13
. For essential surjectivity, let
C
be an object of
D_{\pc}(X^{\an})
. By full faithfulness, it suffices to prove that, for all affine open subschemes
j:\Spec{A}\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}X
, the image of
C
under the composition

\textstyle{D_{\pc}(X^{\an})\subseteq D(X^{\an})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces D(X^{\alg})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{j^{\alg,*}}
\textstyle{D(A^{\alg})}

lies in the image of
D_{\pc}(A)\subseteq D(A)\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}D(A^{\alg})
. Since
j^{\alg,*}
preserves pseudocompact objects, it suffices to prove that
j^{\alg,*}C
lies in the image of
D(A)\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}D(A^{\alg})
. Noetherian approximation yields a finitely generated ideal sheaf
{\mathscr{Z}}
of
X
whose vanishing locus equals the closed subset
X\smallsetminus\Spec{A}\subseteq X
, so after replacing
X
with its blowup along
{\mathscr{Z}}
, we can assume that
\Spec{A}=X\smallsetminus D
for an effective Cartier divisor
D
on
X
. Then
j_{*}^{\alg}j^{\alg,*}C=\varinjlim_{n\geq 0}C\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-nD)
and hence

\displaystyle\pi_{*}j_{*}^{\alg}j^{\alg,*}C=\varinjlim_{n\geq 0}\pi_{*}\big(C\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-nD)\big).

Note that
\pi_{*}j_{*}^{\alg}j^{\alg,*}C
is the image of
j^{\alg,*}C
in
D_{\blacksquare}(R,R^{+})
. Because
C\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-nD)
lies in
D_{\pc}(X^{\an})
, Corollary
A.17
shows that
\pi_{*}\big(C\otimes{\mathscr{O}}_{X}(-nD)\big)
lies in
D_{\pc}(R)
, so the image of
j^{\alg,*}C
in
D_{\blacksquare}(R,R^{+})
lies in the image of
D(R)\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}D_{\blacksquare}(R,R^{+})
. Therefore
j^{\alg,*}C
lies in the image of
D(A)\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}D(A^{\alg})
, as desired.
∎

A.19.

Finally, we prove Theorem H. Recall that the assignment

\displaystyle\Spa(A,A^{+})\mapsto\AnSpec(A,A^{+})_{\blacksquare}

from Tate affinoid adic spaces to
\{\mbox{analytic stacks}\}
sends rational covers to steady localization covers
[
59
, Proposition 2.3.2]
, so it naturally glues to a functor

\displaystyle(-)_{\blacksquare}:\{\mbox{analytic adic spaces}\}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\{\mbox{analytic stacks}\}.

For the rest of this section, assume that
R
is sousperfectoid, and write
S
for
\Spa(R,R^{+})
. Then for any smooth scheme
X
over
R
, the adic space
X^{\an}_{S}
as in Definition
1.1
exists by
[
25
, Proposition IV.4.9]
.

Theorem
.

For any smooth proper scheme
X
over
R
, the functor

\displaystyle\{\mbox{vector bundles on X}\}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\{\mbox{vector bundles on }X^{\an}_{S}\}

is an equivalence of categories.

Proof.

By covering
X^{\an}
by small enough steady subspaces of the form
\AnSpec A_{\absolutevalue{\ul{f}}\leq 1}
, we see that
X^{\an}
is isomorphic to
(X^{\an}_{S})_{\blacksquare}
. Then Theorem
A.18
yields a natural equivalence
D_{\pc}(X)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}D_{\pc}((X_{S}^{\an})_{\blacksquare})
, hence a natural equivalence between the full subcategories consisting of perfect objects
C
as in
[
14
, Proposition 9.2]
such that
-\otimes C
is
t
-exact.

For
D_{\pc}(X)
,
[
68
, Tag 0658]
shows that this subcategory is naturally equivalent to
\{\mbox{vector bundles on }X\}
. For
D_{\pc}((X^{\an}_{S})_{\blacksquare})
, applying
[
1
, Theorem 5.50]
and
[
68
, Tag 0658]
to the above cover of
X^{\an}\cong(X^{\an}_{S})_{\blacksquare}
by objects of the form
\AnSpec A_{\absolutevalue{f}\leq 1}
shows that this subcategory is naturally equivalent to
\{\mbox{vector bundles on }X^{\an}_{S}\}
.
∎

A.20.

As an application of Theorem
A.19
, we conclude this section by proving that the analytification of algebraic stacks of bundles agrees with analytic stacks of bundles. This answers a question of Heuer–Xu
[
40
, Remark 8.1.2]
.

Let
K
be an algebraically closed nonarchimedean field over
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, and write
\Perfd_{K}
for the category of affinoid perfectoid spaces over
\Spa{K}
. Endow
\Perfd_{K}
with the v-topology. Recall from
[
40
, Definition 8.4.4]
the diamondification functor

\displaystyle(-)^{\Diamond}:\{\mbox{algebraic stacks over }K\}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\{\mbox{small v-stacks on }\Perfd_{K}\}.

Let
X
be a smooth proper scheme over
K
, and let
G
be a linear algebraic group over
K
. Write
{\mathcal{B}}_{G,X}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{G,X}
for the algebraic stacks over
K
of
G
-bundles and
G
-Higgs bundles on
X
, respectively. Write
\Bun_{G,X}
and
\Hig_{G,X}
for the small v-stacks on
\Perfd_{K}
as in
[
39
, Definition 7.12.(1)]
and
[
39
, Definition 7.12.(2)]
, respectively.

Theorem
.

The natural morphisms of v-stacks on
\Perfd_{K}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{G,X}^{\Diamond}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Bun_{G,X}\mbox{ and }{\mathcal{H}}_{G,X}^{\Diamond}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Hig_{G,X}

are isomorphisms.

Proof.

This follows immediately from Theorem
A.19
.
∎

Appendix B
\ell
-adic realizations of Berkovich motives

In §
5
and §
6
, we use Berkovich (i.e. overconvergent) motivic sheaves as in
[
65
,
66
]
because they enjoy good formal properties. For example, there are
!
-pushforwards, and when evaluated on condensed anima, they can be described in terms of classical sheaves on profinite sets. We start this section by recalling this material.

Difficulties arise when we need to compare Berkovich motives for (analytifications of) moduli stacks of global shtukas
\Sht^{I}_{G,V}
with their classical
\ell
-adic sheaves. Ideally, we would start from a motivic intersection cohomology sheaf on
\Sht^{I}_{G,V}
, analytify it, and then pass to
\ell
-adic realizations. However, such motivic intersection cohomology sheaves are only known when
G
is split
[
58
]
, and one would still need to compare Berkovich motives with classical motives. To avoid this, we develop some tools that keep track of the relationship between
\ell
-adic sheaves on varieties,
\ell
-adic sheaves on their analytifications, and motivic sheaves on said analytifications.

We conclude this section by recalling the description of motivic sheaves on
\breve{F}_{v}
and
\Div_{v}^{1}
and describing their relationship with motivic nearby cycles.

Notation

In this section, we work over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
.

B.1.

We start by recalling the theory of
Berkovich motives

[
65
]
. Endow the category
\{\mbox{perfectoid rings over }\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}\}^{\mathrm{op}}
with the arc-topology as in
[
65
, Definition 3.1]
, which is subcanonical by
[
65
, Theorem 3.14]
. Write
\{\mbox{arc-sheaves over }\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}\}
for the
\infty
-category of sheaves of anima on
\{\mbox{perfectoid rings over }\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}\}^{\mathrm{op}}
, and recall from
[
65
, p. 54]
the
{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}]
-linear overconvergent motivic
6
-functor formalism
D_{\mot}(-)
on
\{\mbox{arc-sheaves over }\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}\}
.

B.2.

When evaluating on stacks arising from profinite sets (i.e.
condensed anima
), Berkovich motives enjoy the following description. Recall from
[
41
, Construction 3.5.16]
the
{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}]
-linear
6
-functor formalism
D(-,{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}])
on
\{\mbox{condensed anima}\}
, and write

\displaystyle\underline{(-)}:\{\mbox{condensed anima}\}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\{\mbox{arc-sheaves over }\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}\}

for the left Kan extension of the assignment
X\mapsto\underline{X}
from profinite sets. Then the proof of
[
66
, Proposition 2.1]
implies we have a morphism of
3
-functor formalisms

\displaystyle D(-,{\mathbb{Z}}[\textstyle\frac{1}{q}])\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D_{\mot}(\underline{(-)})

on
\{\mbox{condensed anima}\}
with respect to
!
-able morphisms for
D(-,{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}])
.

Proposition
.

This induces an isomorphism of
6
-functor formalisms

\displaystyle D(-,{\mathbb{Z}}[\textstyle\frac{1}{q}])\otimes_{D({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}])}D_{\mot}(*)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}D_{\mot}(\underline{(-)})

on
\{\mbox{condensed anima}\}
with respect to
!
-able morphisms for
D(-,{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}])
.

Proof.

When evaluating on finite sets, this is immediate. For any cofiltered limit
X=\varprojlim_{i}X_{i}
of finite sets, we have
\underline{X}=\varprojlim_{i}\underline{X_{i}}
, so
[
65
, Lemma 10.4]
shows that

\displaystyle D(X,{\mathbb{Z}}[\textstyle\frac{1}{q}])\otimes_{D({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}])}D_{\mot}(*)

\displaystyle=\big[\varinjlim_{i}D(X_{i},{\mathbb{Z}}[\textstyle\frac{1}{q}])\big]\otimes_{D({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}])}D_{\mot}(*)

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{i}\big[D(X_{i},{\mathbb{Z}}[\textstyle\frac{1}{q}])\otimes_{D({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}])}D_{\mot}(*)\big]

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{i}D_{\mot}(\underline{X_{i}})=D_{\mot}(\underline{X}).

Finally, the result follows from the uniqueness in
[
66
, Theorem 3.4.11]
.
∎

B.3.

Berkovich motives are related to étale sheaves in the following way. Write

\displaystyle a^{\prime*}:\{\mbox{small v-stacks over }\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}\}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\{\mbox{arc-sheaves over }\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}\}

for the left Kan extension of the assignment
\Spa(R,R^{+})\mapsto\Hom_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}(R,-)

[
66
, p. 3]
. For all positive integers
m
and any small v-stack
X
over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
,
[
65
, Proposition 12.3]
yields a natural fully faithful symmetric monoidal functor

\displaystyle\Mod_{{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m}}(D_{\mot}(a^{\prime*}X))\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}D_{\et}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m})

that is compatible with pullback and whose essential image consists of overconvergent objects as in
[
25
, Proposition IV.2.4]
.

Using this, one can define
\ell
-adic realization functors as follows. Write
\widehat{D}_{\et}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
for
\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}D_{\et}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m})
, so that taking
\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}
of the above induces a functor

\displaystyle\widehat{r}_{\ell,X}:D_{\mot}(a^{\prime*}X)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}\Mod_{{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m}}(D_{\mot}(a^{\prime*}X))\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}\widehat{D}_{\et}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}).

Write
\widehat{D}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
for
\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}D({\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m})
. Then the natural functor
D_{\perf}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\widehat{D}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
is fully faithful
[
10
, Lemma 4.2]
, and
[
25
, p. 168]
identifies
\widehat{D}_{\et}(*,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
with
\widehat{D}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
. Under this identification,
[
65
, Theorem 11.1]
shows that
\widehat{r}_{\ell,*}
restricts to a functor
D_{\mot}(*)^{\omega}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D_{\perf}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
, so
[
65
, Theorem 11.1]
indicates that applying
\Ind
yields a functor
r_{\ell,*}:D_{\mot}(*)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
.

B.4.

For the rest of this section, we will use the following base change of Berkovich motives. For any small v-stack
X
over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
, write
D_{\mot}(X)
for
D_{\mot}(a^{\prime*}X)
.

Let
\Lambda
be a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra, and consider the composition

\displaystyle D_{\mot}(*)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{r_{\ell,*}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}D(\Lambda).

Write
D(X,\Lambda)
for the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
D_{\mot}(X)\otimes_{D_{\mot}(*)}D(\Lambda)
, so that
D(-,\Lambda)
is a
\Lambda
-linear
6
-functor formalism on
\{\mbox{small v-stacks over }\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}\}
.

As a consequence of Proposition
B.2
, evaluating
D(-,\Lambda)
on classifying stacks yields categories of smooth representations. More precisely, let
G
be a locally pro-
p
group, let
K
be a compact open subgroup of
G
, and write
j:*/\underline{K}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}*/\underline{G}
for the associated morphism of small v-stacks.

Corollary
.

The symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
D(*/\underline{G},\Lambda)
is naturally equivalent to the derived category of smooth representations of
G
over
\Lambda
. Under this identification,
j_{!}:D(*/\underline{K},\Lambda)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D(*/\underline{G},\Lambda)
corresponds to
\cInd_{K}^{G}
.

Proof.

View
G
as a condensed set, and consider the condensed anima
*/G
. Then the arc-sheaf
\underline{*/G}
is naturally isomorphic to
a^{\prime*}(*/\underline{G})
, so the result follows from Proposition
B.2
,
[
41
, Proposition 5.3.10]
, and
[
41
, Proposition 5.4.4]
.
∎

B.5.

To facilitate comparisons with classical étale
\ell
-adic sheaf theories for varieties over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
in §
5
, we will consider the following notion of
Z
ariski-
c
onstructible
motives (or étale sheaves) relative to a base. Let
Z
be a separated finite type scheme over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
, and let
C
be a small v-stack over
Z^{\Diamond}
.

Definition
.

For any separated finite type scheme
X
over
Z
,

a)

Write
D_{\mot}^{Z\backslash C}(X^{\Diamond}_{C})\subseteq D_{\mot}(X^{\Diamond}_{C})
for the full subcategory generated under cones and retracts by objects of the form
f_{C,!}^{\Diamond}{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}](n)
, where
f:Y\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}X
runs over separated finite type morphisms and
n
runs over integers.

b)

For all positive integers
m
, write
D_{\et}^{Z\backslash C}(X^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m})\subseteq D_{\et}(X^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m})
for the full subcategory generated under cones and retracts by objects of the form
f_{C,!}^{\Diamond}({\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m})
, where
f:Y\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}X
runs over separated finite type morphisms.

c)

Write
D_{\et}^{Z\backslash C}(X^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\subseteq\widehat{D}_{\et}(X^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
for the full subcategory

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}D_{\et}^{Z\backslash C}(X^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m}).

B.6.

One immediately has the following relationship between étale sheaves on varieties over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
and étale sheaves on their analytifications. Note that
D_{\mot}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C})
,
\widehat{D}_{\et}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
and
D(-,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
are
6
-functor formalisms on

\displaystyle\{\mbox{separated finite type schemes over }Z\}.

Now
[
63
, Proposition 27.1]
,
[
63
, Proposition 27.2]
, and
[
63
, Proposition 27.4]
yield a fully faithful morphism of
3
-functor formalisms

\displaystyle c^{*}:D(-,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}\widehat{D}_{\et}((-)^{\Diamond},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})

on
\{\mbox{separated finite type schemes over }Z\}
, and proper base change shows that pullback induces a morphism of
3
-functor formalisms

\displaystyle|_{C}:\widehat{D}_{\et}((-)^{\Diamond},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\widehat{D}_{\et}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})

on
\{\mbox{separated finite type schemes over }Z\}
.

B.7.

One also has the following relationship between Zariski-constructible motives and étale sheaves.

Proposition
.

1)

D_{\mot}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C})
restricts to a
3
-functor formalism
D_{\mot}^{Z\backslash C}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C})
.

2)

\widehat{D}_{\et}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
restricts to a
3
-functor formalism
D^{Z\backslash C}_{\et}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
.

3)

The composition
|_{C}\circ c^{*}
sends
D_{\cons}(-,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
to
D^{Z\backslash C}_{\et}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
.

4)

The functor
\widehat{r}_{\ell,(-)^{\Diamond}_{C}}
sends
D^{Z\backslash C}_{\mot}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C})
to
D^{Z\backslash C}_{\et}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
and induces a morphism of
3
-functor formalisms on
\{\mbox{separated finite type schemes over }Z\}
.

Proof.

Part 1) and part 2) follow immediately from proper base change. For part 3), recall that
D_{\cons}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m})
is generated under cones and retracts by objects of the form
f_{!}({\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m})
, where
f:Y\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}X
runs over separated finite type morphisms. Therefore
B.6
and proper base change show that
|_{C}\circ c^{*}
sends
D_{\cons}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m})
to
D_{\et}^{Z\backslash C}((-)_{C}^{\Diamond},{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m})
. Finally, taking
\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}
yields the desired result.

For the first statement in part 4), let
f:Y\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}X
be a separated finite type morphism, and let
n
be an integer. By Nagata compactification, there exists a proper morphism
\overline{f}:\overline{Y}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}X
and an open embedding
j:Y\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\overline{Y}
such that
f=\overline{f}\circ j
. Then
j^{\Diamond}_{C}
is a partially proper open embedding, so the image of
j_{C,!}^{\Diamond}{\mathbb{Z}}[\textstyle\frac{1}{q}](n)
in
D_{\et}(\overline{Y}^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m})
is isomorphic to
j^{\Diamond}_{C,!}({\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m})
. Because
f^{\Diamond}_{C}
is proper and of finite cohomological dimension as in
[
65
, Definition 4.17]
, we see from
[
65
, Proposition 12.3]
that the image of
f^{\Diamond}_{C,!}{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}](n)=\overline{f}^{\Diamond}_{C,*}j^{\Diamond}_{C,!}{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}](n)
in
D_{\et}(X^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m})
is isomorphic to
\overline{f}^{\Diamond}_{C,*}j_{C,!}^{\Diamond}({\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m})=f^{\Diamond}_{C,!}({\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m})
. Hence taking
\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}
yields the desired statement. Finally, the second statement in part 4) follows from
B.3
and the above argument.
∎

B.8.

Finally, we
\Ind
-extend the preceding discussion. Proposition
B.7
.2) implies that
\Ind
-extending yields a morphism

\displaystyle\Upsilon:\Ind D_{\mot}^{Z\backslash C}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D_{\mot}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C})

of
3
-functor formalisms on
\{\mbox{separated finite type schemes over }Z\}
, and Proposition
B.7
.3) indicates that applying
\Ind
to
|_{C}\circ c^{*}:D_{\cons}(-,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D^{Z\backslash C}_{\et}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
yields a morphism of
3
-functor formalisms

\displaystyle\rho:D(-,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Ind D^{Z\backslash C}_{\et}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})

on
\{\mbox{separated finite type schemes over }Z\}
. Finally, Proposition
B.7
.4) shows that
\widehat{r}_{\ell,(-)^{\Diamond}_{C}}
induces a morphism of
3
-functor formalisms

\displaystyle D^{Z\backslash C}_{\mot}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D^{Z\backslash C}_{\et}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})

on
\{\mbox{separated finite type schemes over }Z\}
, so applying
\Ind
yields a morphism

\displaystyle r_{\ell,(-)^{\Diamond}_{C}}:\Ind D^{Z\backslash C}_{\mot}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Ind D^{Z\backslash C}_{\et}((-)^{\Diamond}_{C},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})

of
3
-functor formalisms on
\{\mbox{separated finite type schemes over }Z\}
.

B.9.

Write
F
for
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}(\!(\pi)\!)
, fix a separable closure
\overline{F}
of
F
, and write
C
for the completion of
\overline{F}
. Then there is a simple description of Zariski-constructible étale sheaves on
\Spa{C}
(relative to any
Z
). More precisely,
\widehat{D}_{\et}(\Spa{C},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
is naturally isomorphic to
\widehat{D}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})
, and under this identification,
B.6
implies that

\displaystyle D_{\et}^{Z\backslash\!\Spa{C}}(\Spa{C},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})=D_{\perf}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}).

We prove an analogous description of Zariski-constructible motives on
\Spa{C}
:

Proposition
.

We have
D_{\mot}^{Z\backslash\!\Spa C}(\Spa C)=D_{\mot}(\Spa C)^{\omega}
.

Proposition
B.9
and
[
65
, Proposition 10.1]
imply that

\displaystyle\Upsilon:\Ind D^{Z\backslash\Spa{C}}_{\mot}(\Spa{C})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D_{\mot}(\Spa{C})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

By considering
f:Z\times X\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}Z
for smooth projective schemes
X
over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
,
[
65
, Proposition 10.1]
shows that
D_{\mot}(\Spa C)^{\omega}
lies in
D_{\mot}^{Z\backslash\!\Spa{C}}(\Spa C)
. For the reverse inclusion, it suffices to prove that, for any separated finite type morphism
f:Y\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Spec C
, the object
f^{\an,\Diamond}_{!}{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}]
in
D_{\mot}(\Spa C)
is compact. Since
(-)^{\an,\Diamond}
factors through limit perfection, we can assume that
Y
is reduced.

We now induct on
\operatorname{dim}{Y}
, where the
\operatorname{dim}{Y}=0
case is immediate. By excision
[
65
, Proposition 4.25]
and the induction hypothesis, we can assume that
Y
is irreducible. Then
[
17
, Theorem 4.1]
yields a smooth projective morphism
\widetilde{f}:\widetilde{Y}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Spec C
, a dense open subspace
V\subseteq Y
, a dense open subspace
i:\widetilde{V}\displaystyle\mathop{\hookrightarrow}\widetilde{Y}
, and a finite étale morphism
j:\widetilde{V}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}V
over
C
. By excision
[
65
, Proposition 4.25]
and the induction hypothesis, we can assume that
Y=V
. Write
d
for the product of all primes
\ell\neq p
dividing
\deg{j}
. Because
(f_{!}^{\an,\Diamond}{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}])\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}]}{\mathbb{Z}}/d^{m}=f_{!}^{\an,\Diamond}({\mathbb{Z}}/d^{m})
, spreading out implies that the image of
f_{!}^{\an,\Diamond}{\mathbb{Z}}[\textstyle\frac{1}{q}]
in

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}\Mod_{{\mathbb{Z}}/d^{m}}(D_{\mot}(\Spa{C}))=\widehat{D}\big(\prod_{\ell\mid d}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\big)

is compact. Therefore it suffices to prove that the image
f_{!}^{\an,\Diamond}{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{dq}]
of
f_{!}^{\an,\Diamond}{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}]
in
\Mod_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{dq}]}(D_{\mot}(\Spa{C}))
is compact.

The proof of
[
65
, Proposition 10.1]
shows that
\widetilde{f}_{!}^{\an,\Diamond}{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{dq}]
is compact in

\displaystyle\Mod_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{dq}]}(D_{\mot}(\Spa{C})),

so excision
[
65
, Proposition 4.25]
and the induction hypothesis imply that the same holds for
(\widetilde{f}\circ i)_{!}^{\an,\Diamond}{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{dq}]
. Finally, the trace map exhibits
f_{!}^{\an,\Diamond}{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{dq}]
as a retract of
(\widetilde{f}\circ i)_{!}^{\an,\Diamond}{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{dq}]
, which yields the desired result.
∎

B.10.

Write
\breve{F}
for
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}(\!(\pi)\!)
, write
\phi:\Spd\breve{F}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}\Spd\breve{F}
for the geometric
q
-Frobenius automorphism over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
, and write
\Div^{1}
for the small v-sheaf
(\Spd\breve{F})/\phi^{\mathbb{Z}}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
.

Let us recall the following descriptions of motives on
\Spd\breve{F}
and
\Div^{1}
. Fix a group isomorphism
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}^{\times}\cong{\mathbb{Q}}/{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}]
, and choose a compatible system
\{\pi^{1/n}\}_{p\nmid n}
of
n
-th roots of
\pi
in
\overline{F}
. Recall that these choices induce a short exact sequence of certain group schemes over
{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}]

[
66
, p. 10]

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ID_{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\WD_{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{{\mathbb{Z}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1.}

Lemma
.

These choices induce compatible equivalences

\displaystyle D(\Spd\breve{F},\Lambda)\cong D_{\qcoh}\big((\Spec\Lambda)/\!\ID_{F}\!\big)\mbox{ and }D(\Div^{1},\Lambda)\cong D_{\qcoh}\big((\Spec\Lambda)/\!\WD_{F}\!\big).

Proof.

Write
MG_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}
for the algebraic stack over
{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}]
from
[
65
, p. 62]
, and recall that we have a natural functor
D_{\qcoh}(MG_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D_{\mot}(*)

[
65
, p. 62]
. Consider the morphism
\Spec{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}]\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}MG_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}
induced by the isomorphism
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}^{\times}\cong{\mathbb{Q}}/{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}]
as in
[
66
, p. 10]
; note that the isomorphism
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}^{\times}\cong{\mathbb{Q}}/{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{p}]
also induces a commutative square

\textstyle{D_{\qcoh}(MG_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D_{\mot}(*)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{\ell,*}}
\textstyle{D({\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}).}

Write
\widetilde{X}_{\Div^{1}}
and
X_{\Div^{1}}
for the algebraic stacks over
MG_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}
from
[

, Definition 4.1]
. Then
