, Theorem 4.2]
and its proof yield compatible equivalences

\displaystyle D_{\qcoh}(\widetilde{X}_{\Div^{1}})\otimes_{D_{\qcoh}(MG_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}})}D_{\mot}(*)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}D_{\mot}(\Spd\breve{F}),

\displaystyle D_{\qcoh}(X_{\Div^{1}})\otimes_{D_{\qcoh}(MG_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}})}D_{\mot}(*)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}D_{\mot}(\Div^{1}).

so the above commutative square induces compatible equivalences

\displaystyle D_{\qcoh}(\widetilde{X}_{\Div^{1}}\times_{MG_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}}\Spec\Lambda)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}D(\Spd\breve{F},\Lambda),

\displaystyle D_{\qcoh}(X_{\Div^{1}}\times_{MG_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}}\Spec\Lambda)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}D(\Div^{1},\Lambda).

Finally,
[
66
, p. 10]
compatibly identifies

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}_{\Div^{1}}\times_{MG_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}}\Spec\Lambda

\displaystyle\cong(\Spec\Lambda)/\!\ID_{F},

\displaystyle X_{\Div^{1}}\times_{MG_{\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}}}\Spec\Lambda

\displaystyle\cong(\Spec\Lambda)/\!\WD_{F}\!.\qed

B.11.

We conclude this section by describing how
motivic nearby cycles
behave after
\ell
-adic realization. Write
f:\Spa{C}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}*
for the structure morphism. Recall that
f_{*}
yields an equivalence
D_{\mot}(\Spa{C})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\Mod_{f_{*}{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}]}(D_{\mot}(*))
and that our choice of
\{\pi^{1/n}\}_{p\nmid n}
from
B.10
induces a morphism
f_{*}{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}]\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}{\mathbb{Z}}[\textstyle\frac{1}{q}]
of
{\mathbb{E}}_{\infty}
-algebras in
D_{\mot}(*)

[
65
, p. 61]
. Under this identification, write
\Psi:D_{\mot}(\Spa{C})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D_{\mot}(*)
for the functor corresponding to
-\otimes_{f_{*}{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}]}{\mathbb{Z}}[\textstyle\frac{1}{q}]

[
65
, Definition 11.3]
.

Recall that pullback yields an equivalence
[
25
, p. 168]

\displaystyle\widehat{D}_{\et}(*,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}\widehat{D}_{\et}(\Spa{C},{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}).

Proposition
.

The functor
\Psi
preserves compact objects. Moreover, we have a commutative square

\textstyle{D_{\mot}(\Spa C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{r}_{\ell,\Spa C}}
\scriptstyle{\Psi}
\textstyle{\widehat{D}_{\et}(\Spa C,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell})}
\textstyle{D_{\mot}(*)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{r}_{\ell,*}}
\textstyle{\widehat{D}_{\et}(*,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\sim

Proposition
B.11
and
B.3
imply that
\widehat{r}_{\ell,\Spa C}
restricts to a functor

\displaystyle D_{\mot}(\Spa C)^{\omega}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D_{\perf}({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}),

so
[
65
, Proposition 10.1]
indicates that applying
\Ind
yields a functor

\displaystyle r_{\ell,\Spa C}:D_{\mot}(\Spa C)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D({\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}).

Proof.

Because
f^{*}:D_{\mot}(*)\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}D_{\mot}(\Spa{C})
is left adjoint to
f_{*}
, it corresponds to
-\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}]}f_{*}{\mathbb{Z}}[\textstyle\frac{1}{q}]
. Therefore
\Psi\circ f^{*}
is naturally isomorphic to the identity, so the first statement follows from
[
65
, Proposition 10.1]
and
[
65
, Theorem 11.1]
.

For the second statement, the proof of
[
65
, Proposition 11.4]
implies that, for all positive integers
m
, applying
-\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}]}{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{m}
to
f_{*}{\mathbb{Z}}[\frac{1}{q}]\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}{\mathbb{Z}}[\textstyle\frac{1}{q}]
gives an isomorphism. This yields the desired result.
∎

Appendix C
Global rigid inner forms, by Peter Dillery

In the following,
G
always denotes a connected reductive group over
F
a global function field associated to the projective curve
C
with constant field
\mathbb{F}_{q}
. We will break from the notation of the rest of the paper by denoting
F^{s}
a separable closure of
F
inside a fixed algebraic closure
\overline{F}
. The set of all places of
F
will be denoted by
V_{F}
. We denote the Kottwitz gerbe over
F
by
\textnormal{Kott}_{F}
, and the Kottwitz gerbe over each
F_{v}
by
\textnormal{Kott}_{v}
. For a set of places
\Sigma
of
F
, we denote by
O_{F,\Sigma}
the ring of
\Sigma
-integers of
F
.

C.1.

Theorem
4.11
says that the
B(F,G)_{\textnormal{basic}}
-inner forms
G_{b}
of a quasi-split connected reductive group
G
appear naturally in the description of the semistable locus of
\Bun_{G,F}
. The goal of this appendix is to use results in the main body of the paper to prove fundamental geometric properties of the “extended” stack
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}^{e}
introduced in
[
21
]
, which has geometry analogous to that of
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}
but whose semistable locus encodes global rigid inner forms of
G
, in the sense of
[
20
]
, rather than just
B(F,G)_{\textnormal{basic}}
-inner forms. The advantage of using global rigid inner forms is that they enable a statement (
[
21
, Conjecture 5.7]
) of a multiplicity formula for discrete automorphic representations that applies to arbitrary connected reductive groups over
F
. It is reasonable to believe that a
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}^{e}
-adapted version of the machinery in the main part of this paper together with the conjectural
\textnormal{Kott}_{v}\times_{F_{v}}\textnormal{Kal}_{v}
-adapted refined Langlands correspondence from
[
21
, Conjecture 5.1]
at each
v
can be used to prove cases of this multiplicity formula. We emphasize that the construction presented here is inspired by the work of
[
22
]
in the local case.

C.2.

We review the notion of local and global rigid inner forms. One can use class field theory to construct a global gerbe
\textnormal{Kal}_{F}\to\textnormal{Spec}(F)
(
[
20
, Proposition 3.21]
) and for each place
v\in V_{F}
a local gerbe
\textnormal{Kal}_{v}\to\textnormal{Spec}(F_{v})
(
[
19
, Theorem 3.4]
) which are banded by profinite multiplicative group schemes
P
and
u_{v}
, respectively. We refer the reader to
[
21
, §2.1]
for a more detailed summary of these two gerbes and their bands. We give these gerbes the étale topology inherited from
\textnormal{Spec}(F)
.

For
?\in\{F,v\}
, the basic cohomology
H^{1}(\textnormal{Kal}_{?},G)_{\textnormal{basic}}
is defined as all isomorphism classes of étale
G
-torsors on
\textnormal{Kal}_{?}
whose restriction to the band of
\textnormal{Kal}_{?}
is an
F
-rational homomorphism
P\to Z_{G}
or
u_{v}\to Z_{G}
. A key property of these gerbes is that the natural map

H^{1}(\textnormal{Kal}_{?},G)_{\textnormal{basic}}\to H^{1}(F,G_{\textnormal{ad}})

is always surjective. In other words, any inner form of
G
can be realized as coming from a
G
-torsor on one of these gerbes.

There is a variant of this gerbe defined in
[
22
]
and
[
21
]
which is better–suited to geometry:

Definition
.

Define
B_{e}(G)
(resp.
B_{e}(G_{F_{v}})
) to be all isomorphism classes of étale
G
-torsors on the gerbe
\textnormal{Kott}_{F}\times_{F}\textnormal{Kal}_{F}
(resp.
\textnormal{Kott}_{v}\times_{F_{v}}\textnormal{Kal}_{v}
) whose restriction to the band of
\textnormal{Kal}_{F}
(resp.
\textnormal{Kal}_{F_{v}}
) factors through
Z_{G}
. Define the subset
B_{e}(G)_{\textnormal{basic}}
(resp.
B_{e}(G_{F_{v}})_{\textnormal{basic}}
) to be those classes whose restriction to the entire band of the gerbe
\textnormal{Kott}_{F}\times_{F}\textnormal{Kal}_{F}
(resp.
\textnormal{Kott}_{v}\times_{F_{v}}\textnormal{Kal}_{v}
) factors through
Z_{G}
.

In the same way that there is a bijection
B(F,G)_{\textnormal{basic}}\xrightarrow{\sim}(\pi_{1}(G)[V_{F^{s}}]_{0})_{\textnormal{Gal}(F^{s}/F)}
, one has a linear-algebraic description of
B_{e}(G)_{\textnormal{basic}}
given by
[
21
, Theorem 3.10]
which recovers the
B(F,G)
-bijection and the
H^{1}(\textnormal{Kal}_{F},G)_{\textnormal{basic}}
-bijection from
[
20
, Theorem 4.11]
. We omit the details here for sake of brevity, but remark that the proof relies on an explicit Tannakian description
[
21
, Proposition 3.1]
of the gerbe
\textnormal{Kott}_{F}\times_{F}\textnormal{Kal}_{F}
generalizing the description of
\textnormal{Kott}_{F}
using Drinfeld isoshtukas.

C.3.

The key ingredient for defining the stack
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}^{e}
is a generalization of
\textnormal{Kal}_{F}
which is defined over a fixed affine open subset
U=C\setminus|\Sigma|
of the curve
C
for a finite set of places
\Sigma
. This gerbe is denoted by
\textnormal{Kal}_{F,\Sigma}\to U
.

The gerbe
\textnormal{Kal}_{F,\Sigma}
is built from finite level gerbes. Fix a set of lifts
\dot{\Sigma}
of
\Sigma
in
V_{F^{s}}
and denote by
F_{\Sigma}
the maximal
\Sigma
-unramified extension of
F
. Fix also a cofinal system of pairs
\{(E_{i},n_{i})\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
n_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
,
F\subseteq E_{i}\subseteq F_{\Sigma}
and if
j\geq i
then
E_{i}\subseteq E_{j}
and
n_{i}\mid n_{j}
. Define
P_{\dot{\Sigma},i}
to be the
O_{F,\Sigma}
-group scheme Cartier dual to
(\frac{1}{n_{i}}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z})[\Gal(E_{i}/F)\times\Sigma_{F_{i}}]_{0,0}
consisting of all elements whose
[(\sigma,w)]
-coefficient is zero unless
\sigma^{-1}(w)\in\dot{\Sigma}_{E_{i}}
. The subscript “
0,0
” means we insist that the elements must be killed by both augmentation maps. There is (
[
21
, Corollary 2.12]
) a “level
i
” canonical class
\xi_{i}\in H_{\textnormal{fppf}}^{2}(O_{F,\Sigma},P_{\dot{\Sigma},i})
, and these classes are compatible via a system of transition maps
\{P_{\dot{\Sigma},j}\to P_{\dot{\Sigma},i}\}
defined loc. cit. such that
P_{\dot{\Sigma}}=\varprojlim P_{\dot{\Sigma},i}
.

To define the canonical class corresponding to
\textnormal{Kal}_{F,\Sigma}
, one takes the unique lift of
\varprojlim\xi_{i}
in
H^{2}_{\textnormal{fppf}}(F,P_{\dot{\Sigma}})
which, for each
v\in V_{F}
, localizes in some suitable sense to the class in
H^{2}_{\textnormal{fppf}}(F_{v},u_{v})
corresponding to
\textnormal{Kal}_{v}
—for the full details see
[
21
, Theorem 2.16]
. One can define a transition map
\textnormal{Kal}_{F,\Sigma^{\prime}}\to\textnormal{Kal}_{F,\Sigma}
for all
\Sigma\subseteq\Sigma^{\prime}
and pullback by these maps induces a bijection

\varinjlim_{\Sigma}H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\textnormal{Kal}_{F,\Sigma},G)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\textnormal{Kal}_{F},G).

C.4.

We now recall the definition of the stack
\Bun_{G,F}^{e}
from
[
21
, §4]
, which is inspired by the ideas of
[
22
]
. Each gerbe
\textnormal{Kal}_{F,\Sigma}
defines (
[
21
, Lemma 4.2]
) an adic gerbe
\textnormal{Kal}_{F,\Sigma}^{\ad}\to\textnormal{Spa}(O_{F,\Sigma})
(for the v-topology), and, for an affinoid perfectoid space
S
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, define
H^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\mathfrak{U}_{S},G)_{\textnormal{$\textnormal{Kal}$-basic}}
as all isomorphism classes of étale
G
-torsors
\mathscr{G}
on
\mathfrak{U}_{S}:=U_{S}^{\textnormal{an}}\times_{\textnormal{Spa}(O_{F,\Sigma})}\textnormal{Kal}_{F,\Sigma}^{\ad}
whose restriction to the band is a morphism

(P_{\dot{\Sigma}}^{\textnormal{ad}})_{S}\xrightarrow{\lambda_{\mathscr{G}}}Z_{G,S}.

We denote all such torsors (not isomorphism classes) by
Z^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\mathfrak{U}_{S},G)_{\textnormal{$\textnormal{Kal}_{F}$-basic}}
.

The main definition is then (
[
21
, Definition 4.6]
):

Definition
.

(1)

Given
U
a dense affine open of
C
, define a functor
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e}
from affinoid perfectoid spaces over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
to groupoids by

S\mapsto\{(\mathscr{G},\phi)|\mathscr{G}\in Z^{1}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(\mathfrak{U}_{S},G)_{\textnormal{$\textnormal{Kal}_{F}$-basic}},\hskip 5.69046pt\phi\colon\mathscr{G}\xrightarrow{\sim}(\textnormal{Frob}_{S}\times\textnormal{id})^{*}\mathscr{G}\};

(2)

Define a functor
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}^{e}
from affinoid perfectoid spaces over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
to groupoids by

S\mapsto\varinjlim_{U}\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e}(S).

It is proved in
[
21
, Lemma 4.7]
that both of these functors are small v-stacks.

C.5.

The assignment
(\mathscr{G},\phi)\in\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e}(S)\mapsto\lambda_{\mathscr{G}}
defines a map

\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e}\xrightarrow{\Lambda}\underline{\textnormal{Hom}}_{U}(P_{\dot{\Sigma}},Z_{G}),

where
\underline{\textnormal{Hom}}_{U}(P_{\dot{\Sigma}},Z_{G})
is the functor sending
S
to
\textnormal{Hom}_{U_{S}^{\textnormal{an}}}((P_{\dot{\Sigma}}^{\textnormal{ad}})_{S},Z_{G,S})
. We now recall one more definition
[
21
, Definition 4.11]
.

Observe that we have a canonical isomorphism for every geometric point
\bar{s}
of a perfectoid space
S
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}

\Hom_{U}(P_{\dot{\Sigma}},Z_{G})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Hom_{U_{\bar{s}}^{\textnormal{an}}}((P_{\dot{\Sigma}}^{\textnormal{ad}})_{\bar{s}},Z_{G,\bar{s}}).

In view of this, given
\lambda\in\Hom(P_{\dot{\Sigma}}^{\textnormal{ad}},Z_{G})
, define
\textnormal{Bun}^{e,\lambda}_{G,U}
as the substack characterized by

\textnormal{Bun}^{e,\lambda}_{G,U}(S)=\{x\in\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,U}(S)|\lambda_{\bar{x}}=\lambda\in\Hom_{U_{\bar{s}}^{\textnormal{an}}}((P_{\dot{\Sigma}}^{\textnormal{ad}})_{\bar{s}},Z_{G,\bar{s}})\},

where
\bar{s}
runs over all geometric points of
S
and
\bar{x}
denotes the image of
x
at
\bar{s}
.

A key result which allows us to use the results in the main body of paper in this new setting is
[
21
, Corollary 4.13]
, which says that
\Lambda
induces a decomposition (as a v-stack)

\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e}\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in\textnormal{Hom}_{F}(P_{\dot{\Sigma}},Z_{G})}\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e,\lambda},

and analogously for
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}^{e}
.

There is an analogue
[
21
, Definition 4.9]
of the notions introduced in §
2.9
:

Definition
.

(1)

Define
\textnormal{Bun}^{e,1}_{G,F}
to be the substack characterized by

\textnormal{Bun}^{e,1}_{G,F}(S)=\{x\in\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,F}(S)|\bar{x}=\textnormal{triv}\in\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}(\overline{s})\},

where
\bar{s}
runs over all geometric points of
S
and
\bar{x}
denotes the image of
x
at
\bar{s}
.

(2)

Given
b
an element of
B_{e}(G)_{\textnormal{basic}}
, define
\textnormal{Bun}^{e,b}_{G,F}
as the substack characterized by
\textnormal{Bun}^{e,b}_{G,F}(S)=\{x\in\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,F}(S)|\bar{x}=b\in\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}(\overline{s})\}
, where
\bar{s}
runs over all geometric points of
S
and
\bar{x}
denotes the image of
x
at
\bar{s}
.

The identical twisting argument from the proof of Proposition
2.9
shows that:

Proposition
.

(1)

For
b\in B_{e}(G)_{\textnormal{basic}}
pulled back from
\textnormal{Kott}_{F,\Sigma}\times_{U}\textnormal{Kal}_{F,\Sigma}
, there is a natural isomorphism
\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,U}\xrightarrow{\sim}\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G_{b},U}
sending
\textnormal{Bun}^{e,b}_{G,U}
to
\textnormal{Bun}^{e,1}_{G_{b},U}
.

(2)

For
b\in H^{1}(\textnormal{Kott}_{F,\Sigma}\times_{U}\textnormal{Kal}_{F,\Sigma})_{\textnormal{basic}}
above a fixed
\lambda\in\Hom_{U}(P_{\dot{\Sigma}},Z_{G})
, we have an isomorphism
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e,\lambda}\xrightarrow{\sim}\textnormal{Bun}_{G_{b},U}
.

In the above
\textnormal{Kott}_{F,\Sigma}
is a “
U
-level” version of
\textnormal{Kott}_{F}
defined in
[
21
, §2.3]
. Combining this result with
[
21
, Corollary 4.13]
proves that the stack
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e}
is isomorphic to a disjoint union of the stacks
\textnormal{Bun}_{G^{\prime},U}
for varying inner forms
G^{\prime}
of
G
(this is a global analogue of
[
22
, Exemple 12.6]
).

We therefore deduce from Proposition
4.5
that:

Corollary
.

Each
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e}
is an Artin
v
-stack.

C.6.

We now prove some more refined results about the geometry of
\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,F}
.

First, we deduce the following analogue of Theorem
2.10
, which, in particular, identifies
\Bun_{G,U}^{e}(\Spd\overline{\mathbb{F}_{q}})
with
B_{e}(G)
:

Theorem
.

The perfect v-stack
\Bun_{G,U}^{e,\red}
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
is the v-sheafification of

\displaystyle\Spec{B}\mapsto\left\{\begin{tabular}[]{c}$\textnormal{Kal}_{F}$-basic $G$-torsors $\mathscr{G}$ on $U_{B}\times_{U}\textnormal{Kal}_{F,\Sigma}$ equipped with\\
an isomorphism $\phi:\mathscr{G}\displaystyle\mathop{\rightarrow}^{\sim}(\Frob_{B}\times\textnormal{id})^{*}\mathscr{G}$\end{tabular}\right\}.

Moreover, when each connected component of
\Spec{B}
is a valuation ring, no sheafification is needed.

Proof.

[
21
, Corollary 4.13]
implies that
\Bun_{G,U}^{e,\red}\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in\textnormal{Hom}_{F}(P_{\dot{\Sigma}},Z_{G})}\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e,\lambda,\red}
. We have seen in Proposition
C.5
that picking
b\in H^{1}(\text{Kott}_{F,\Sigma}\times\text{Kal}_{F,\Sigma})_{\text{basic}}
above a fixed
\lambda
(we can do this by
[
21
, Proposition 2.29]
) gives an identification
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e,\lambda}\xrightarrow{\sim}\textnormal{Bun}_{G_{b},U}
. A similar (but easier) argument as in the proof of
[
21
, Proposition 4.12]
gives an analogous disjoint union decomposition of the functor (1) according to
\Hom_{U}(P_{\dot{\Sigma}},Z_{G})
, and twisting by
b
gives an isomorphism from the presheaf mapping
\Spec{B}
to all
\mathscr{G}
as in (1) such that
\lambda_{\mathscr{G}}=\lambda
at all geometric points of
B
to the functor in (
Theorem
). We can then use Theorem
2.10
to deduce the result.
∎

C.7.

This subsection is purely review from
[
21
]
and concerns the localization properties of the stack
\Bun^{e}_{G,F}
. First, we define for a place
v\in V_{F}
the stack
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F_{v}}^{e}
to be the same as
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e}
but with
F_{v}
in place of
O_{F,\Sigma}
and
\textnormal{Kal}_{v}
in place of
\textnormal{Kal}_{F,\Sigma}
, as defined in
[
22
, §12]
.

We recall from
[
21
, §4.2]
the functor
\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,\mathbb{A}}
defined by

S\mapsto\varinjlim_{\Sigma}\prod_{v\notin\Sigma}\textnormal{Bun}_{G,O_{v}}(S)\times\prod_{v\in\Sigma}\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,F_{v}}(S),

where the limit is over all finite subsets of places
\Sigma
. This defines a small v-stack, and we have a morphism

\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,F}\to\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,\mathbb{A}}

induced by a localization map
\textnormal{Kal}_{v}\to\textnormal{Kal}_{F}
at each place as discussed in
[
21
, §2.1]
.

One then has
[
21
, Proposition 4.16]
:

Proposition
.

There is a morphism

\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,U}\to\prod_{v\notin\Sigma}\textnormal{Bun}_{G,O_{v}}\times\prod_{v\in\Sigma}\textnormal{Bun}^{e,(\Sigma)}_{G,F_{v}}\subseteq\prod_{v\notin\Sigma}\textnormal{Bun}_{G,O_{v}}\times\prod_{v\in\Sigma}\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,F_{v}}

which makes the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{v\notin\Sigma}\textnormal{Bun}_{G,O_{v}}\times\prod_{v\in\Sigma}\textnormal{Bun}^{e,(\Sigma)}_{G,F_{v}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,\mathbb{A}}}

Cartesian.

In the above,
\textnormal{Bun}^{e,(\Sigma)}_{G,F_{v}}
is a minor modification of the stack
\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,F_{v}}
adapted to the completion of
F_{\Sigma}
at a fixed place above
v
, see
[
21
, §4.2]
for the full details.

C.8.

There is an analogue of the semistable locus of
\Bun_{G,F}
:

Definition
.

Set
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}^{e,\textnormal{ss}}
to be the preimage of

\varinjlim\prod_{v\notin\Sigma}\textnormal{Bun}_{G,O_{v}}\times\prod_{v\in\Sigma}\textnormal{Bun}^{e,\textnormal{ss}}_{G,F_{v}}

in
\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{F,G}
, where
\textnormal{Bun}^{e,\textnormal{ss}}_{G,F_{v}}
is the local semistable locus as defined in
[
22
]
. This does not depend on the choice of localization maps, is a small v-stack, and is an open substack of
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}^{e,\textnormal{ss}}
.

One can explicitly describe the semistable locus of
\Bun_{G,F}^{e}
using
B_{e}(G)_{\textnormal{basic}}
.

Theorem
.

(1)

The substack
\textnormal{Bun}^{e,1}_{G,U}\subseteq\textnormal{Bun}^{e}_{G,U}
is open, and is isomorphic to
*/\underline{G(O_{F,\Sigma})}
.

(2)

For
b\in B_{e}(G)_{\textnormal{basic}}
, the substack
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}^{e,b}\subseteq\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}^{e}
is open and isomorphic to
\ast/\underline{G_{b}(F)}
.

(3)

We have an equality of stacks
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}^{e,\textnormal{ss}}=\bigsqcup_{b\in B_{e}(G)_{\textnormal{basic}}}\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}^{e,b}
.

Proof.

For the first statement, we observe that the image of
\textnormal{Bun}^{e,1}_{G,U}
via
\Lambda
is determined by its value at any geometric point, and is thus the trivial morphism
0_{P}
. By Proposition
C.5
we can identify the inclusion
\textnormal{Bun}^{e,1}_{G,U}\hookrightarrow\text{Bun}_{G,U}^{e,0_{P}}
with the inclusion
\text{Bun}_{G,U}^{1}\hookrightarrow\text{Bun}_{G,U}
, so that the desired result is true by combining
[
21
, Proposition 4.12]
with Theorem
2.4
.

The second part is a consequence of the first statement and Proposition
C.5
. The disjointness part of the second statement can be deduced from the disjointness of the
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}^{b}
after decomposing
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,F}^{e}
using
[
21
, Corollary 4.13]
. Similarly, the verification that this union is exhaustive can be done using the decomposition (as stacks)

\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e,\textnormal{ss}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in\textnormal{Hom}_{F}(P_{\dot{\Sigma}},Z_{G})}\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e,\textnormal{ss},\lambda}

induced by
[
21
, Corollary 4.13]
and then observing that the identification
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e,\lambda}\xrightarrow{\sim}\textnormal{Bun}_{G_{b},U}^{e}
for
b\in B_{e}(G)_{\text{basic}}
identifies
\textnormal{Bun}_{G,U}^{e,\textnormal{ss},\lambda}
with
\textnormal{Bun}_{G_{b},U}^{\textnormal{ss}}
. Now one can use Theorem
4.11
.
∎
